## Luke 1:1 (id: 20425)

* Many people had already begun to write accounts of Jesus’s life. Luke was not the first to write this history. Most scholars think Luke used the Gospel of Mark and other written and spoken sources.
* *fulfilled*: This word can mean "finished" or "completed," but Luke is referring to Jesus making Old Testament promises come true ([4:21](https://ref.ly/Luke4:21); [24:44](https://ref.ly/Luke24:44)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:1


## Luke 1:1–4 (id: 20420)

Luke begins his Gospel with a formal introduction written in the style of skilled Greek writers of his time. These first four verses use a careful and formal style. They are among the best\-written Greek in the New Testament.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Theophilus (ID: `person:Theophilus`); Certainty (ID: `keyterm:Certainty`)

## Luke 1:3 (id: 20435)

* As a good historian, Luke *carefully* *investigated* the history of Jesus from the start to provide a reliable story.
* *an orderly account*: Some versions translate this phrase as “in consecutive order.” But it means an organized or *carefully* written *account*, not a sequence of time.
* The name *Theophilus* means “loved by God” or “one who loves God.” It is likely a personal name, not meant to describe a name.

    Different theories suggest he might be:

    1. an unbeliever who was influential in society,
        2. a person newly converted to Christianity needing guidance,
        3. the judge at the apostle Paul’s trial (the book of Acts ends with Paul in prison in Rome, waiting for his trial, or
        4. the person who funded Luke’s writing project.
        The fourth option is the most likely because it was common when writing literature to honor the person who funded it. The Greek word translated as *most excellent* indicates someone of important social status.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Theophilus (ID: `person:Theophilus`)

## Luke 1:5 (id: 20443)

* *Herod king of Judea*: See the profile on "Herod the Great."
* *a priest named Zechariah*: He was a descendant of Aaron, the brother of Moses ([Exodus 28:1](https://ref.ly/Exod28:1)).
* *who belonged to the priestly division of Abijah*: See the study note on Luke 1:8–9 and also [1 Chronicles 24:10](https://ref.ly/1Chr24:10).
* *Elizabeth was a descendant of Aaron*: Jews considered it especially spiritual for a priest to marry a woman from a priestly family.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:5
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 1:8-9 (ID: `20453@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Herod the Great (ID: `500534@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abijah (ID: `person:Abijah.6`); Herod (ID: `person:Herod`); Zechariah (ID: `person:Zechariah`); Elizabeth (ID: `person:Elizabeth`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 1:5–2.52 (id: 20439)

The Gospel of Luke's first two chapters describe the birth of Jesus and introduce key themes that appear later in Luke and Acts. The announcements and birth stories of Jesus and John the Baptist appear together, emphasizing their roles in bringing God's salvation. 

At each point, Luke's Gospel shows Jesus to be greater. John is the forerunner announcing the coming of Jesus, while Jesus is the Savior.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Zechariah (ID: `person:Zechariah`); Elizabeth (ID: `person:Elizabeth`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Mercy`); David (ID: `person:David`); Law (ID: `keyterm:Law`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.4`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)

## Luke 1:7 (id: 20452)

*Elizabeth was barren*: Giving birth to children was a woman's greatest role in this culture. Being unable to have children made women feel ashamed. God miraculously intervened in the lives of various Old Testament women so they could have children ([Genesis 18:10–12](https://ref.ly/Gen18:10-Gen18:12); [25:21](https://ref.ly/Gen25:21); [30:22–23](https://ref.ly/Gen30:22-Gen30:23); [Judges 13](https://ref.ly/Judg13:1-Judg13:25); [1 Samuel 1:1–28](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:1-1Sam1:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Elizabeth (ID: `person:Elizabeth`)

## Luke 1:8–9 (id: 20453)

* *Zechariah's division was on duty … he was chosen by lot:* It was common in Old Testament times to cast lots (similar to throwing dice) to determine God’s will ([1 Chronicles 26:13–16](https://ref.ly/1Chr26:13-1Chr26:16); [Nehemiah 11:1](https://ref.ly/Neh11:1); [Proverbs 16:33](https://ref.ly/Prov16:33); [Jonah 1:7](https://ref.ly/Jonah1:7)). There were 24 orders of priests that took turns serving in the temple ([1 Chronicles 24:1–19](https://ref.ly/1Chr24:1-1Chr24:19)). They cast lots to choose which priest would serve in the sanctuary of the temple. There were many priests, so this honor might come only once in a priest's life. The angel appeared at the most holy moment of Zechariah's life.
* *to enter the temple of the Lord and burn incense*: Luke uses the Greek word *naos* to refer to the temple building. He did mean the whole temple complex (*hieron*), which he refers to as "the temple courts"([Luke 2:27](https://ref.ly/Luke2:27)). The temple complex included the Holy Place and the Most Holy Place (see [Hebrews 9:1–5](https://ref.ly/Heb9:1-Heb9:5)). The priests burned incense twice a day in the Holy Place.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:8-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 1:12–13 (id: 20421)

* *he was startled and gripped with fear*: Fear and awe are common reactions when someone sees an angel or experiences the presence of God ([Judges 6:22–23](https://ref.ly/Judg6:22-Judg6:23); [13:22](https://ref.ly/Judg13:22); [Isaiah 6:5](https://ref.ly/Isa6:5)). The person is often reassured by a saying such as "Do not be afraid."
* *you are to give him the name John*: In Hebrew, the name *John* means "the Lord has shown favor." This favor came to Elizabeth and Zechariah through their son and to the Israelite nation through the Christ (Messiah), whose coming John would announce.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:12-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zechariah (ID: `person:Zechariah`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Elizabeth (ID: `person:Elizabeth`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); John (ID: `person:John`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 1:15 (id: 20422)

* *He shall never take wine or strong drink*: In the Old Testament, avoiding alcohol was part of the Nazirite vow (a special commitment to God; see [Numbers 6:1–21](https://ref.ly/Num6:1-Num6:21)). This commitment or promise to God was usually temporary (see [Acts 18:18](https://ref.ly/Acts18:18); [21:23](https://ref.ly/Acts21:23), [26](https://ref.ly/Acts21:26)).

    It was a commitment for life for some people, such as:

    + Samson ([Judges 13:4–7](https://ref.ly/Judg13:4-Judg13:7)),
        + Samuel (compare [1 Samuel 1:11](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:11)), and
        + John the Baptist.
* *filled with the Holy Spirit even from his mother’s womb*: See [Luke 1:41](https://ref.ly/Luke1:41), [44](https://ref.ly/Luke1:44).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strong Drink (ID: `realia:StrongDrink`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`)

## Luke 1:17 (id: 20423)

*he will go on before the Lord in the spirit and power of Elijah*: The prophet Malachi prophesied that God would send Elijah before the time of God's salvation (see [Malachi 3:1](https://ref.ly/Mal3:1); [4:5–6](https://ref.ly/Mal4:5-Mal4:6)). John the Baptist filled this role by preparing the way for the Lord's coming.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`)

## Luke 1:19 (id: 20424)

*I am Gabriel*: The Bible names two angels:

## Luke 1:20 (id: 20426)

* *now you will be silent and unable to speak*: God's discipline for Zechariah’s lack of faith also confirmed the prophecy. The Greek word translated as "unable to speak" in [1:22](https://ref.ly/Luke1:22) can also mean "deaf" ([7:22](https://ref.ly/Luke7:22); compare [1:62](https://ref.ly/Luke1:62)).
* Zechariah is contrasted with Mary, the mother of Jesus, who responded with faith ([1:38](https://ref.ly/Luke1:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`)

## Luke 1:24 (id: 20427)

*for five months remained in seclusion*: Why she did this is uncertain and does not seem related to any Old Testament custom or command. Perhaps it was an act of spiritual commitment to honor God for answered prayer.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Elizabeth (ID: `person:Elizabeth`)

## Luke 1:26 (id: 20428)

Nazareth was a small village in Galilee, southwest of the Sea of Galilee. People did not think highly of it (see [John 1:46](https://ref.ly/John1:46)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:26

## Luke 1:27 (id: 20429)

* *pledged in marriage*: In Jewish culture, families arranged marriages, and pledging someone in marriage was a formal legal contract. Girls were usually married by their mid\-teens.
* *Joseph, who was of the house of David*: See [Matthew 1:1–16](https://ref.ly/Matt1:1-Matt1:16). Joseph's ancestry is important because the Christ (Messiah) was to be a descendant of King David ([2 Samuel 7:11–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:11-2Sam7:16); [Isaiah 9:7](https://ref.ly/Isa9:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.4`); David (ID: `person:David`); Be Engaged (ID: `keyterm:Engaged`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 1:31 (id: 20430)

*you are to give Him the name Jesus*: The name *Jesus* means "the Lord saves."

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 1:32–33 (id: 20431)

These verses echo God’s covenant with David (a binding promise God made with King David; [2 Samuel 7:11–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:11-2Sam7:16)). This promise became the foundation for the hope of a coming Messiah (the Christ, God’s chosen king and savior; see also [Isaiah 9:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa9:6-Isa9:7); [11:1–5](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:5); [Jeremiah 23:5–6](https://ref.ly/Jer23:5-Jer23:6); [33:15–16](https://ref.ly/Jer33:15-Jer33:16); [Ezekiel 37:24–25](https://ref.ly/Ezek37:24-Ezek37:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:32-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`)

## Luke 1:35 (id: 20432)

*The Holy Spirit will come upon you*: The conception of Jesus (how he began to grow in Mary’s womb) was a miracle, by *the power of the Most High* (a title for God). The text does not explain how this happened.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`)

## Luke 1:36 (id: 20433)

*Elizabeth your relative*: The King James Version calls Elizabeth Mary’s cousin, but the Greek word is less specific. It refers more generally to a female relative, so the exact family relationship is not known.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Elizabeth (ID: `person:Elizabeth`)

## Luke 1:39 (id: 20434)

The hill country of Judah was (129 to 161 kilometers (80 to 100 miles) from Nazareth. This journey would take at least four days to travel on foot.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:39

## Luke 1:41 (id: 20436)

* *The baby leaped in her womb*(see [1:15](https://ref.ly/Luke1:15)): John was already announcing the Messiah’s coming.
* *Elizabeth was filled with the Holy Spirit*: In the Old Testament, being filled with the Spirit was often associated with a prophetic gift. The Spirit of prophecy was again appearing in Israel (see [1:67](https://ref.ly/Luke1:67); [2:25](https://ref.ly/Luke2:25), [27](https://ref.ly/Luke2:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Elizabeth (ID: `person:Elizabeth`)

## Luke 1:46–55 (id: 20437)

Mary’s song is the first of three songs of praise in the birth story of Jesus. It is called the *Magnificat* (Latin for “magnifies”). The song has many similarities to Hannah’s prayer in [1 Samuel 2:1–10](https://ref.ly/1Sam2:1-1Sam2:10). 

The fact that God cares for the oppressed and reverses their fortunes is a common theme throughout Luke’s Gospel. The coming of God’s kingdom brings salvation to rejected and outcast people.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:46-55

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Mercy`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Regard as Happy (ID: `keyterm:RegardAsHappy`); Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Savior (ID: `keyterm:Savior`); Mind (ID: `keyterm:Mind.2`); Empty (ID: `keyterm:Empty`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Luke 1:48 (id: 20438)

*all generations will call me blessed*: In Middle Eastern culture, honor and shame were very important. People also deeply valued the family name they would leave to future generations.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:48

*His arm* represents God's great power (see [Psalm 98:1](https://ref.ly/Ps98:1); [Isaiah 40:10](https://ref.ly/Isa40:10)).

## Luke 1:54–55 (id: 20441)

* Gabriel’s words in [1:32–33](https://ref.ly/Luke1:32-Luke1:33) refer back to God’s promise to King David. Here, Mary refers to God’s promise to Abraham ([Genesis 12:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:3); [17:3–8](https://ref.ly/Gen17:3-Gen17:8)).
* The arrival of Jesus brought glory to Israel and salvation to Gentiles (see [Luke 2:32](https://ref.ly/Luke2:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:54-55

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Mercy`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Luke 1:59 (id: 20442)

* God commanded every Israelite to circumcise their boys when eight days old ([Genesis 17:9–14](https://ref.ly/Gen17:9-Gen17:14); [Leviticus 12:3](https://ref.ly/Lev12:3)). Circumcision marked the child's entry into Israel's covenant community.
* *they were going to name him after his father Zechariah*: Naming a child after a relative was common. The family and friends were surprised because John was not the name of any of his relatives.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:59

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zechariah (ID: `person:Zechariah`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Circumcision (ID: `keyterm:Circumcision`)

*they made signs*: This was because Zechariah was not able to speak or hear (see study note on 1:20). Zechariah showed his faith and obedience to God by naming the child John. As a result, God restored his voice and hearing.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:62-63
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 1:20 (ID: `20426@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); John (ID: `person:John`); Tablet (ID: `realia:Tablet`)

*the Lord's hand*: This *anthropomorphism* (describing God with human characteristics, compare [1:51](https://ref.ly/Luke1:51)) meant that God was at work in John’s life.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:66

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 1:67–79 (id: 20446)

This second song of praise in the birth story of Jesus is called the *Benedictus* (Latin for “Blessed” or “Praise”). The song expects that God’s Messiah (his chosen leader) will save the Israelites from their enemies. Salvation includes physical rescue and forgiveness of sins ([1:77](https://ref.ly/Luke1:77)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:67-79

* *He has raised up a horn of salvation*: A *horn of salvation* refers to the horn of a strong animal like a ram or an ox. To *raise up a horn* means to show strength and power (see also [Psalms 89:24](https://ref.ly/Ps89:24); [132:17](https://ref.ly/Ps132:17)).
* *in the house of His servant David*: See study note on Luke 1:32–33.

## Luke 1:71 (id: 20449)

*salvation from our enemies*: This refers to what many people in Israel hoped for. They expected God to rescue them from their physical enemies, especially the Romans. But Jesus came to bring a deeper salvation. He saves people from sin, from Satan, and from death.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:71

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

* *And you, child*: Zechariah turned from speaking about the coming Messiah to address his newborn son, John, who would prepare the Messiah’s way.
* *called a prophet of the Most High*: JJohn was the last and greatest in the line of Old Testament prophets (see [7:28](https://ref.ly/Luke7:28)).

## Luke 1:80 (id: 20454)

* *the child grew and became strong in spirit*: Compare [2:40](https://ref.ly/Luke2:40), [52](https://ref.ly/Luke2:52); [1 Samuel 2:21](https://ref.ly/1Sam2:21), [26](https://ref.ly/1Sam2:26); [3:19](https://ref.ly/1Sam3:19).
* John likely lived in the wilderness west of the Jordan River and southeast of Jerusalem. This area was a place of spiritual testing ([Deuteronomy 8:2](https://ref.ly/Deut8:2); [Psalms 78](https://ref.ly/Ps78:1-Ps78:72), [95](https://ref.ly/Ps95:1-Ps95:11), [107](https://ref.ly/Ps107:1-Ps107:43)). It was also a place for spiritual retreat and preparation ([Exodus 3](https://ref.ly/Exod3:1-Exod3:22); [1 Kings 19:4–6](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:4-1Kgs19:6), [11–18](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:11-1Kgs19:18); [Isaiah 40:3](https://ref.ly/Isa40:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 1:80

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`)

## Luke 2:1 (id: 20720)

* Caesar Augustus was also called Octavian. He ruled the Roman Empire from 27 BC to AD 14\. Before his rule, Rome was a republic governed by the Senate. The Senate gave Augustus supreme power, and Rome became an empire. Augustus protected the empire’s borders and began the Pax Romana (“Roman Peace”). This was a long period of peace and success around the Mediterranean Sea.
* A census was an official count of the people. Rome used a census to register people for taxes.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Augustus (ID: `person:Augustus`); Register (ID: `keyterm:Register`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 2:1–7 (id: 20714)

After describing the birth of John, Luke gives a similar account of the birth of Jesus. Luke emphasizes the lowliness (very humble condition) of the birth of Jesus. Although Jesus was the Messiah (God’s chosen king and deliverer), he entered the world in humble circumstances.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:1-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Register (ID: `keyterm:Register`); Bethlehem (ID: `place:Bethlehem`); Quirinius (ID: `person:Quirinius`); Augustus (ID: `person:Augustus`); Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.4`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); David (ID: `person:David`); Be Engaged (ID: `keyterm:Engaged`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Census (ID: `keyterm:Census`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`); Manger (ID: `realia:Manger`); Inn (ID: `realia:Inn`)

## Luke 2:2 (id: 20729)

*Quirinius was governor of Syria*: He held this position from AD 6 to AD 9\. Earlier, from 6 to 4 BC, he also had authority in Judea, perhaps through a form of joint rule. He may have begun the census (an official count of people for taxes).

Historical records mention three other censuses at that time in Syria, Gaul, and Spain. It would not be unusual to have one in Judea, even though no non\-biblical record mentions it.

Jesus was born around 6 to 4 BC, before the death of Herod the Great in 4 BC (see [Matthew 2:1–19](https://ref.ly/Matt2:1-Matt2:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Quirinius (ID: `person:Quirinius`); Census (ID: `keyterm:Census`)

## Luke 2:3–4 (id: 20730)

Since Joseph was a descendant of King David*,* he returned to David’s hometown of Bethlehem ([1 Samuel 17:12](https://ref.ly/1Sam17:12), [58](https://ref.ly/1Sam17:58); [20:6](https://ref.ly/1Sam20:6)). Bethlehem is a small village in Judea, eight kilometers (five miles) south of Jerusalem. The prophet Micah foretold that the Christ (the Messiah, God’s chosen king) would be born there ([Micah 5:2](https://ref.ly/Mic5:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.4`); David (ID: `person:David`); Bethlehem (ID: `place:Bethlehem`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Register (ID: `keyterm:Register`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`)

## Luke 2:5 (id: 20747)

*Mary, who was pledged to him in marriage*: See study note on 1:27.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:5
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 1:27 (ID: `20429@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); Be Engaged (ID: `keyterm:Engaged`); Register (ID: `keyterm:Register`)

## Luke 2:7 (id: 20748)

* *wrapped Him in swaddling cloths*: This was a common way to show motherly love and care (see [Ezekiel 16:4](https://ref.ly/Ezek16:4)).
* *a* *manger*: This was a feeding container for animals.
* *there was no room for them in the inn*: The word translated as *inn* does not refer to a commercial inn (a place where travelers pay to stay). Such inns were likely not found in a small town like Bethlehem. It more likely refers to a guest room in a private home or a simple shelter for travelers. Since there was no usual place to stay, Joseph and Mary went to an area where animals were kept.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Manger (ID: `realia:Manger`); Inn (ID: `realia:Inn`)

## Luke 2:8 (id: 20749)

* Shepherds were sometimes described in Judaism as wandering people and even as dishonest troublemakers. However, this view was not the same everywhere. In the Old Testament, shepherds are often seen in a positive way (for example, [Psalm 23:1](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1)). Jesus came to people like these, who were ordinary, poor, and humble.
* *residing in the fields*: This detail suggests a warmer time of year, perhaps from March to November, although some shepherds stayed outside all year. We do not know the exact month Jesus was born.
* They guarded their flocks against thieves and wild animals.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Guard Against (ID: `keyterm:GuardAgainst`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Luke 2:9–10 (id: 20750)

* *terrified*: Like Zechariah and Mary, the angel's presence was both glorious and frightening. The angel told the shepherds, *"Do not be afraid!"* (compare [1:11–13](https://ref.ly/Luke1:11-Luke1:13), [28–30](https://ref.ly/Luke1:28-Luke1:30)).
* *I bring you good news*: This Greek verb (*euangelizō*, meaning “to bring good news”) comes from the same root as the word *euangelion* (“good news” or “gospel”). In the Berean Standard Bible, *euangelion* is often translated as “gospel” (for example, [Galatians 2:5](https://ref.ly/Gal2:5), [7](https://ref.ly/Gal2:7), [14](https://ref.ly/Gal2:14)). Isaiah’s prophecies describe God’s salvation as "good tidings" or "good news" (see, for example, [Isaiah 52:7](https://ref.ly/Isa52:7); [61:1](https://ref.ly/Isa61:1)).
* *for all the people*: In Luke’s writings, *the people* (a singular term in Greek) always refers to the people of Israel. The shepherds were told that Israel’s salvation had arrived. It would eventually go to all nations.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:9-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Luke 2:11 (id: 20715)

The word "Messiah" means "Anointed One." It comes from the Hebrew word *mashiakh* and is translated into Greek as *Christos*. The Messiah refers to the promised savior who would come from King David's family line (see study note on 1:32–33). As the apostles started preaching the good news about Jesus to the non\-Jewish world, Christians started using *Christos* more as a name than a title.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:11
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 1:32-33 (ID: `20431@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Savior (ID: `keyterm:Savior`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); David (ID: `person:David`)

## Luke 2:12 (id: 20716)

*a sign*: Shepherds would notice the contrast between the baby’s security and his lowly circumstances. He was wrapped in strips of cloth, but he was lying in a manger, an animal feeding box. This was unusual and showed that God was at work.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Manger (ID: `realia:Manger`)

## Luke 2:13 (id: 20717)

*the heavenly host*: These armies from heaven show God's ultimate power and authority ([2 Kings 6:17](https://ref.ly/2Kgs6:17); [Psalm 148:2](https://ref.ly/Ps148:2)). *LORD of Hosts* is a common Old Testament name for God (for example, [1 Samuel 1:11](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:11); [17:45](https://ref.ly/1Sam17:45); [2 Samuel 7:8](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:8); [Isaiah 5:16](https://ref.ly/Isa5:16); [Romans 9:29](https://ref.ly/Rom9:29); [James 5:4](https://ref.ly/Jas5:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Heavenly (ID: `keyterm:Heavenly`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Luke 2:14 (id: 20718)

* *Glory to God in the highest*: This short hymn is known as the *Gloria in excelsis Deo* from the Latin translation of the first line. The Messiah’s coming brings glory to God in the heavens and peace to humans on earth.
* *peace to men on whom His favor rests*: *Peace* means "complete well\-being," not just the absence of hostility. The traditional translation, “on earth peace, good will toward men” in the King James Version, is based on the reading of some late manuscripts. The Berean Standard Bible text follows other early versions. Peace comes to those who receive God's grace.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Height (ID: `keyterm:Height`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`)

## Luke 2:19 (id: 20719)

*Mary treasured up all these things and pondered them in her heart*: Mary kept these things in her heart. This may mean she thought deeply about what had happened and wondered what the future of Jesus would be. It may also mean that Luke learned about these events from Mary herself.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 2:20 (id: 20721)

*glorifying and praising God*: These are major themes throughout Luke’s Gospel (for example, [1:64](https://ref.ly/Luke1:64); [2:13](https://ref.ly/Luke2:13), [28](https://ref.ly/Luke2:28); [5:25–26](https://ref.ly/Luke5:25-Luke5:26); [7:16](https://ref.ly/Luke7:16); [13:13](https://ref.ly/Luke13:13); [17:15](https://ref.ly/Luke17:15), [18](https://ref.ly/Luke17:18); [18:43](https://ref.ly/Luke18:43); [19:37](https://ref.ly/Luke19:37); [23:47](https://ref.ly/Luke23:47); [24:53](https://ref.ly/Luke24:53)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:20

## Luke 2:22 (id: 20723)

* *the time of purification*: The law of Moses required women to complete a 40\-day ceremony of purification after giving birth ([Leviticus 12](https://ref.ly/Lev12:1-Lev12:8)). After this period, they would offer the sacrifice commanded by the law ([Luke 2:24](https://ref.ly/Luke2:24)).
* *to present Him to the Lord*: Every person or thing born first belonged to the Lord, so the Israelites would *present* (or consecrate) them to God. Humans were bought back with a redemption price (see [Exodus 13:1–2](https://ref.ly/Exod13:1-Exod13:2), [15](https://ref.ly/Exod13:15); [34:19–20](https://ref.ly/Exod34:19-Exod34:20); [Numbers 3:11–13](https://ref.ly/Num3:11-Num3:13); [18:15–16](https://ref.ly/Num18:15-Num18:16); [Nehemiah 10:35–36](https://ref.ly/Neh10:35-Neh10:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Law (ID: `keyterm:Law`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`)

## Luke 2:24 (id: 20724)

*A pair of turtledoves or two young pigeons*: This quote from [Leviticus 12:8](https://ref.ly/Lev12:8) refers to the ceremony to make a woman ceremonially pure after giving birth to a child. Joseph and Mary offered the sacrifice prescribed for the poor.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Law (ID: `keyterm:Law`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Dove (ID: `fauna:Dove`); Rooster (ID: `fauna:Rooster`)

## Luke 2:25 (id: 20725)

* *Simeon … was righteous and devout*: This is like Zechariah and Elizabeth ([1:6](https://ref.ly/Luke1:6)). They represent the faithful people of Israel who were waiting for God's salvation ([Isaiah 10:20–22](https://ref.ly/Isa10:20-Isa10:22)). Some think Simeon was a priest, but the text only says he was a *devout* man in Jerusalem.
* *He was waiting for the consolation of Israel*: This refers to [Isaiah 40:1](https://ref.ly/Isa40:1), which promised "comfort" to Israel after returning from exile in Babylon.
* *the Holy Spirit was upon him*: See study note on Luke 1:41.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:25
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 1:41 (ID: `20436@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

*the Lord's Christ* (or *the Lord's Anointed*): This title was first used for kings like David. It later came to refer to the Christ or Messiah (God’s chosen king and deliverer), who would come from David’s family line.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

This third great hymn of the birth narrative is called the *Nunc dimittis* (“now dismiss”), from the first words of the Latin translation. The hymn praises God for allowing Simeon to see God’s salvation.

## Luke 2:30 (id: 20731)

*my eyes have seen Your salvation*: This phrase echoes [Isaiah 52:12](https://ref.ly/Isa52:12). The wording is similar and recalls the language of that passage.

## Luke 2:31 (id: 20732)

The plural form of *people* means all peoples (see study note on 2:9–10). It includes both Jews and Gentiles. Gentiles are people who are not part of the Jewish people.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:31
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 2:9-10 (ID: `20750@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 2:32 (id: 20733)

* The prophet Isaiah predicted that the kingdom of God would bring salvation to all the nations ([Isaiah 42:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa42:6-Isa42:7); [49:6](https://ref.ly/Isa49:6)).
* *the glory of your people Israel*: This quote from [Isaiah 46:13](https://ref.ly/Isa46:13) says that God’s salvation will bring glory to Israel. Israel’s role in the Old Testament was to bring glory to God by revealing God’s light to the nations.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`)

## Luke 2:34 (id: 20734)

* *to cause the rise and fall of many in Israel*: Many Israelites did not accept Jesus as the Christ (see [Isaiah 8:14–15](https://ref.ly/Isa8:14-Isa8:15)).
* *a sign*: See [Luke 11:29–30](https://ref.ly/Luke11:29-Luke11:30); compare [Isaiah 8:18](https://ref.ly/Isa8:18).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.2`); Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`)

## Luke 2:35 (id: 20735)

* *The thoughts of many hearts*: Israel's true inner response to God would be made known through how they respond to Jesus.
* *a sword will pierce your soul as well*: Mary would experience great pain watching her son suffer and die on the cross.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 2:36–38 (id: 20736)

* Anna is the Greek version of the Hebrew name Hannah, Samuel’s mother ([1 Samuel 1–2](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:1-1Sam2:36)). Jewish tradition recognizes seven Old Testament women as prophets:

    + Sarah,
        + Miriam,
        + Deborah,
        + Hannah,
        + Abigail,
        + Huldah, and
        + Esther.
        In this story, Anna was also a prophetic witness to the identity of Jesus as the Christ.

* *the tribe of Asher*: This wasone of the 10 northern tribes of Israel. Asher was the eighth son of the patriarch Jacob ([Genesis 30:12–13](https://ref.ly/Gen30:12-Gen30:13)). Most of the Israelites who returned from exile in Babylon were from the tribe of Judah. Other Israelites also knew the family line of their tribe during the time of Jesus. For example, the apostle Paul was "of the tribe of Benjamin" ([Philippians 3:5](https://ref.ly/Phil3:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:36-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Phanuel (ID: `person:Phanuel`); Anna (ID: `person:Anna`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Tribe of Asher (ID: `group:Asher`); Liberation (ID: `keyterm:Liberation`); Asher (ID: `person:Asher`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 2:37 (id: 20737)

* *was a widow to the age of eighty\-four*: In the ancient world, people associated old age with wisdom and honor.
* *She never left the temple*: This is likely an intentional extreme statement (an exaggeration). It is the same as saying something like, “She was there all the time.” This statement shows her total commitment to God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

*He was filled with wisdom, and the grace of God was upon Him* (compare [1:80](https://ref.ly/Luke1:80); [2:52](https://ref.ly/Luke2:52)): The two statements about the wisdom of Jesus ([2:40](https://ref.ly/Luke2:40), [52](https://ref.ly/Luke2:52)) emphasize the story of his unusual wisdom at a young age.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:40

## Luke 2:41 (id: 20740)

The Jewish Passover feast celebrates God's deliverance of Israel from slavery in Egypt. It was one of three major annual feasts required for Jewish men to attend ([Exodus 23:14–17](https://ref.ly/Exod23:14-Exod23:17); [Deuteronomy 16:16](https://ref.ly/Deut16:16)). The family of Jesus faithfully followed God’s law and practiced their faith.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Luke 2:41–51 (id: 20739)

Jesus visiting Jerusalem at age 12 is the only story from his childhood in the gospels. The main theme is his early spiritual insight. This includes how he is increasingly aware of his special relationship with God and his unique mission.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:41-51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 2:42 (id: 20741)

*He was twelve years old*: A Jewish boy took on his covenant responsibilities (duties in God’s agreement with his people) at about age 13\. The *bar mitzvah* (a later ceremony marking this stage) developed afterward to express this custom.

By bringing Jesus to Jerusalem at this time, his parents were preparing him to take his place in the covenant community.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:42


## Luke 2:44 (id: 20742)

*Assuming He was in their company*: Joseph and Mary were probably traveling with a large caravan of relatives and friends from Galilee. Jesus probably spent a lot of time with his friends and relatives, so his parents were not worried at first.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:44


## Luke 2:46 (id: 20743)

The *three days* likely included one day traveling from Jerusalem, one day traveling back, and one day searching. 

•*in the temple courts*: He was in the temple complex, not the sanctuary. 

•*listening to them and asking them questions*: Jesus was acting like a student. In rabbinic teaching (the teaching methods of Jewish teachers), a teacher often answered a student’s question by asking more questions. Jesus asked wise questions and gave wise answers, and this amazed the teachers.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 2:47 (id: 20744)

*astounded at His understanding and His answers*: Jesus fulfilled [Isaiah 11:2](https://ref.ly/Isa11:2).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:47


## Luke 2:49 (id: 20745)

*Did you not know that I had to be in My Father’s house?*: Jesus knew his most important relationship was with his Father in heaven.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 2:51 (id: 20746)

Although Jesus recognized his relationship to his heavenly Father, he was obedient to his earthly parents.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 2:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`)

## Luke 3:1 (id: 20932)

* *In the fifteenth year of the reign of Tiberius Caesar*: Tiberius Caesar began to rule with his stepfather, Caesar Augustus, in AD 11\. He became the sole emperor in AD 14 and ruled until AD 37\. Luke may be counting from either AD 11 or AD 14\. If so, John began his public ministry around AD 26/27 or AD 29/30\. Jesus began his own ministry soon after John.
* Pontius Pilate was governor of Judea from AD 26 to 36 (see “Pontius Pilate” Profile).
* *Herod tetrarch of Galilee*: See “Herod Antipas” Profile.
* Antipas’s half\-brother Philip was another son of Herod the Great. Philip ruled as tetrarch of the region northeast of Galilee from 4 BC until his death in AD 34\. *Tetrarch* means “the ruler of a fourth of a kingdom.” The term later meant any minor ruler. Philip was a half brother of both Antipas and Herod Philip ([Matthew 14:3](https://ref.ly/Matt14:3); [Mark 6:17](https://ref.ly/Mark6:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:1
* **Associated Articles:** Herod Antipas (ID: `500541@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`); Pontius Pilate (ID: `500543@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Philip (ID: `person:Philip.3`); Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`); Abilene (ID: `place:Abilene`); Lysanias (ID: `person:Lysanias`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Ituraea (ID: `place:Ituraea`); Augustus (ID: `person:Augustus`); Trachonitis (ID: `place:Trachonitis`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`)

## Luke 3:1–4.13 (id: 20925)

After the birth story, Luke prepares his audience for the public ministry of Jesus. He describes the work of John the Baptist, who comes before the Messiah (the Christ, God's chosen king). He also tells about the baptism, family line (genealogy), and temptation of Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:1-2

## Luke 3:2 (id: 20939)

* Annas and Caiaphas were Jewish high priests. Annas served from AD 6 to 15 until the Romans removed him. His son\-in\-law, Caiaphas, was the high priest from AD 18 to 36\. Luke mentions both names because Annas was still very influential among the Jews (see [John 18:13–14](https://ref.ly/John18:13-John18:14), [24](https://ref.ly/John18:24); [Acts 4:6](https://ref.ly/Acts4:6)).
* *the word of God* *came to* *John son of Zechariah*: John's role as a prophet was similar to that of the Old Testament prophets (see, for example, [Isaiah 6](https://ref.ly/Isa6:1-Isa6:13); [Jeremiah 1](https://ref.ly/Jer1:1-Jer1:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John`); Annas (ID: `person:Annas`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Caiaphas (ID: `person:Caiaphas`); Zechariah (ID: `person:Zechariah`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`)

## Luke 3:3 (id: 20943)

*preaching a baptism of repentance for the forgiveness of sins*: John seems to have adapted the practice of baptism from Jewish rites using water to cleanse, or set apart, people and objects for God’s service (compare [Leviticus 8:6](https://ref.ly/Lev8:6)). John called people to repent of their sins and prepare spiritually for the Messiah’s coming. Their baptism publicly demonstrated their repentance and spiritual cleansing.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Luke 3:4–6 (id: 20944)

* The quotation is from the ancient Greek version of [Isaiah 40:3–5](https://ref.ly/Isa40:3-Isa40:5). It describes God bringing the Jews back from exile in Babylon. After they returned ([Ezra 1–2](https://ref.ly/Ezra1:1-Ezra2:70)). John shouted in the wilderness to prepare God’s people for the Lord’s coming. God’s salvation is portrayed as a new exodus, bringing deliverance like the first exodus from Egypt.
* *make straight paths for Him … the rough ways smooth*: This image reflects the Middle Eastern custom of preparing a road for a king (compare [Luke 19:36–38](https://ref.ly/Luke19:36-Luke19:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Straightness (ID: `keyterm:Straightness`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Luke 3:7 (id: 20945)

God's *coming wrath* is the judgment for all who reject the gospel. Israel as a nation would experience this wrath in the destruction of Jerusalem in AD 70 (see [21:20–23](https://ref.ly/Luke21:20-Luke21:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`)

## Luke 3:8 (id: 20946)

* *We have Abraham as our father*: Some Jews believed that being part of Abraham's family line guaranteed their salvation (see [John 8:33–39](https://ref.ly/John8:33-John8:39); [Acts 7:2](https://ref.ly/Acts7:2); [Romans 4:1](https://ref.ly/Rom4:1)).
* *And do not begin to say to yourselves, 'We have Abraham as our father.'*: God chose the Israelites as his people, but he could reject them. If he wanted, he could create children of Abraham from these very stones (compare [Hosea 1:3–9](https://ref.ly/Hos1:3-Hos1:9); [Romans 9:6–8](https://ref.ly/Rom9:6-Rom9:8), [27–28](https://ref.ly/Rom9:27-Rom9:28); [11:17–21](https://ref.ly/Rom11:17-Rom11:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Luke 3:9 (id: 20947)

*every tree that does not produce good fruit*: If Israel did not repent and produce good works, God would judge them to be as worthless as a vineyard without grapes (see [Isaiah 5:1–7](https://ref.ly/Isa5:1-Isa5:7); compare[Luke 13:7–9](https://ref.ly/Luke13:7-Luke13:9); [20:9–19](https://ref.ly/Luke20:9-Luke20:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Axe (ID: `realia:Axe`)

## Luke 3:11 (id: 20926)

*two tunics*: The Greek word refers to a long shirt worn next to the skin. Even the smallest surplus should be shared with others in need.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`)

## Luke 3:12 (id: 20927)

*even tax collectors*: In the first century, many people strongly disliked tax collectors. They were known for taking extra money and were seen as working for the Roman rulers, who were widely hated.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`)

## Luke 3:14 (id: 20928)

These soldiers were probably not Roman, but local Jewish troops under the authority of Herod Antipas. They might have been assigned to protect the tax collectors. John does not condemn either tax collectors or soldiers but calls them to honesty and integrity.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:14


## Luke 3:16 (id: 20929)

* *the straps of whose sandals I am not worthy to untie*: The untying of sandals was done only by slaves. John was showing great honor to Jesus the Messiah.
* *Baptize you with the Holy Spirit and fire*: Old Testament prophets said God would give his Spirit to his people in the last times ([Isaiah 32:15](https://ref.ly/Isa32:15), [Ezekiel 36:28–29](https://ref.ly/Ezek36:28-Ezek36:29); [Joel 2:28](https://ref.ly/Joel2:28)). This prophecy partly came true on the day of Pentecost ([Acts 2](https://ref.ly/Acts2:1-Acts2:47)). Fire often represents judgment (see [Luke 3:9](https://ref.ly/Luke3:9)). It can also represent the spiritual cleansing and purifying of God's people (see [1 Corinthians 3:10–15](https://ref.ly/1Cor3:10-1Cor3:15); compare [Isaiah 4:4](https://ref.ly/Isa4:4); [Malachi 3:2](https://ref.ly/Mal3:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strap (ID: `realia:Strap`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); John (ID: `person:John`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Sandal (ID: `realia:Sandal`)

## Luke 3:17 (id: 20930)

* After being harvested, the kernels of wheat would be beaten off the stalks and then tossed in the air with a winnowing fork to separate them from the chaff. The heavy grain would fall to the ground as the lighter chaff blew away. This is an image of separation for judgment.
* *unquenchable fire*: See [Isaiah 66:24](https://ref.ly/Isa66:24).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Clean Out (ID: `keyterm:CleanOut`); Winnowing Fork (ID: `realia:WinnowingFork`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Threshing Floor (ID: `realia:ThreshingFloor`); Barn (ID: `realia:Barn`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`)

## Luke 3:19–20 (id: 20931)

Herod Antipas was the ruler of Galilee*. He* divorced his first wife and married Herodias*.* Herodias was the wife of his half\-brother Herod Philip. 

When John publicly criticized Herod for this sin, Herod imprisoned and later executed him (compare [9:9](https://ref.ly/Luke9:9); [Mark 6:16–29](https://ref.ly/Mark6:16-Mark6:29)). Herod’s execution of John is also recorded by the Jewish historian Josephus (*Antiquities* 18\.5\.2\).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Herodias (ID: `person:Herodias`); John (ID: `person:John`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Luke 3:21 (id: 20934)

* *And as He was praying*: Luke shows that Jesus trusted God and depended on him through prayer ([5:16](https://ref.ly/Luke5:16); [6:12](https://ref.ly/Luke6:12); [9:18](https://ref.ly/Luke9:18), [28](https://ref.ly/Luke9:28); [11:1](https://ref.ly/Luke11:1); [22:32](https://ref.ly/Luke22:32), [41](https://ref.ly/Luke22:41), [44](https://ref.ly/Luke22:44); [23:34](https://ref.ly/Luke23:34), [46](https://ref.ly/Luke23:46)).
* *heaven was opened*: This image was a sign of

    + God’s presence ([Isaiah 64:1](https://ref.ly/Isa64:1)),
        + divine revelation ([Ezekiel 1:1](https://ref.ly/Ezek1:1); [John 1:51](https://ref.ly/John1:51)), and
        + the end of an age (a major period in God’s plan; [Revelation 4:1](https://ref.ly/Rev4:1); [15:5](https://ref.ly/Rev15:5); [19:11](https://ref.ly/Rev19:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 3:21–22 (id: 20933)

The baptism of Jesus marked the beginning of his public ministry and his anointing as the Christ (the Messiah, the one chosen and set apart by God). God spoke from heaven and confirmed who Jesus is and the mission he came to do.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:21-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Beloved (ID: `keyterm:Beloved`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Dove (ID: `fauna:Dove`)

## Luke 3:22 (id: 20935)

* The Holy Spirit might have looked like a dove, or it might have come down as a dove would.
* *You are My beloved Son; in You I am well pleased*: This statement connects with [Psalm 2:7](https://ref.ly/Ps2:7) and [Isaiah 42:1](https://ref.ly/Isa42:1).

    + [Psalm 2:7](https://ref.ly/Ps2:7) shows that Jesus is the Messiah (the Christ, the promised King) from David’s family line.
        + [Isaiah 42:1](https://ref.ly/Isa42:1) shows the servant of the Lord (the one chosen to carry out God’s work).
        God again declared who Jesus is from heaven at his transfiguration (when his appearance was changed in glory; [Luke 9:35](https://ref.ly/Luke9:35)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Beloved (ID: `keyterm:Beloved`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Dove (ID: `fauna:Dove`)

## Luke 3:23 (id: 20937)

* *Jesus Himself was about thirty years old*: This is the only place in the New Testament that mentions how old Jesus was during his public ministry. The number is not exact. Jesus was born between 6 and 4 BC (see study note on 2:2). He began his ministry between AD 27 and 29 (see study note on 3:1). This means he was probably about 31 to 35 years old when he began his public ministry.
* *He was regarded as the son of Joseph*: People understood Jesus to be Joseph’s son. He was Joseph’s legal son, but not his biological son.
* *the son of Heli*: If this passage gives Mary’s genealogy (a family line), as some think (see study note on 3:23–38), then Joseph may have been Heli’s son\-in\-law rather than his biological son. This is one possible meaning of the Greek sentence.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:23
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 2:2 (ID: `20729@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 3:1 (ID: `20932@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 3:23-38 (ID: `20936@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.4`); Heli (ID: `person:Heli`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 3:23–38 (id: 20936)

This record of the family line of Jesus is in the opposite order from Matthew’s list ([Matthew 1:1–17](https://ref.ly/Matt1:1-Matt1:17)). Luke traces the family line of Jesus back to Adam. This may show that Jesus is connected to all people as the Son of God ([Luke 3:38](https://ref.ly/Luke3:38)). 

Matthew’s list moves from Abraham down to Joseph. It shows that Jesus is the rightful heir (legal ruler) of David’s throne and the one who receives God’s promises to Abraham.

The two lists are very different. Because of this, some people suggest that Luke’s list is actually Mary’s family line (see study note on 3:23). If so, Mary may have been one of Luke’s main sources when he wrote his Gospel.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:23-38
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 3:23 (ID: `20937@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Luke 3:27 (id: 20938)

Zerubbabel was the governor of Judea. The Persians chose him when the Jews returned from exile in Babylon. He supervised the effort to build the temple again ([Ezra 3:2](https://ref.ly/Ezra3:2), [8](https://ref.ly/Ezra3:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joanan (ID: `person:Joanan`); Zerubbabel (ID: `person:Zerubbabel`); Rhesa (ID: `person:Rhesa`); Neri (ID: `person:Neri`); Shealtiel (ID: `person:Shealtiel`)

## Luke 3:31 (id: 20940)

Nathan was King David’s third son, born in Jerusalem ([2 Samuel 5:14](https://ref.ly/2Sam5:14)). This is not the same person as Nathan the prophet ([2 Samuel 7:2](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Menna (ID: `person:Menna`); Mattatha (ID: `person:Mattatha`); Melea (ID: `person:Melea`); Nathan (ID: `person:Nathan`); David (ID: `person:David`)

## Luke 3:32 (id: 20941)

*Boaz* married Ruth, who was from Moab (see [Ruth 2–4](https://ref.ly/Ruth2:1-Ruth4:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Boaz (ID: `person:Boaz`); Salmon (ID: `person:Salmon`); Obed (ID: `person:Obed`); Jesse (ID: `person:Jesse`); Nahshon (ID: `person:Nahshon`)

## Luke 3:38 (id: 20942)

* Seth was the third son of Adam and Eve. He was born after Cain killed Abel and God sent Cain away ([Genesis 4:25–26](https://ref.ly/Gen4:25-Gen4:26)).
* Adam was the son of God because he had no earthly father. God created him. When Satan tempted Jesus in the wilderness, Satan repeatedly says to Jesus, “If you are the Son of God...” Adam was the first son of God and failed when tested. But Jesus, the Son of God in the fullest sense, successfully resisted temptation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 3:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Enosh (ID: `person:Enosh`); Seth (ID: `person:Seth`); Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 4:1–13 (id: 20948)

The devil tried to tempt Jesus to ignore his Father's plan of salvation by seeking power and glory for himself. The temptation in the wilderness for 40 days is similar to Israel's 40 years of testing there. Israel failed the test, but Jesus succeeded.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:1-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Put to the Test (ID: `keyterm:PutToTheTest`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Bow Down (ID: `keyterm:BowDown`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Protect (ID: `keyterm:Protect`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Pinnacle (ID: `realia:Pinnacle`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 4:2 (id: 20960)

*tempted by the devil*: The term *diabolos* ("devil") is a Greek translation of the Hebrew *satan*, meaning “accuser” or “adversary” ([1 Chronicles 21:1](https://ref.ly/1Chr21:1); [Job 1:1–2:13](https://ref.ly/Job1:1-Job2:13); [Zechariah 3:1–2](https://ref.ly/Zech3:1-Zech3:2)). Luke uses both terms ("devil," [Luke 4:3](https://ref.ly/Luke4:3), [6](https://ref.ly/Luke4:6), [13](https://ref.ly/Luke4:13); [8:12](https://ref.ly/Luke8:12); "Satan," [10:18](https://ref.ly/Luke10:18); [11:18](https://ref.ly/Luke11:18); [13:16](https://ref.ly/Luke13:16); [22:3](https://ref.ly/Luke22:3), [31](https://ref.ly/Luke22:31)). 

Satan tempted Adam and Eve and failed. Jesus is called the second Adam because he is a new beginning for humanity. He resisted temptation, unlike Adam and Eve. Because of this, he reversed the judgment against Adam and Eve.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Put to the Test (ID: `keyterm:PutToTheTest`)

## Luke 4:3 (id: 20968)

*If You are the Son of God*: Both Israel and Adam are called God’s son ([3:38](https://ref.ly/Luke3:38); [Exodus 4:22–23](https://ref.ly/Exod4:22-Exod4:23); [Hosea 11:1](https://ref.ly/Hos11:1)). Jesus, the Son of God in the fullest sense, succeeded where Adam and Israel failed.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`)

## Luke 4:4 (id: 20972)

*Man shall not live on bread alone*: Israel complained constantly about hunger in the wilderness, but Jesus depended on God’s strength to sustain him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 4:6 (id: 20973)

*I can give it to anyone I wish*: The New Testament elsewhere describes Satan’s limited rule over the present world order ([John 12:31](https://ref.ly/John12:31); [14:30](https://ref.ly/John14:30); [16:11](https://ref.ly/John16:11); [Ephesians 2:2](https://ref.ly/Eph2:2); [1 John 5:19](https://ref.ly/1John5:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 4:8 (id: 20974)

*Worship the Lord your God and serve Him only*: When Israel encountered difficulties in the wilderness, they started to worship false gods ([Deuteronomy 9:12](https://ref.ly/Deut9:12)). Jesus refused to worship Satan.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Bow Down (ID: `keyterm:BowDown`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 4:10–11 (id: 20949)

After being driven back twice by Scripture, the devil quoted Scripture to Jesus. He cited a passage that speaks of God’s protection for his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:10-11

## Luke 4:12 (id: 20950)

*Do not put the Lord your God to the test*: In the wilderness, Israel constantly tested God’s faithfulness, but Jesus responded with complete trust in God.

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Put to the Test (ID: `keyterm:PutToTheTest`)

## Luke 4:13 (id: 20951)

The next *opportune time* could have been when Judas betrayed Jesus ([22:3–6](https://ref.ly/Luke22:3-Luke22:6), [21–22](https://ref.ly/Luke22:21-Luke22:22), [47–48](https://ref.ly/Luke22:47-Luke22:48)). The greatest test occurred in the garden of Gethsemane ([22:39–46](https://ref.ly/Luke22:39-Luke22:46)).

## Luke 4:14 (id: 20952)

* *Jesus returned to Galilee*: The story of the ministry of Jesus in Galilee begins here, and continues through [9:50](https://ref.ly/Luke9:50).
* *in the power of the Spirit*: At his baptism, Jesus was anointed and empowered to accomplish his role as the Messiah (see [3:22](https://ref.ly/Luke3:22); [4:1](https://ref.ly/Luke4:1), [14](https://ref.ly/Luke4:14), [18](https://ref.ly/Luke4:18)).

## Luke 4:16–30 (id: 20953)

The sermon Jesus gave in Nazareth showed what his whole public ministry would be about. He returned to the synagogue in his hometown to announce the good news that God’s salvation had come. The people were pleased at first. But then Jesus reminded them that God also reaches out to Gentiles, not only to Jews. This made the crowd so angry that they tried to kill him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:16-30

## Luke 4:18–19 (id: 20954)

*the year of the Lord’s favor*: This refers to the Year of Jubilee ([Leviticus 25:8–55](https://ref.ly/Lev25:8-Lev25:55)). During this time, people forgave debts, freed slaves, and returned land to its original owners. In [Isaiah 61](https://ref.ly/Isa61:1-Isa61:11), the Year of Jubilee is a metaphor for God’s salvation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:18-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`)

## Luke 4:21 (id: 20955)

* *is fulfilled*: Jesus announced that his presence marked the new age of salvation (God’s saving work among his people).
* *Today*: Compare [2:11](https://ref.ly/Luke2:11); [5:26](https://ref.ly/Luke5:26); [19:9](https://ref.ly/Luke19:9); [23:43](https://ref.ly/Luke23:43).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`); Written (ID: `keyterm:Scripture`)

## Luke 4:23 (id: 20956)

* *Physician, heal yourself!*: This was a common saying in ancient times. It could mean something like, "show your ability to heal" or "help your own people, not just others."
* *that You did in Capernaum*: See [4:31–44](https://ref.ly/Luke4:31-Luke4:44).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Capernaum (ID: `place:Capernaum`)

## Luke 4:25–26 (id: 20957)

*widow of Zarephath*: See [1 Kings 17–18](https://ref.ly/1Kgs17:1-1Kgs18:46).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:25-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); Zarephath (ID: `place:Zarephath`); Sidon (ID: `place:Sidon`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 4:27 (id: 20958)

*Naaman the Syrian*: See [2 Kings 5](https://ref.ly/2Kgs5:1-2Kgs5:27).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Naaman (ID: `person:Naaman.3`); Syrians (ID: `group:Syria`); Elisha (ID: `person:Elisha`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Luke 4:28 (id: 20959)

The people in the synagogue were angry because Jesus mentioned that God had shown favor to Gentiles (non\-Jewish people). Many Jews believed that only they received God’s favor. They were also living under Roman rule, and Gentiles ruled over them. So Jesus’s words felt very offensive to them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 4:30 (id: 20961)

*Jesus passed through the crowd*: The main emphasis is on the royal status and authority of Jesus. His time had not yet come. His time had not yet come (compare [John 7:30](https://ref.ly/John7:30), [44](https://ref.ly/John7:44); [8:20](https://ref.ly/John8:20), [59](https://ref.ly/John8:59)). This attempt to kill Jesus pointed ahead to his crucifixion. His escape also points ahead to his resurrection.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:30


## Luke 4:31 (id: 20962)

Jesus left Nazareth and made Capernaum his main base during his work in Galilee (see [Mark 2:1](https://ref.ly/Mark2:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Capernaum (ID: `place:Capernaum`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`)

## Luke 4:33 (id: 20963)

*the spirit of an unclean demon*: Here *unclean* means "defiled" or "wicked." 

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 4:34 (id: 20964)

* *Have you come to destroy us?*: Although there was only one demon present, the use of *us* suggests that all of Satan’s forces knew about and were fearful of the coming of Jesus.
* *I know who You are*: In ancient times, people believed that naming a spiritual power gave them control over it. The demon might have been trying to gain power over Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`)

## Luke 4:36 (id: 20965)

*With authority and power He commands*: Magicians and exorcists often used long rituals and special spoken formulas (incantations). The people were amazed that Jesus simply spoke a word with his own authority and drove out the demon.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 4:38 (id: 20966)

* *the home of Simon*: Simon was from Bethsaida ([John 1:44](https://ref.ly/John1:44)), but he currently lived and worked as a fisherman in Capernaum.
* *whose mother\-in\-law*: Peter was married (see [1 Corinthians 9:5](https://ref.ly/1Cor9:5)). The text does not mention his wife's name.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 4:39 (id: 20967)

*rebuked* *the fever*: Luke uses the same Greek word when Jesus "rebuked the demon" ([4:35](https://ref.ly/Luke4:35)). Jesus has full authority over both physical and spiritual powers.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`)

## Luke 4:41 (id: 20969)

* *You are the Son of God!*: This was a title used for the Christ (God’s chosen king and deliverer, the Messiah; see [2 Samuel 7:14](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:14); [Psalms 2:7](https://ref.ly/Ps2:7); [89:26–27](https://ref.ly/Ps89:26-Ps89:27)). Luke often connects the titles *Christ* and *the Son of God* ([Luke 9:35](https://ref.ly/Luke9:35); [22:66–71](https://ref.ly/Luke22:66-Luke22:71); [Acts 9:20](https://ref.ly/Acts9:20), [22](https://ref.ly/Acts9:22)).
* would not allow them to speak: Jesus often showed his authority over demons by making them silent. He wanted to reveal his identity in his own way, not through their announcement.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`)

## Luke 4:43 (id: 20970)

* *preach the good news*: The Greek verb here is related to the noun often translated as "gospel" or "good news" (see study note on 2:9–10; see also [1:19](https://ref.ly/Luke1:19); [7:22](https://ref.ly/Luke7:22); [Matthew 4:23](https://ref.ly/Matt4:23); [24:14](https://ref.ly/Matt24:14); [Mark 1:1](https://ref.ly/Mark1:1), [15](https://ref.ly/Mark1:15); [16:15](https://ref.ly/Mark16:15); [Acts 8:4](https://ref.ly/Acts8:4), [12](https://ref.ly/Acts8:12); [Romans 1:1–6](https://ref.ly/Rom1:1-Rom1:6), [15–17](https://ref.ly/Rom1:15-Rom1:17); [10:15–17](https://ref.ly/Rom10:15-Rom10:17); [Galatians 1:6–9](https://ref.ly/Gal1:6-Gal1:9); [Ephesians 2:14–18](https://ref.ly/Eph2:14-Eph2:18); [3:6–7](https://ref.ly/Eph3:6-Eph3:7)).
* The kingdom of God was the main message of Jesus (see [Mark 1:15](https://ref.ly/Mark1:15)). God began to show his rule (his kingly authority) through what Jesus said and did.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:43
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 2:9-10 (ID: `20750@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 4:44 (id: 20971)

Luke likely used the word *Judea* in a broad sense, meaning “the land of the Jews.” At that time, Jesus was ministering in Galilee in the north (see [5:1](https://ref.ly/Luke5:1)), not in Judea in the south.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 4:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 5:1–11 (id: 20975)

This is the first time Luke records Jesus calling the disciples to follow him. Matthew and Mark describe an earlier calling of the four brothers who were fishermen ([Matthew 4:18–22](https://ref.ly/Matt4:18-Matt4:22); [Mark 1:16–20](https://ref.ly/Mark1:16-Mark1:20)). Jesus showed great authority in the miraculous catch of fish and in his call of Simon Peter.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:1-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Casting Net (ID: `realia:CastingNet`); Fishnet (ID: `realia:Fishnet`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); James (ID: `person:James`); Gennesaret (ID: `place:Gennesaret`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Zebedee (ID: `person:Zebedee`)

## Luke 5:3 (id: 20996)

*And sitting down, He taught the people from the boat*: By sitting a little away from the shore, Jesus had a place to speak without the people pressing against him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Luke 5:4 (id: 20997)

*let down your nets for a catch*: Peter was naturally unsure when a teacher told fishermen how to catch fish. It seemed strange for three reasons:

1. When the sun rose, the fish went down to the bottom of the lake. This is why they usually fished at night.
2. They used a heavy net that hung from the side of the boat, held by two men (or stretched between two boats), so they could not fish in deep water.
3. They had just fished all night and caught nothing, so it now seemed even less likely they would catch anything.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Casting Net (ID: `realia:CastingNet`); Fishnet (ID: `realia:Fishnet`)

## Luke 5:7 (id: 20998)

* James and John were in the other boat. Along with their father Zebedee, they were business partners with Peter and Andrew (see [5:10](https://ref.ly/Luke5:10); [Mark 1:19–20](https://ref.ly/Mark1:19-Mark1:20); [Mark 1:16](https://ref.ly/Mark1:16)).
* *boats so full that they began to sink*: This dramatic and amazing event showed the authority of Jesus over the forces of nature.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Luke 5:8 (id: 20999)

*I am a sinful man*: Compare [Isaiah 6:5](https://ref.ly/Isa6:5).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`)

## Luke 5:10 (id: 20976)

Jesus used fishing as a picture to explain how his followers would gather people into the kingdom of God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** James (ID: `person:James`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Zebedee (ID: `person:Zebedee`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`)

## Luke 5:11 (id: 20977)

*they left everything*: They gave up their work, security, and family ties, which was remarkable in a culture that placed great importance on loyalty to family and clan.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Luke 5:12 (id: 20978)

*who was covered with leprosy*: In ancient times, *leprosy* did not refer only to the modern disease known as leprosy (Hansen's disease). It included various skin problems. To prevent it from spreading, ancient societies forced lepers to live on the edges of communities. The law of Moses gave instructions for how to identify and isolate leprosy ([Leviticus 13–14](https://ref.ly/Lev13:1-Lev14:57)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 5:13 (id: 20979)

* *Jesus reached out His hand and touched the man*: Most people feared catching the disease through physical contact. But Jesus showed compassion. Neither disease nor evil could contaminate him. Instead, he brought healing and salvation.
* *Be clean!*: This act had spiritual and physical significance, since leprosy separated Israelites from social contact and religious practice.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Infectious Skin Disease (ID: `keyterm:InfectiousSkinDisease`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`)

## Luke 5:14 (id: 20980)

* Jesus often told those he healed not to tell anyone. He did this to avoid drawing too much public attention. But news about these events spread quickly (see [5:15–16](https://ref.ly/Luke5:15-Luke5:16); see also “The Secret of the Messiah” Theme Note).
* *go, show yourself to the priest and present the offering*: A priest had to declare that those healed of leprosy were clean. Then the priest would offer a sacrifice on their behalf.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:14
* **Associated Articles:** The Secret of the Messiah (ID: `500351@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`)

## Luke 5:17 (id: 20982)

* *Pharisees*: The Pharisees were a group of Jewish teachers who carefully followed the law of Moses and their traditions. See “The Pharisees” Profile.
* *teachers of the law*:These teachers werealso called *scribes* and *lawyers*. They were experts in explaining the law of Moses. Most teachers of the law were Pharisees, but some were Sadducees. The Sadducees were another Jewish group who had different beliefs, especially about the law and the resurrection (life after death).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:17
* **Associated Articles:** The Pharisees (ID: `500535@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 5:17–26 (id: 20981)

The healing of the paralyzed man began the conflicts between Jesus and the religious leaders that continued through his public ministry until his crucifixion in Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:17-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bring In (ID: `keyterm:BringIn`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Blaspheme (ID: `keyterm:Blaspheme`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Tile (ID: `realia:Tile`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`)

## Luke 5:19 (id: 20983)

*through the tiles*: Houses in Palestine usually had flat roofs made from wooden beams covered with reeds and mud or clay. This roof may have had tiles. Or Luke may have described it this way for his Greek audience.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bring In (ID: `keyterm:BringIn`); Tile (ID: `realia:Tile`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Luke 5:22 (id: 20984)

*Knowing what they were thinking*: While the Pharisees accused Jesus of blasphemy (showing disrespect toward God), he knew what was in their minds. This is something only God can do.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 5:23–24 (id: 20985)

Jesus showed his authority to forgive sins by healing the man.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:23-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Luke 5:24 (id: 20986)

*Son of Man*: See “The Son of Man” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:24
* **Associated Articles:** The Son of Man (ID: `500342@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

## Luke 5:27 (id: 20988)

* *tax collector*: See study note on 3:12.
* Levi's tax booth was likely a place to collect money for transporting items.
* Levi was also called Matthew ([Matthew 9:9](https://ref.ly/Matt9:9); see “Matthew (Levi)” Profile).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:27
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 3:12 (ID: `20927@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Matthew (Levi) (ID: `500536@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`)
