## Luke 5:27–32 (id: 20987)

When Jesus called Levi to become his disciple, this led to a second conflict with the religious leaders. It also introduced the idea that Jesus came to save sinners.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:27-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); Matthew (ID: `person:Matthew`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Tax Office (ID: `keyterm:TaxOffice`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 5:29 (id: 20989)

*a great banquet for Jesus*: *Levi* introduced Jesus to his friends and fellow tax collectors to thank him for making Levi a disciple. It was common in the ancient world to repay honor with honor.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Matthew (ID: `person:Matthew`); Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 5:30 (id: 20990)

Meals showed a person’s social standing in the ancient world. Eating with someone meant you accepted them. The Pharisees refused to eat with tax collectors and other sinners or to enter their homes. They believed this would make them unclean. They expected Jesus, as a rabbi (a Jewish teacher), to do the same.

But Jesus broke these social rules. He ate with sinners and allowed sinful people to touch him ([5:29](https://ref.ly/Luke5:29); [7:39](https://ref.ly/Luke7:39); [15:1](https://ref.ly/Luke15:1)). The religious leaders strongly criticized him for this and mockingly called him “a friend of tax collectors and sinners” ([7:34](https://ref.ly/Luke7:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`)

## Luke 5:31–32 (id: 20991)

Jesus responded that, like a doctor*,* his mission was to work with sick people*,* not the healthy. Jesus was not called to minister to self\-righteous people, but to those who recognized their spiritual need for God’s grace and healing.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:31-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 5:33 (id: 20992)

* *John’s disciples*: Most Jewish rabbis had students who learned from them and served them (see also [Acts 19:1–7](https://ref.ly/Acts19:1-Acts19:7)).
* *frequently fast and pray*: Many devout Jews fasted two days a week ([Luke 18:12](https://ref.ly/Luke18:12)). Fasting was associated with spiritual preparation and repentance ([Esther 4:16](https://ref.ly/Esth4:16); [2 Samuel 12:22](https://ref.ly/2Sam12:22); [Joel 1:14](https://ref.ly/Joel1:14); [Jonah 3:5](https://ref.ly/Jonah3:5)). Fasting is not a way to become righteous (to be made right with God; [Isaiah 58:4–5](https://ref.ly/Isa58:4-Isa58:5); [Jeremiah 14:12](https://ref.ly/Jer14:12)). But it is a spiritual pratice that helps a person become closer to God. Fasting is often connected to prayer and spiritual retreat.
* *but Yours keep on eating and drinking*: The point was that they did not fast regularly.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); John (ID: `person:John`)

## Luke 5:34–35 (id: 20993)

*Can you make the guests of the bridegroom fast*: Just as it would be inappropriate for anyone to fast at a wedding celebration, it was inappropriate for people to fast while the Christ (the Messiah, God's chosen king) was with them. God’s final salvation is a great wedding feast that God prepares for his people ([Isaiah 25:6–8](https://ref.ly/Isa25:6-Isa25:8); [Luke 14:15–24](https://ref.ly/Luke14:15-Luke14:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:34-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`)

## Luke 5:36–38 (id: 20994)

*a new garment … new wine*: New cloth shrinks when it is washed and can tear old cloth. New wine expands as it ferments and can burst old, brittle wineskins. In both cases, both the old and the new are ruined.

These examples show that the old and the new do not work well together. Jesus did not come to patch up the old covenant, but to bring a new one. The kingdom of God brings a new way of thinking and living.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:36-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Must (ID: `realia:Must`); Skin-Bottle (ID: `realia:Skin-bottle`); Patch (ID: `realia:Patch`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)

## Luke 5:39 (id: 20995)

*The old is better*: The religious leaders were resistant to change.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 5:39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`)

## Luke 6:1 (id: 21008)

*His disciples began to pick the heads of grain*: The law allowed this kind of grazing while walking through someone else’s field ([Deuteronomy 23:24–25](https://ref.ly/Deut23:24-Deut23:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`)

## Luke 6:1–11 (id: 21000)

The conflicts between Jesus and the religious leaders continued with two disagreements about the Sabbath. The law of Moses required the Jews to rest on the Sabbath ([Exodus 20:8–11](https://ref.ly/Exod20:8-Exod20:11); [Deuteronomy 5:13–14](https://ref.ly/Deut5:13-Deut5:14)). But the Pharisees had forgotten that the real reason for the Sabbath was to benefit human beings.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:1-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); David (ID: `person:David`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Do Good (ID: `keyterm:DoGood`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 6:2 (id: 21014)

*Why are you doing what is unlawful on the Sabbath?*: They did not accuse the disciples of stealing grain, but of working on the Sabbath day. They were breaking rules established by oral tradition.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`)

## Luke 6:3 (id: 21020)

While fleeing from Saul ([1 Samuel 21](https://ref.ly/1Sam21:1-1Sam21:15)), David and his companions went to Nob, a place where the tabernacle was located. The priest Ahimelech gave them the consecrated Bread of the Presence (special bread set before God) that only priests were allowed to eat ([Exodus 25:30](https://ref.ly/Exod25:30); [Leviticus 24:9](https://ref.ly/Lev24:9)). 

Jesus meant that meeting human needs is more important than strictly following the law. There may also be a comparison between David and Jesus, since both were chosen and anointed as kings of Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); David (ID: `person:David`)

## Luke 6:5 (id: 21024)

*Lord of the Sabbath*: The saying is a play on words with a double meaning: 

* *Son of man* often means "human being." So Jesus was affirming that the Sabbath day was made to serve human beings.
* *Son of Man* was also a title for the Christ (God's chosen king; see [Daniel 7:13–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:13-Dan7:14)).

Jesus is *Lord of the Sabbath* because he created it.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`)

## Luke 6:6–7 (id: 21025)

* *were watching Him closely*: Jesus had a reputation for putting human needs above Sabbath commands. So, the religious leaders hoped to catch Jesus working on the Sabbath day.
* *if He would heal on the Sabbath*: The Jewish rabbis in the time of Jesus debated whether the law allowed people to offer medical help on the Sabbath. It was usually allowed only in extreme emergencies. The *withered hand* was clearly not an emergency.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 6:8 (id: 21026)

*Get up and stand among us*: Jesus did not avoid controversy. He was willing to confront the Pharisees’ hypocrisy directly.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:8


## Luke 6:9 (id: 21027)

*to do good or to do evil, to save life or to destroy it?*: This question challenged the religious leaders' intentions. They were plotting against Jesus (an evil act) because he was going to heal someone (a good act).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Do Good (ID: `keyterm:DoGood`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Luke 6:12 (id: 21001)

*He spent the night in prayer to God*: See study note on 3:21. Jesus prayed before important events and decisions such as choosing the Twelve, who would carry on his ministry after him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:12
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 3:21 (ID: `20934@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)

## Luke 6:13 (id: 21002)

* *Disciples* were students and followers, while *apostles* were specially commissioned representatives.
* The number *twelve* symbolically represents the twelve tribes of Israel. The apostles represented the restored people of God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 6:14 (id: 21003)

*Simon*, *whom He named Peter*: The name *Peter* means "rock" or "stone" (see [Matthew 16:18](https://ref.ly/Matt16:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bartholomew (ID: `person:Bartholomew`); Philip (ID: `person:Philip`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Andrew (ID: `person:Andrew`); James (ID: `person:James`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`)

## Luke 6:15 (id: 21004)

* Matthew, also called Levi, was the tax collector for the Romans (see [5:27](https://ref.ly/Luke5:27); [Matthew 9:9](https://ref.ly/Matt9:9); [10:3](https://ref.ly/Matt10:3)).

* *Simon called* *the Zealot*: This title probably meant that he had joined the Zealot movement, a group of Jews actively seeking to overthrow the Roman government.
* The apostles Jesus chose were very different from each other. These two men would not normally have worked together.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simon (ID: `person:Simon.2`); Matthew (ID: `person:Matthew`); Alphaeus (ID: `person:Alphaeus`); Thomas (ID: `person:Thomas`); James (ID: `person:James.2`)

## Luke 6:16 (id: 21005)

* Judas (son of James) was probably also called Thaddaeus ([Matthew 10:3](https://ref.ly/Matt10:3); [Mark 3:18](https://ref.ly/Mark3:18)). He was a different person from Judas Iscariot or Jude the half\-brother of Jesus (who wrote the New Testament book of Jude).
* *Judas Iscariot*: *Iscariot* probably means “from Kerioth,” a village 19 kilometers (12 miles) south of Hebron.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judas (ID: `person:Judas`); Judas (ID: `person:Judas.2`); James (ID: `person:James.2`)

## Luke 6:17 (id: 21007)

*all over Judea, Jerusalem, and the seacoast of Tyre and Sidon*: Tyre and Sidon were coastal cities on the Mediterranean Sea northwest of Galilee. Jesus was gaining wide popularity.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Sidon (ID: `place:Sidon`)

## Luke 6:17–49 (id: 21006)

The main theme of the great sermon of Jesus is that those who repent and enter the kingdom of God will live in a truly righteous way.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:17-49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Do Good (ID: `keyterm:DoGood`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); Condemn (ID: `keyterm:Condemn`); Foundation (ID: `realia:Foundation`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Beam (ID: `realia:Beam`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Sidon (ID: `place:Sidon`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Storeroom (ID: `realia:Storeroom`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`); Acacia (ID: `flora:Acacia`); Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`); Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Luke 6:20–21 (id: 21009)

*poor … you who hunger*: Compare [Matthew 5:3](https://ref.ly/Matt5:3), [6](https://ref.ly/Matt5:6). This statement likely refers to both physical and spiritual poverty. Poverty and suffering tend to make us more dependent on God. The Old Testament includes many promises that God will feed and comfort his people ([Psalms 22:26](https://ref.ly/Ps22:26); [107:36–41](https://ref.ly/Ps107:36-Ps107:41); [126:1–6](https://ref.ly/Ps126:1-Ps126:6); [Isaiah 49:10–13](https://ref.ly/Isa49:10-Isa49:13); [51:3](https://ref.ly/Isa51:3); [65:13](https://ref.ly/Isa65:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:20-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 6:20–23 (id: 21010)

God blesses those who admit their weaknesses and seek strength from God. The value system of God’s kingdom is radically different from the world’s value system, in which power and strength represent success.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:20-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 6:23 (id: 21011)

*treated the prophets in the same way*: See “The Suffering Prophet” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:23
* **Associated Articles:** The Suffering Prophet (ID: `500360@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 6:24–26 (id: 21012)

Promises of blessing for the poor and oppressed are balanced in Luke with predictions of sorrow for the rich and powerful. Those who trust in themselves will be humbled, while those who depend on God will be blessed. The Old Testament prophets often pronounced woes against nations and rulers who oppressed God’s people and rejected his authority (for example, [Isaiah 3:11](https://ref.ly/Isa3:11); [5:8](https://ref.ly/Isa5:8); [Jeremiah 50:27](https://ref.ly/Jer50:27); [Zechariah 11:17](https://ref.ly/Zech11:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:24-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 6:27 (id: 21013)

*Love your enemies*: People were commonly taught in the ancient world to love their family and friends and hate their enemies. Jesus announced a radical new ethic of loving even one’s enemies and overcoming evil through self\-sacrificial love for others, not through violence and hatred (compare [Romans 12:14–21](https://ref.ly/Rom12:14-Rom12:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`)

## Luke 6:30 (id: 21015)

*if anyone takes what is yours, do not demand it back*: This means we can stop the cycle of hate and violence by responding with good, not evil ([Romans 12:17](https://ref.ly/Rom12:17)). We win the world through love that expects nothing in return. 

Jesus was not promoting injustice, as though believers should let criminals take advantage of the weak. God established governments to punish evildoers ([Romans 13:1–5](https://ref.ly/Rom13:1-Rom13:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:30


## Luke 6:31 (id: 21016)

*Do to others as you would have them do to you*: This saying is often called the Golden Rule. Versions of the Golden Rule appear in many religious and philosophical traditions. In the teaching of Jesus, however, love for all people (including enemies) is not merely one virtue among others but the foundational ethic that governs all behavior.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:31


## Luke 6:34–36 (id: 21017)

The law of Moses did not allow charging interest on loans to fellow Israelites in order to prevent taking advantage of those who were poor ([Exodus 22:25](https://ref.ly/Exod22:25); [Leviticus 25:35–37](https://ref.ly/Lev25:35-Lev25:37); [Deuteronomy 23:20](https://ref.ly/Deut23:20)). Jesus also taught his followers to lend without expecting repayment. By doing good even to those who do wrong, Christians act as children of the Most High and imitate our Father in heaven, who is merciful to everyone (compare [Romans 5:8](https://ref.ly/Rom5:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:34-36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); Do Good (ID: `keyterm:DoGood`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`)

## Luke 6:37–42 (id: 21018)

*Do not judge*: Jesus condemned judging others in a way that is not honest by acting better than them. Those who judge others this way will experience that same standard. God wants his people to love others by warning them about the dangers of sin ([Galatians 6:1](https://ref.ly/Gal6:1); [Hebrews 3:13](https://ref.ly/Heb3:13)). To encourage is an act of love. To judge as a hypocrite is an act of pride.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:37-42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Beam (ID: `realia:Beam`); Condemn (ID: `keyterm:Condemn`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Luke 6:38 (id: 21019)

*pressed down, shaken together*: A generous grain merchant would top off the buyer’s container, press it down, shake it to make more room, and then pour until the grain overflowed into the buyer’s lap.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:38


## Luke 6:41 (id: 21021)

*the beam in your own eye*: Jesus did not say that we should ignore the speck in another person’s eye. Instead, he said that we must first correct our own faults so that we can see clearly enough to help remove the speck. Jesus condemned hypocritical judgment, but he wanted his followers to lovingly help one another turn away from sin.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Beam (ID: `realia:Beam`)

## Luke 6:45 (id: 21022)

What people receive and value inside themselves will shape how they live. What they keep within their thoughts and desires will come out in their actions.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Storeroom (ID: `realia:Storeroom`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 6:49 (id: 21023)

*he one who hears My words and does not act on them*: See [Ezekiel 33:31–33](https://ref.ly/Ezek33:31-Ezek33:33).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 6:49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Foundation (ID: `realia:Foundation`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 7:1–10 (id: 21028)

This episode shows God’s message of salvation is for Gentiles as well as Jews.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:1-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Capernaum (ID: `place:Capernaum`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 7:2 (id: 21039)

*centurion*: This type of Roman officer was in charge of a group of about 100 soldiers (a *century*). A Roman legion had 60 centuries, totaling 6,000 soldiers.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:2


## Luke 7:3 (id: 21047)

Jewish elders were leaders in the Jewish community. Matthew’s shorter account says that the centurion approached Jesus himself ([Matthew 8:5–13](https://ref.ly/Matt8:5-Matt8:13)). Luke gives more details and explains that Jewish elders, and later the centurion’s friends, spoke to Jesus for him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 7:5 (id: 21053)

*for he loves our nation and has built our synagogue*: Wealthy Gentiles often supported Jewish communities in the Roman Empire. This suggests that the centurion might have been "God\-fearing" like Cornelius in [Acts 10:2](https://ref.ly/Acts10:2). A God\-fearing person was a Gentile who worshiped the God of Israel but had not fully converted to Judaism.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 7:6 (id: 21054)

*I am not worthy to have You come under my roof*: The man was probably trying to respect Jewish customs. Faithful Jews would not enter the home of a Gentile, because they believed this would make them ceremonially unclean (see [Acts 10:28](https://ref.ly/Acts10:28); [11:12](https://ref.ly/Acts11:12)). The centurion might also have been acknowledging the greater status of Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 7:8 (id: 21055)

*I myself am a man under authority*: The Roman army was famous for its organization and discipline. The centurion recognized authority when he saw it in the words and actions of Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 7:9 (id: 21056)

*I tell you, not even in Israel have I found such great faith*: While many in Israel were unresponsive to the good news of salvation, some Gentiles believed and found salvation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Luke 7:11 (id: 21030)

The location of the village of Nain is uncertain. It might be the modern town of Nein, 9\.6 kilometers (6 miles) southeast of Nazareth.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Nain (ID: `place:Nain`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 7:11–17 (id: 21029)

Jesus raised three people from the dead: 

* the dead man in this passage,

* Jairus’s daughter ([8:40–56](https://ref.ly/Luke8:40-Luke8:56)), and
* Lazarus ([John 11:38–44](https://ref.ly/John11:38-John11:44)).

Jesus has power over life and death (compare [1 Kings 17:17–24](https://ref.ly/1Kgs17:17-1Kgs17:24); [2 Kings 4:18–37](https://ref.ly/2Kgs4:18-2Kgs4:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:11-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Nain (ID: `place:Nain`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Bier (ID: `realia:Bier`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 7:12 (id: 21031)

* *a dead man being carried out*: In Judaism, a large crowd of mourners and intense wailing signified great love for the person who died. In some cases, people hired people to mourn.
* Widows were the most vulnerable members of society, and God had special concern for them ([Exodus 22:22](https://ref.ly/Exod22:22); [Deuteronomy 10:18](https://ref.ly/Deut10:18); [27:19](https://ref.ly/Deut27:19)). This widow’s only son was her means of support and hope for the future. His death was a terrible loss (see [Jeremiah 6:26](https://ref.ly/Jer6:26); [Amos 8:10](https://ref.ly/Amos8:10); [Zechariah 12:10](https://ref.ly/Zech12:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 7:13–14 (id: 21032)

Here, the word *coffin* may refer to:

* a long box in which someone is buried, or
* a funeral bier (a frame or portable stand used to carry the coffin or the body of the person who had died).

A Jew became ceremonially unclean by touching a dead body, coffin, or bier ([Leviticus 21:1](https://ref.ly/Lev21:1), [11–12](https://ref.ly/Lev21:11-Lev21:12)). But when Jesus touched the man with leprosy, he did not become unclean ([Luke 5:13](https://ref.ly/Luke5:13)). Instead, he replaced disease and death with cleansing and life.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:13-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Bier (ID: `realia:Bier`)

## Luke 7:16 (id: 21033)

*A great prophet*: Jesus was like the Old Testament prophets Elijah and Elisha.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Luke 7:18–23 (id: 21034)

*John’s disciples*: See study note on 5:33. John was now in prison ([3:19–20](https://ref.ly/Luke3:19-Luke3:20)). He wondered if Jesus was truly the Christ since Jesus did not act like a powerful political king who would defeat the Romans. In response, Jesus defined his role as God's chosen king.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:18-23
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 5:33 (ID: `20992@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John`); Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`)

## Luke 7:22 (id: 21035)

*The blind receive sight, the lame wal*k: The prophet Isaiah describes God’s salvation as the renewal of his fallen creation ([Isaiah 26:19](https://ref.ly/Isa26:19); [29:18–19](https://ref.ly/Isa29:18-Isa29:19); [35:5–6](https://ref.ly/Isa35:5-Isa35:6); [61:1–2](https://ref.ly/Isa61:1-Isa61:2)). Jesus did not come to end Roman rule over Judea. This may explain why he leaves out the words "proclaim liberty to the captives" when he quotes [Isaiah 61:1](https://ref.ly/Isa61:1). He came for a greater purpose: to reverse the effects of sin and death.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); John (ID: `person:John`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`)

## Luke 7:23 (id: 21036)

*Blessed is the one who does not fall away on account of Me*: The blessing is for those who are willing to leave behind their own expectations about the Christ and accept Jesus’s path of suffering.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`)

## Luke 7:27 (id: 21037)

The quotation is from [Malachi 3:1](https://ref.ly/Mal3:1). In [Malachi 4:5–6](https://ref.ly/Mal4:5-Mal4:6), this messenger is identified as the prophet Elijah. Though John denied that he was Elijah ([John 1:21–23](https://ref.ly/John1:21-John1:23)), he came in the spirit and power of Elijah to prepare the way for God's chosen king ([Luke 1:17](https://ref.ly/Luke1:17); see [3:4–6](https://ref.ly/Luke3:4-Luke3:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Luke 7:28 (id: 21038)

Jesus praised John as the greatest person who ever lived under the old covenant. But even the least person in the kingdom of God is greater than John. 

The blessings of the new covenant are

* free and complete forgiveness of sins,
* the indwelling presence of the Holy Spirit, and
* intimate knowledge of God.

These blessings give believers in Jesus a new and greater position than even John enjoyed.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); John (ID: `person:John`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 7:31–35 (id: 21040)

*To what, then, can I compare the men of this generation?*: Jesus compares Israel to fickle children in the marketplace who play games of make\-believe. They called John the Baptist to dance (a wedding game), but the serious prophet refused. Instead, he called them to mourn and repent of their sins. 

Then they called on Jesus to mourn (a funeral game), but he was the bridegroom who announced the joyful celebration banquet of the kingdom of God. It would not have been appropriate for him to mourn (compare [Mark 2:19–20](https://ref.ly/Mark2:19-Mark2:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:31-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); John (ID: `person:John`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Flute (ID: `realia:Flute`)

## Luke 7:33 (id: 21041)

John the Baptist did not spend his time eating bread or drinking wine. He lived a simple life of self\-denial that fit his role as a prophet. He announced God’s kingdom, the coming judgment of God, and the need for repentance and cleansing from sin.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); John (ID: `person:John`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`)

## Luke 7:35 (id: 21042)

*Wisdom is vindicated*: This means wisdom is proven right by the lives of those who follow it. The book of Proverbs describes wisdom as a woman who calls God’s people to a life of godliness and obedience to God’s commands ([Proverbs 1:20–33](https://ref.ly/Prov1:20-Prov1:33); [8:1–9:6](https://ref.ly/Prov8:1-Prov9:6)). Wisdom’s children are those who live righteously, as God’s word instructs.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`)

## Luke 7:36 (id: 21044)

*one of the Pharisees invited Jesus to eat with him*: Jesus has more positive contact with the Pharisees in Luke than in the other Gospels. He was a respected teacher and healer, so it is not surprising that this Pharisee invited him to dinner. Not all Pharisees opposed Jesus. Some were interested in his teaching, while others were simply curious about him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 7:36–50 (id: 21043)

What Jesus said in [7:35](https://ref.ly/Luke7:35) is shown in this story by one of “wisdom’s children” (see study note on 7:35). She is a repentant sinner (a person who turns away from sin). She shows deep gratitude for the forgiveness she has received.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:36-50
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 7:35 (ID: `21042@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Simon (ID: `person:Simon.6`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Myrrh (ID: `realia:Myrrh`); Myrrh (ID: `flora:Myrrh`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Owe (ID: `keyterm:Owe`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Alabaster Jar (ID: `realia:AlabasterJar`); Denarius (ID: `realia:Denarius`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 7:37 (id: 21045)

* *a sinful woman*: This suggests she was known in the community as a person with a bad moral reputation. Some people think she may have been a prostitute, but the passage does not state this explicitly.
* Alabaster is a soft stone, often white, yellow, or red. People used it to make costly containers, such as this beautiful jar.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Alabaster Jar (ID: `realia:AlabasterJar`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Myrrh (ID: `realia:Myrrh`); Myrrh (ID: `flora:Myrrh`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 7:38 (id: 21046)

* *she stood behind Him*: At a banquet, people ate while lying on their sides around a low table, with their feet stretched out behind them. Because of this, the woman could not reach the head of Jesus to anoint him (to pour oil on him as a sign of honor), so she poured the perfume on his feet.

    *wipe them with her hair*: In that culture, a woman normally kept her hair covered in public. Letting her hair down could bring shame and even give a husband reason for divorce. By wiping the feet of Jesus with her hair and kissing them, she showed deep respect and honor. She set aside normal social rules because she was so thankful.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Myrrh (ID: `realia:Myrrh`); Myrrh (ID: `flora:Myrrh`)

## Luke 7:40 (id: 21048)

*But Jesus answered him*: See study note on 5:22.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:40
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 5:22 (ID: `20984@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simon (ID: `person:Simon.6`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 7:41 (id: 21049)

*five hundred denarii*: This was nearly two years' wages for a person who earned a living by working different jobs each day. In first\-century Galilee, people who were poor often owed large debts to people who were wealthy. 

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Owe (ID: `keyterm:Owe`); Denarius (ID: `realia:Denarius`)

## Luke 7:43 (id: 21050)

*the one who was forgiven more*: Those who had been forgiven the most responded with greater gratitude and love. The woman did her extraordinary act of sacrificial love because Jesus had forgiven her sins.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simon (ID: `person:Simon.6`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Luke 7:44–46 (id: 21051)

Simon failed to show Jesus the usual acts of hospitality (ways to welcome a guest). The roads were dusty, so a servant would normally wash a guest’s feet. People in the Middle East often greet guests with a kiss on both cheeks, and this is still common today. Hosts also put olive oil on a guest’s head as a way to show honor and respect.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:44-46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simon (ID: `person:Simon.6`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Myrrh (ID: `realia:Myrrh`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Myrrh (ID: `flora:Myrrh`)

## Luke 7:47 (id: 21052)

*for* *she has loved much*: It was not that her great love for Jesus caused him to forgive her, but that the forgiveness she had already received from Jesus caused her love.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 7:47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Luke 8:2 (id: 21074)

*Magdalene* means “from Magdala.” Madgala was a village north of Tiberias on the western side of the Sea of Galilee. Mary Magdalene plays an important role in the accounts of the burial and resurrection of Jesus ([24:10](https://ref.ly/Luke24:10); [Mark 15:40](https://ref.ly/Mark15:40), [47](https://ref.ly/Mark15:47); [John 19:25](https://ref.ly/John19:25); [20:1](https://ref.ly/John20:1), [18](https://ref.ly/John20:18)). Some people have said that Mary Magdalene was the woman in [Luke 7:36–38](https://ref.ly/Luke7:36-Luke7:38). This idea has led to the belief that she was a former prostitute. However, the Bible does not say this, and there is no clear evidence for it.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mary Magdalene (ID: `person:Marymagdalene`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`)

## Luke 8:2–3 (id: 21064)

*as well as some women*: In the time of Jesus, Jewish rabbis (religious teachers with authority) did not have women disciples. So this was unusual. Some of these women, like Joanna, came from wealthy families and gave financial support to the ministry of Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Joanna (ID: `person:Joanna`); Susanna (ID: `person:Susanna`); Mary Magdalene (ID: `person:Marymagdalene`); Chuza (ID: `person:Chuza`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`)

## Luke 8:4–15 (id: 21081)

The parable of the farmer scattering seed shows that people respond in different ways to the message of Jesus about God’s kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:4-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`); Acacia (ID: `flora:Acacia`); Honest (ID: `keyterm:Honest`); Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`); Put to the Test (ID: `keyterm:PutToTheTest`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Luke 8:5 (id: 21092)

* *A farmer* would scatter *seed* and then plow it into the soil.
* The *seed* that fell on a walking path did not remain in the *soil*, so it was stepped on and eaten by birds.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`)

## Luke 8:6 (id: 21093)

*fell on rocky ground*: There was often a limestone layer 23 to 30\.5 centimeters (9 to 12 inches) under the soil, so that the rain was trapped and seed sprouted quickly in the moist soil. But when the rain stopped, the sun evaporated the trapped moisture and the plant died.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:6


## Luke 8:7 (id: 21094)

*fell among thorns*: One type of weed looked very much like wheat as it grew, but it had larger roots and took away the moisture and nutrients that the wheat needed.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`); Acacia (ID: `flora:Acacia`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`)

## Luke 8:8 (id: 21095)

*He who has ears to hear, let him hear*: This meant everyone who was willing to listen.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:8


## Luke 8:10 (id: 21057)

* *The mysteries of the kingdom of God*: What was hidden in the past about God’s reign was now revealed through the words and actions of Jesus.
* *though seeing … they may not understand*: This quote is from the Greek version of [Isaiah 6:9](https://ref.ly/Isa6:9). This passage appears often in the New Testament to explain why Israel rejected the gospel (see also [Matthew 13:14–15](https://ref.ly/Matt13:14-Matt13:15); [Mark 4:12](https://ref.ly/Mark4:12); [John 12:40](https://ref.ly/John12:40); [Acts 28:26–27](https://ref.ly/Acts28:26-Acts28:27)). In its original context, the passage refers to God’s certain judgment of Israel at the hands of the Assyrians. Israel’s sin and rebelliousness had taken them beyond the point of no return, so God blinded them in preparation for judgment. Those who rejected the message of Jesus were unable to see the truth clearly.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 8:11 (id: 21058)

*the seed is the word of God*: This means Jesus is preaching the good news about God's kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`)

## Luke 8:12 (id: 21059)

Satan tries to stop those who have yet to trust in Christ from understanding the truth.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 8:13 (id: 21060)

Deep roots indicate spiritual depth and maturity. The Greek word translated *testing* can also mean “temptation” or “trials.”

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`); Put to the Test (ID: `keyterm:PutToTheTest`)

## Luke 8:16–17 (id: 21061)

A small clay lamp was filled with olive oil and placed in a space in the wall (an alcove). The message of Jesus gives light to those who listen, and it shows their true attitudes.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:16-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lampstand (ID: `realia:Lampstand.2`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Luke 8:18 (id: 21062)

*Pay attention, therefore, to how you listen*: Compare with [Proverbs 9:9](https://ref.ly/Prov9:9).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:18


## Luke 8:19–20 (id: 21063)

Jesus had four brothers: 

* James,
* Joseph,
* Judas, and
* Simon.

The Gospels also mention sisters, but not how many Jesus had ([Matthew 13:55](https://ref.ly/Matt13:55); [Mark 6:3](https://ref.ly/Mark6:3)). These children were probably born to Joseph and Mary after the virgin birth of Jesus. However, some Christians believe that Mary remained a virgin throughout her life. According to this view, Joseph was a widower who already had children from an earlier marriage when he married Mary.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:19-20


## Luke 8:21 (id: 21065)

*those who hear God's word and carry it out*: Jesus was not rejecting his family. He was saying that spiritual relationships, like being part of God's family, are more important than family bonds.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 8:22–25 (id: 21066)

When Jesus calmed the *windstorm*, he showed his control over the elements. This act suggested he was God, since God controls the seas (see [Psalms 65:7](https://ref.ly/Ps65:7); [89:9](https://ref.ly/Ps89:9); [104:6–7](https://ref.ly/Ps104:6-Ps104:7); [107:23–32](https://ref.ly/Ps107:23-Ps107:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:22-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Luke 8:23 (id: 21067)

A powerful windstorm came down on the lake. Severe storms often occur on the Sea of Galilee, which is in a valley surrounded by high hills.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:23


## Luke 8:24 (id: 21068)

*Jesus got up and rebuked the wind and the raging waters*: Just as Jesus *rebuked* demons and diseases ([4:39,](https://ref.ly/Luke4:39) [41](https://ref.ly/Luke4:41)), he showed his authority by rebuking the sea (compare [2 Samuel 22:16](https://ref.ly/2Sam22:16); [Psalms 18:15](https://ref.ly/Ps18:15); [104:7](https://ref.ly/Ps104:7); [106:9](https://ref.ly/Ps106:9); [Isaiah 50:2](https://ref.ly/Isa50:2); [Nahum 1:4](https://ref.ly/Nah1:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`)

## Luke 8:26 (id: 21070)

*the region of the Gerasenes*: Gerasa was a city in the Decapolis, located about 48 kilometers (30 miles) southeast of the Sea of Galilee. Jesus traveled to this mainly Gentile (non\-Jewish) area on purpose to teach and help the people there.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gerasa (ID: `place:Gerasa`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`)

## Luke 8:26–39 (id: 21069)

*Jesus had commanded the unclean spirit to come out of the man*:This is known as an exorcism. This story showed the power Jesus has over spiritual forces. It also contrasted the healed man's faith and commitment to Jesus with the fear and rejection of people in the town.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:26-39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Gerasa (ID: `place:Gerasa`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); Abyss (ID: `keyterm:Abyss`); Legion (ID: `deity:Legion`); Be Demon Possessed (ID: `keyterm:DemonPossessed`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); A Man (ID: `person:GenericMale`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Fetter (ID: `realia:Fetter`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Chain (ID: `realia:Chain`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 8:27 (id: 21071)

*he stayed in the tombs*: Touching a tomb or a dead body made a Jew unclean, or ceremonially defiled. Jesus willingly entered this unclean place to rescue a person enslaved by Satan.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 8:28 (id: 21072)

*Jesus, Son of the Most High God*: See study notes on 4:34, 41.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:28
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 4:34 (ID: `20964@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 4:41 (ID: `20969@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 8:29 (id: 21073)

*unclean* *spirit*: The word *unclean* means not fit to be in God’s presence. An unclean spirit is a spirit that opposes God and does not belong near him (see also study note on 4:33).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:29
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 4:33 (ID: `20963@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fetter (ID: `realia:Fetter`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Chain (ID: `realia:Chain`)

## Luke 8:30 (id: 21075)

A Roman legion included about 6,000 soldiers. This number shows that the man had many demons.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Legion (ID: `deity:Legion`); A Man (ID: `person:GenericMale`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 8:31 (id: 21076)

*the Abyss*: The Greek word *abussos* means "bottomless." It often refers to the prison of evil spirits or fallen angels under the surface of the world ([Revelation 9:1–2](https://ref.ly/Rev9:1-Rev9:2), [11](https://ref.ly/Rev9:11); [11:7](https://ref.ly/Rev11:7); [17:8](https://ref.ly/Rev17:8); [20:1](https://ref.ly/Rev20:1), [3](https://ref.ly/Rev20:3); compare [2 Peter 2:4](https://ref.ly/2Pet2:4)). The metaphor comes from the apparently bottomless depth of the ocean (the ancients had no means for sounding the ocean depths, so to them it was bottomless).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abyss (ID: `keyterm:Abyss`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 8:32 (id: 21077)

*a large herd of pigs*: Jews considered pigs to be ceremonially unclean animals. This indicates that the people in this area were Gentiles. The spiritually unclean demons were only suitable to be in pigs.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`)

## Luke 8:35 (id: 21078)

*sitting at Jesus’ feet*: The man sat in the position of a disciple, indicating his willingness to follow Jesus (see [8:37](https://ref.ly/Luke8:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`)

## Luke 8:37 (id: 21079)

*asked Jesus to depart from them*: The power of Jesus over spiritual forces scared the people. Losing their pigs made them angry. Their rejection contrasts with the faith of the healed man.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Gerasa (ID: `place:Gerasa`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Luke 8:39 (id: 21080)

*Return home*: The role of disciples is to proclaim to the world what Jesus has done for them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 8:40–56 (id: 21082)

These two accounts of healing show that Jesus has authority over disease and death. They also show the power of faith.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:40-56

## Luke 8:41 (id: 21083)

*synagogue* *leader*: The leader of the local synagogue was the officer who maintained the local synagogue and organized services (see also [8:49](https://ref.ly/Luke8:49); [13:14](https://ref.ly/Luke13:14); [Acts 13:15](https://ref.ly/Acts13:15); [18:8](https://ref.ly/Acts18:8), [17](https://ref.ly/Acts18:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jairus (ID: `person:Jairus`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 8:43 (id: 21084)

*bleeding*: The woman might have suffered from ongoing bleeding from her monthly cycle. The suffering was both physical and spiritual. According to the law of Moses, such bleeding made the woman ceremonially unclean. This would prevent her from participating in community worship ([Leviticus 15:25–30](https://ref.ly/Lev15:25-Lev15:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`)

## Luke 8:44 (id: 21085)

*the fringe of His cloak*: This likely refers to the little pieces of material (tassels) that pious Jews wore on the edges of their clothing (see [Numbers 15:38–39](https://ref.ly/Num15:38-Num15:39); [Deuteronomy 22:12](https://ref.ly/Deut22:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Tassel (ID: `realia:Tassel`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`)

## Luke 8:45 (id: 21086)

*Who touched Me?*: Jesus recognized the touch of faith by a needy person.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`)

## Luke 8:48 (id: 21087)

*your faith has healed you. Go in peace*: Jesus also said this to the woman who anointed his feet ([7:50](https://ref.ly/Luke7:50)). The Greek word translated here as *healed* can mean spiritual or physical salvation. *Peace* (compare to the Hebrew word *shalom*) means wholeness, not only the lack of conflict (see [1:79](https://ref.ly/Luke1:79); [2:14](https://ref.ly/Luke2:14), [29](https://ref.ly/Luke2:29); [7:50](https://ref.ly/Luke7:50); [19:38](https://ref.ly/Luke19:38), [42](https://ref.ly/Luke19:42); [24:36](https://ref.ly/Luke24:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Luke 8:51 (id: 21088)

Peter, John, and James formed a close group within the disciples of Jesus (see [5:1–11](https://ref.ly/Luke5:1-Luke5:11); [9:28](https://ref.ly/Luke9:28); [Mark 14:32–33](https://ref.ly/Mark14:32-Mark14:33)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John.2`); James (ID: `person:James`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 8:52 (id: 21089)

* *everyone was weeping and mourning*: See study note on 7:12.
* *she is not dead but asleep*: Jesus emphasized that the girl's condition was temporary. For Christians, death is like being asleep for a temporary period (compare [John 11:11](https://ref.ly/John11:11); [Acts 7:60](https://ref.ly/Acts7:60); [13:36](https://ref.ly/Acts13:36); [1 Corinthians 11:30](https://ref.ly/1Cor11:30); [15:6](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:6), [18](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:18), [20](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:20), [51](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:51); [1 Thessalonians 4:14–15](https://ref.ly/1Thess4:14-1Thess4:15); [5:10](https://ref.ly/1Thess5:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:52
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 7:12 (ID: `21031@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 8:55 (id: 21090)

*Her spirit returned*: Like the raising of the widow’s son ([7:15](https://ref.ly/Luke7:15)), the girl’s normal mortal life was restored. At the final resurrection, believers will receive glorified bodies that do not die (see study note on 24:39).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:55
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 24:39 (ID: `20911@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`)

## Luke 8:56 (id: 21091)

*not to tell anyone*: See “The Secret of the Messiah” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 8:56
* **Associated Articles:** The Secret of the Messiah (ID: `500351@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)


## Luke 9:1–6 (id: 21096)

Jesus now sent his 12 disciples to announce the kingdom of God and demonstrate its authority by casting out demons and healing the sick. Jesus was training them to carry on his work after he was gone.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Staff (ID: `realia:Staff`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 9:3 (id: 21121)

*Take nothing for the journey*: They were to depend on God and on the kindness of the people they served.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Staff (ID: `realia:Staff`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`)

## Luke 9:4 (id: 21129)

*Whatever house you enter, stay there*: They were to develop lasting relationships and be content without seeking better lodging or provisions.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 9:5 (id: 21138)

*shake the dust off your feet*: Using a symbol of rejection, they were to leave such a town to the judgment it deserved.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`)

## Luke 9:8 (id: 21140)

Jews expected the prophet Elijah would arrive before God's final judgment (see study note on 1:17). Some Jews expected a prophet like Moses would come ([Deuteronomy 18:15](https://ref.ly/Deut18:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:8
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 1:17 (ID: `20423@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`)

## Luke 9:9 (id: 21141)

*I beheaded John*: See [Mark 6:14–29](https://ref.ly/Mark6:14-Mark6:29).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); John (ID: `person:John`)

## Luke 9:10 (id: 21098)

* *He withdrew*: Jesus knew the disciples needed a time to focus on spiritual matters.
* Bethsaida was on the northern shore of the Sea of Galilee, east of the Jordan River. It was the hometown of Peter, Andrew, and Philip ([John 1:44](https://ref.ly/John1:44); [12:21](https://ref.ly/John12:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bethsaida (ID: `place:Bethsaida`)

## Luke 9:10–17 (id: 21097)

The feeding of the 5,000 recalls God’s miraculous feeding of Israel with manna in the wilderness ([Exodus 16](https://ref.ly/Exod16:1-Exod16:36); see also [2 Kings 4:42–44](https://ref.ly/2Kgs4:42-2Kgs4:44)). It points forward to God’s final salvation as a great feast for all nations ([Isaiah 25:6–8](https://ref.ly/Isa25:6-Isa25:8); [65:13–14](https://ref.ly/Isa65:13-Isa65:14); [Luke 14:15–24](https://ref.ly/Luke14:1-Luke14:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:10-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bethsaida (ID: `place:Bethsaida`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`); Basket (ID: `realia:Basket`)

## Luke 9:11 (id: 21099)

*He welcomed them*: Jesus wanted to go away in private with his disciples, but he had compassion on the crowds and ministered to them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 9:13 (id: 21100)

*You give them something to eat*: After their ministry of healing the sick and casting out demons, Jesus wanted the disciples to demonstrate faith in God’s ability to feed the crowds.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`)

## Luke 9:17 (id: 21101)

*twelve basketfuls of broken pieces that were left over*: God abundantly provides for his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Basket (ID: `realia:Basket`)

## Luke 9:18–27 (id: 21102)

Peter's confession that Jesus was the Christ (or Messiah) marked a turning point. Until then, Jesus had shown his authority as God's promised leader through miracles. Now he began to explain that the Messiah would suffer in an unexpected way. 

Jesus accepted this confession, but he made it clear that his role as Messiah would include suffering and death. He also called his disciples to follow him no matter the cost.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:18-27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); John (ID: `person:John`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); True (ID: `keyterm:True`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 9:19 (id: 21103)

*others say Elijah*:See study note on 9:8.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:19
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 9:8 (ID: `21140@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); John (ID: `person:John`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`)

## Luke 9:20 (id: 21104)

*The Christ*: This phrase could be translated "the LORD's anointed," a title for the king of Israel ([1 Samuel 24:6](https://ref.ly/1Sam24:6),[10](https://ref.ly/1Sam24:10); [2 Samuel 19:21](https://ref.ly/2Sam19:21); [Psalm 2:2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:2)). David was the LORD's anointed, and so was the Christ.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`)

## Luke 9:21–22 (id: 21105)

*not to tell this to anyone*: Jesus did not want to inspire the people to fight against Rome. His mission was to suffer, die, and rise from the dead.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:21-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`)

## Luke 9:22 (id: 21106)

* *must suffer many things*: This was the first prophecy of Jesus about his coming death in Jerusalem (see [9:44](https://ref.ly/Luke9:44); [17:25](https://ref.ly/Luke17:25); [18:31–34](https://ref.ly/Luke18:31-Luke18:34)). Jesus would fulfill the prophecies in [Isaiah 52:13–53:12](https://ref.ly/Isa52:13-Isa53:12). These prophecies describe the Lord's servant (the Messiah) who offers himself to save God's people. These events were part of God's purpose and plan.

    Despite opposition from human beings and from the spiritual forces of Satan, God works through the actions of human beings to accomplish his purposes. Though wicked men plotted against Jesus and put him to death, God accomplished salvation by raising Jesus from the dead ([Luke 24:7](https://ref.ly/Luke24:7), [26–27](https://ref.ly/Luke24:26-Luke24:27), [44–47](https://ref.ly/Luke24:44-Luke24:47); [Acts 2:23–24](https://ref.ly/Acts2:23-Acts2:24); [3:18](https://ref.ly/Acts3:18); [4:28](https://ref.ly/Acts4:28)).

* *chief priests*: Though Israel had only one high priest, the upper\-class priests were the wealthy ruling class. They served on the Sanhedrin (the main Jewish council) and held positions of power in Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`)

## Luke 9:23 (id: 21108)

*take up his cross daily*: This did not simply mean carrying a heavy burden. It meant dying a violent death on a cross. Believers must be fully willing to die to themselves and live for God, even if following him costs them their lives.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 9:23–27 (id: 21107)

*If anyone wants to come after me*: After predicting his own suffering and death, Jesus taught that all who follow him must also experience death to self.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:23-27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); True (ID: `keyterm:True`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 9:24 (id: 21109)

*whoever wants to save his life*: Trying to keep control of your own life instead of trusting God suggests that putting yourself in God’s place denies that Jesus is king. The result is that a person will lose true life, especially at the final judgment ([9:26](https://ref.ly/Luke9:26); [John 12:25](https://ref.ly/John12:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 9:26 (id: 21110)

*the Son of Man … when He comes in His glory*: See [Daniel 7:13–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:13-Dan7:14); “The Son of Man” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:26
* **Associated Articles:** The Son of Man (ID: `500342@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Luke 9:27 (id: 21111)

This difficult verse might refer to: 

1. the second coming of Christ;
2. the destruction of Jerusalem in AD 70;
3. the resurrection of Jesus; or
4. the transfiguration (when the appearance of Jesus changed and his glory was shown), which immediately follows in [9:28–36](https://ref.ly/Luke9:28-Luke9:36).

The last possibility is the most likely.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** True (ID: `keyterm:True`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 9:28 (id: 21113)

* *About eight days later*: Mark's Gospel says "after six days," referring to full days. Luke only includes parts of days.
* Peter, John, and James were the disciples who were closest to Jesus (see also [8:51](https://ref.ly/Luke8:51); [Mark 14:32–33](https://ref.ly/Mark14:32-Mark14:33)).
* *a mountain*: The traditional site of the transfiguration is Mount Tabor in southern Galilee. Some people think it was Mount Hermon, because it is closer to Caesarea Philippi, where Peter declared that Jesus is the Christ ([Luke 9:18–27](https://ref.ly/Luke9:18-Luke9:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); James (ID: `person:James`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`)

## Luke 9:28–36 (id: 21112)

At the transfiguration, Jesus revealed his true glory in the presence of three disciples. This confirmed Peter's belief that Jesus was the Christ. Moses and Elijah confirmed that Jesus fulfilled the Old Testament prophecies about the Messiah (the Christ, God's chosen king).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:28-36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); James (ID: `person:James`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)

## Luke 9:29 (id: 21114)

*His clothes became radiantly white* (literally *became bright as a flash of lightning*): The glory of Jesus shone through his human body (compare [Ezekiel 1:4](https://ref.ly/Ezek1:4); [Daniel 7:9](https://ref.ly/Dan7:9); see also [Exodus 34:29](https://ref.ly/Exod34:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)

## Luke 9:31 (id: 21115)

*His departure*: The Greek word *exodos* means "departure." In the Old Testament, the exodus from Egypt was God's great act of salvation. Similarly, in the New Testament, the departure of Jesus from this world was God's greatest act of salvation. This includes his death, resurrection, and ascension (going up into heaven). Jesus would accomplish a new and greater exodus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Luke 9:33 (id: 21116)

*Let us put up three shelters*: The Greek word translated *shelter* (*skēnē*) is the same word used in the Greek Old Testament for the *tabernacle*. This was Israel's tent for worship that they moved with them as they traveled in the wilderness. It also refers to the temporary *shelters* that Jews used during the Feast of Booths ([Leviticus 23:42](https://ref.ly/Lev23:42); [Nehemiah 8:14–17](https://ref.ly/Neh8:14-Neh8:17)). Peter might have wanted to extend the event by providing a place for Jesus, Moses, and Elijah to stay.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Luke 9:34 (id: 21117)

*and enveloped them*: In the Bible, clouds often cover or hide God’s presence ([Exodus 16:10](https://ref.ly/Exod16:10); [19:9](https://ref.ly/Exod19:9); [24:16](https://ref.ly/Exod24:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Luke 9:35 (id: 21118)

* The *voice* … *from the cloud*: This reminds audiences of God's voice at the baptism of Jesus ([3:22](https://ref.ly/Luke3:22)).
* *whom I have chosen*: This refers to [Isaiah 42:1](https://ref.ly/Isa42:1), showing Jesus is the Lord's suffering "Servant."
* *Listen to Him!*: This is from [Deuteronomy 18:15](https://ref.ly/Deut18:15), identifying Jesus as the prophet to come who would be like Moses (see [Acts 3:22](https://ref.ly/Acts3:22); [7:37](https://ref.ly/Acts7:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`)

## Luke 9:38 (id: 21119)

*my only child*: Losing an only son was especially disappointing because it meant no one could continue the family name (compare [7:12](https://ref.ly/Luke7:12); see [Genesis 22:16](https://ref.ly/Gen22:16); [1 Kings 17:17–24](https://ref.ly/1Kgs17:17-1Kgs17:24); [Jeremiah 6:26](https://ref.ly/Jer6:26); [Amos 8:10](https://ref.ly/Amos8:10); [Zechariah 12:10](https://ref.ly/Zech12:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:38


## Luke 9:39 (id: 21120)

*A spirit keeps seizing him*: Demons sometimes cause physical sickness, such as not being able to walk or not being able to speak ([13:11](https://ref.ly/Luke13:11); [11:14](https://ref.ly/Luke11:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:39


## Luke 9:41 (id: 21122)

*O unbelieving and perverse generation!*: It is unclear whether Jesus was speaking to the disciples or to the people as a whole.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`)

See study note on 9:22.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:44
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 9:22 (ID: `21106@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 9:45 (id: 21124)

*It was veiled from them*: Its meaning was hidden from them. The disciples were often slow to understand because they could not fully see the truth until after the resurrection (see study note on Mark 6:52).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:45
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 6:52 (ID: `21543@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 9:46 (id: 21126)

Jesus had just told his disciples that he would suffer and die. Yet they began to argue about which of them was the greatest. This showed surprising pride and a lack of concern for what Jesus had just said.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:46


## Luke 9:46–50 (id: 21125)

The disciples did not understand that Jesus would soon die, which was now seen in their pride and selfish attitude.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:46-50

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); John (ID: `person:John.2`)

## Luke 9:47 (id: 21127)

*Jesus, knowing the thoughts of their hearts*: See [5:22](https://ref.ly/Luke5:22); [7:39–40](https://ref.ly/Luke7:39-Luke7:40).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 9:48 (id: 21128)

* *Whoever welcomes this little child*: To welcome means to honor and treat as an equal. This statement is surprising because, in the time of Jesus, children had no social status in the community. People viewed children as the property of their parents. Jesus took the lowest and most vulnerable members of society and announced that welcoming them was the same as welcoming him.
* *in My name*: This means “as my representative.”
* *whoever is the least among all of you, he is the greatest*: True leadership in the kingdom of Jesus comes from serving others, not the exercise of power (see also [Mark 10:42–45](https://ref.ly/Mark10:42-Mark10:45)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 9:50 (id: 21130)

*for whoever is not against you is for you*: Although the man who was casting out demons was not one of the twelve disciples, Jesus said he should not be stopped because he was also proclaiming the message of the kingdom. This statement was a proverb (a wise saying), not an absolute rule, since Jesus made the opposite statement in [11:23](https://ref.ly/Luke11:23). Wisdom is needed to apply a proverb correctly in each situation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:50

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 9:51 (id: 21132)

*Jesus resolutely set out for Jerusalem*: Even though the route was not direct, Jerusalem was his final destination.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Luke 9:51–19.44 (id: 21131)

The journey to Jerusalem marked a new stage in the mission of Jesus as the Messiah, which would include suffering. During this time, he prepared his disciples for what was ahead, and the Jewish leaders opposed him more and more.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:51

## Luke 9:52–53 (id: 21133)

*a village of the Samaritans*: Jews and Samaritans had a long history of conflict and ethnic prejudice. They hated each other (see study note on 10:33). The Samaritans worshiped on Mount Gerizim and probably assumed that Jesus was traveling to Jerusalem to worship there.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:52-53
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 10:33 (ID: `20475@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Samaritans (ID: `group:Samaritan`); Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Luke 9:54 (id: 21134)

*call down fire from heaven to consume them*: Some ancient Greek versions add *as Elijah did.* The prophet Elijah called down fire from heaven three times against God's enemies ([1 Kings 18:38](https://ref.ly/1Kgs18:38); [2 Kings 1:1–17](https://ref.ly/2Kgs1:1-2Kgs1:17)). James and John, in their enthusiasm, wanted to do the same.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:54

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); James (ID: `person:James`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 9:57–62 (id: 21135)

As he traveled toward Jerusalem to suffer and die, Jesus explained the cost to those who wished to follow him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:57-62

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Religious Official (ID: `person:ReligiousOfficial`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); A Man (ID: `person:GenericMale`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Mattock (ID: `realia:Mattock`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`)

## Luke 9:58 (id: 21136)

*no place to lay His head*: Any disciple of Jesus must be ready to go anywhere and to give up home and security.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:58

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`)

## Luke 9:59 (id: 21137)

*let me go and bury my father*: Respect for parents was a very important value in first\-century Israel. Among other things, this meant providing them with an honorable burial. Jesus called for a commitment that took priority over all human relationships.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:59


## Luke 9:61–62 (id: 21139)

* *Let me bid farewell to my family*: This statement is similar to the prophet Elisha's request to Elijah ([1 Kings 19:19–20](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:19-1Kgs19:20)). Jesus asked for an even greater commitment from his followers.
* *puts his hand to the plow and then looks back*: Farmers in the ancient world guided a light plow, a tool used to prepare soil for planting, with one hand and the oxen (work animals used for farming) with the other. Looking away would cause the plow to turn away.

    For a believer, looking back meant placing earthly concerns ahead of God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 9:61-62

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); A Man (ID: `person:GenericMale`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Mattock (ID: `realia:Mattock`)

## Luke 10:1–20 (id: 20455)

*the Lord appointed seventy\-two others*: The mission of the twelve disciples represented the ministry of Jesus to Israel ([9:1–6](https://ref.ly/Luke9:1-Luke9:6)). This mission represented his outreach to the Gentiles. The number 72 represents the nations of the world. [Genesis 10](https://ref.ly/Gen10:1-Gen10:32) lists 70 nations in the Hebrew text, but the Greek Old Testament (the Septuagint) lists 72\. 

Luke, who used the Septuagint, likely wrote 72, and then a later scribe changed it to match the Hebrew text. The main idea is that the good news about Jesus and the kingdom of God is for both Jews and Gentiles.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:1-20
