## Luke 10:2 (id: 20470)

The *harvest* refers to people who need to hear the good news about Jesus and be gathered to God's presence (compare [Isaiah 27:12](https://ref.ly/Isa27:12)). There was urgency to the task, just as crops have to be brought in at the exact time when they are ripe. More workers were needed.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 10:3 (id: 20479)

*lambs among wolves*: are not to dominate others, but to sacrifice themselves for the benefit of others. This makes them vulnerable (see [Jeremiah 5:6](https://ref.ly/Jer5:6); [Ezekiel 22:27](https://ref.ly/Ezek22:27); [Habakkuk 1:8](https://ref.ly/Hab1:8); [Zephaniah 3:3](https://ref.ly/Zeph3:3)). But the good shepherd protects them from the *wolves* ([Psalm 23:1](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Wolf (ID: `fauna:Wolf`)

## Luke 10:4 (id: 20482)

* *Carry no purse*: Compare [9:3](https://ref.ly/Luke9:3). The disciples of Jesus were to depend on God and on the help of those whom they served.
* *Do not greet anyone along the road*: This did not mean being unfriendly, but remaining focused on the task Jesus had given them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Sandal (ID: `realia:Sandal`)

## Luke 10:5 (id: 20483)

God's peace means spiritual blessings and becoming whole. This is known as the shalom of God (see study note on 8:48).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:5
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 8:48 (ID: `21087@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 10:7 (id: 20484)

* *Stay at the same house*: See study note on 9:4.
* *For the worker is worthy of his wages*: Those who minister for Jesus should receive compensation from those they serve (see also [1 Corinthians 9:14](https://ref.ly/1Cor9:14); [1 Timothy 5:18](https://ref.ly/1Tim5:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:7
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 9:4 (ID: `21129@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 10:9 (id: 20485)

* Jesus gave the same mission to the twelve disciples (see [9:1](https://ref.ly/Luke9:1)).
* *Heal the sick*: Healing was a sign of salvation ([Isaiah 29:18–19](https://ref.ly/Isa29:18-Isa29:19); [35:5](https://ref.ly/Isa35:5)). It was physical evidence that the kingdom of God was present (see study note on Luke 7:22).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

* Chorazin and Bethsaida were towns where Jesus taught. They were on the northern shore of the Sea of Galilee.
* Tyre and Sidon were Gentile cities in Phoenicia, on the Mediterranean coast north of Galilee. The prophets had delivered messages of judgment against their evil behavior ([Isaiah 23](https://ref.ly/Isa23:1-Isa23:18); [Ezekiel 28](https://ref.ly/Ezek28:1-Ezek28:26)).

* Capernaum was where Jesus worked during his ministry in Galilee. This town witnessed many of his miracles (see [4:31–41](https://ref.ly/Luke4:31-Luke4:41)).
* *to Hades*: Though Capernaum was blessed by the Christ's presence, it would be judged for its pride and rejection of Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 10:19 (id: 20461)

Snakes and scorpions were symbols of danger and evil (see [Num 21:6–9](https://ref.ly/Num21:6-Num21:9); [Deut 8:15](https://ref.ly/Deut8:15); [1 Kgs 12:14](https://ref.ly/1Kgs12:14); [Isa 11:8](https://ref.ly/Isa11:8); [Ezek 2:6](https://ref.ly/Ezek2:6)). Satan is the ancient serpent who tempted Eve ([Gen 3:13–14](https://ref.ly/Gen3:13-Gen3:14); [Rom 16:20](https://ref.ly/Rom16:20); [Rev 12:9](https://ref.ly/Rev12:9); [20:2](https://ref.ly/Rev20:2)), and demonic forces are described as scorpions ([Rev 9:3–4](https://ref.ly/Rev9:3-Rev9:4)). • Nothing will injure you: This didn’t mean that Jesus’ followers should take dangerous chances. The point is that God protects his servants (e.g., [Acts 28:3–6](https://ref.ly/Acts28:3-Acts28:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Scorpion (ID: `fauna:Scorpion`); To Act Wickedly (ID: `keyterm:ActWickedly`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`)

* Disciples should not rejoice in their spiritual power, but in the greatness of God who freely gives them salvation.
* *your names are written in heaven*: For the record of salvation in heaven's books, see [Exodus 32:32–33](https://ref.ly/Exod32:32-Exod32:33); [Psalm 69:28](https://ref.ly/Ps69:28); [Isaiah 4:3](https://ref.ly/Isa4:3); [Daniel 12:1](https://ref.ly/Dan12:1); [Malachi 3:16–17](https://ref.ly/Mal3:16-Mal3:17); [Philippians 4:3](https://ref.ly/Phil4:3); [Hebrews 12:23](https://ref.ly/Heb12:23); [Revelation 3:5](https://ref.ly/Rev3:5); [13:8](https://ref.ly/Rev13:8); [17:8](https://ref.ly/Rev17:8); [20:12](https://ref.ly/Rev20:12), [15](https://ref.ly/Rev20:15); [21:27](https://ref.ly/Rev21:27).

## Luke 10:21 (id: 20463)

*You have hidden these things*: God uses simple things that a child can understand to shame those who think themselves wise but are foolish in God’s eyes (see [Isaiah 29:14](https://ref.ly/Isa29:14); [1 Corinthians 1:18–31](https://ref.ly/1Cor1:18-1Cor1:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:21

The Father and the Son share an intimate relationship. Jesus came to earth to reveal the Father to a lost world (see [John 1:1–18](https://ref.ly/John1:1-John1:18)).

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

*Love the Lord your God*: Jesus identified [Deuteronomy 6:5](https://ref.ly/Deut6:5) and [Leviticus 19:18](https://ref.ly/Lev19:18) as the two most important commandments ([Mark 12:28–34](https://ref.ly/Mark12:28-Mark12:34)). [Deuteronomy 6:5](https://ref.ly/Deut6:5) is part of the prayer called the *Shema*,which every Jew was to repeat daily.

*Who is my neighbor?*: A neighbor, in this context, was normally identified as a fellow Israelite.

The road from Jerusalem to Jericho declined 1,000 meters (3,300 feet) through rugged terrain where robbers often waited for travelers.

## Luke 10:30–37 (id: 20471)

This parable shows that true neighbors love even their enemies ([10:29](https://ref.ly/Luke10:29)). True faith is not based on a person’s ethnic background or religious group, but on loving God and loving others.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:30-37

## Luke 10:31 (id: 20473)

* *a priest*: Priests were descendants of Aaron from the tribe of Levi ([Exodus 28:1–3](https://ref.ly/Exod28:1-Exod28:3)). They served in the temple and offered sacrifices to God.
* *passed by*: If Aa priest or a Levite touched a dead body, they would become ceremonially unclean and could not serve in the temple. This priest was unwilling to risk ritual impurity by helping a person in serious trouble.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:31


## Luke 10:32 (id: 20474)

*a Levite*: Levites were members of the tribe of Levi. They were not given a tribal homeland in Israel but were dedicated to God ([Numbers 3:41](https://ref.ly/Num3:41), [45](https://ref.ly/Num3:45); [8:18](https://ref.ly/Num8:18); [35:2–3](https://ref.ly/Num35:2-Num35:3); [Deuteronomy 18:1](https://ref.ly/Deut18:1); [Joshua 14:3](https://ref.ly/Josh14:3)). They assisted priests in temple service.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`)

## Luke 10:33 (id: 20475)

*a Samaritan*: This unexpected change in the story probably surprised the listeners. Jews and Samaritans hated each other (see [9:52–53](https://ref.ly/Luke9:52-Luke9:53); [17:16](https://ref.ly/Luke17:16); [John 4:4–42](https://ref.ly/John4:4-John4:42)). After Assyria conquered the northern kingdom of Israel, Assyrian settlers married Israelites who remained in Samaria. Their descendants became known as Samaritans.

Later, after the Judeans returned from Babylon, conflict grew between Jews and Samaritans ([Ezra 4:1–24](https://ref.ly/Ezra4:1-Ezra4:24)). The Samaritans built their own temple on Mount Gerizim. They also used their own version of the Pentateuch, the first five books of the Bible, as Scripture.

This hatred grew worse when the Jewish king John Hyrcanus attacked the Samaritans and destroyed their temple on Mount Gerizim. This happened around 128 BC. Because of this long hatred, no first\-century Jew would expect a despised Samaritan to help a wounded Jew. But in God’s kingdom, a despised foreigner becomes a true neighbor who helps!

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`); Samaritans (ID: `group:Samaritan`); Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`)

## Luke 10:34 (id: 20476)

*Oil* calmed the wound, and *wine* helped keep away infection.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Inn (ID: `realia:Inn`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

*Mary … sat at the Lord's feet*: She took the position of a disciple (compare [Acts 22:3](https://ref.ly/Acts22:3)). Rabbis (Jewish religious teachers with authority) usually did not have female disciples.

## Luke 10:40 (id: 20480)

*Martha was distracted by all the preparations to be made*: Hospitality was highly valued, and Martha was doing what people expected a woman to do in that culture. She was upset that Mary was not helping.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:40

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Martha (ID: `person:Martha`)

## Luke 10:42 (id: 20481)

*only one thing is necessary*: Mary has discovered it. The words of Jesus were surprising. Women were expected to serve in the home, and they were not allowed to learn from a rabbi as disciples. Jesus affirmed Mary’s desire to be his disciple.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 10:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mary (ID: `person:Mary.3`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`)

## Luke 11:4 (id: 20515)

*And lead us not into temptation*: This phrase is difficult in Greek. God does not tempt his people ([James 1:13](https://ref.ly/Jas1:13)). But testing is part of Christian growth ([James 1:2–4](https://ref.ly/Jas1:2-Jas1:4)), so this raises a question: Should believers pray to avoid testing? 

The phrase probably means “protect us when we face temptation.”

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:4

## Luke 11:6 (id: 20518)

*a friend of mine has come to me on a journey*: Standards of hospitality in first\-century Jewish culture required a host to find adequate provisions for a visitor.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:6


## Luke 11:8 (id: 20519)

*because of the man’s persistence* (or *in order to avoid shame,* or *so his reputation will not be damaged*): The man who refused to get up would also be shamed, because hospitality was a duty in that community. In the same way, God protects his good name by caring for his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:8


## Luke 11:11–12 (id: 20486)

*a fish … an egg*:Thesewere common foods. A scorpion can look like an egg when it curls into a ball. A snake or scorpion in place of food would be a cruel trick, giving something dangerous instead of a good meal. No loving father would do that.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`); Scorpion (ID: `fauna:Scorpion`)

## Luke 11:13 (id: 20487)

*give the Holy Spirit*: The parallel passage in the Gospel of Matthew says that the Father will “give good things” ([Matthew 7:11](https://ref.ly/Matt7:11)). Luke highlights the greatest of these gifts: the Holy Spirit. The Holy Spirit guides believers, gives them power to serve God, and marks them as belonging to God in salvation. Luke especially emphasizes the work of the Holy Spirit throughout Luke and Acts.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:13

## Luke 11:14–32 (id: 20488)

Jesus faced two challenges. He answered the first, an accusation that he was driving out demons by Satan's power ([11:](https://ref.ly/Luke11:17-Luke11:26)[15](https://ref.ly/Luke11:15)), in[11:17–26](https://ref.ly/Luke11:17-Luke11:26). The second was a demand for "a sign from heaven" ([11:16](https://ref.ly/Luke11:16)). He answered this in [11:29–32](https://ref.ly/Luke11:29-Luke11:32).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:14-32

## Luke 11:29–30 (id: 20499)

*The sign of Jonah* might refer to the resurrection of Jesus. Jonah's time in the fish was a sign to the Ninevites that God had sent him, similar to the time Jesus spent in the grave ("in the heart of the earth"; [Matthew 12:40](https://ref.ly/Matt12:40)). It could also mean that both Jonah and Jesus preached God's warning to repent (see [Luke 11:32](https://ref.ly/Luke11:32)).

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Jonah (ID: `person:Jonah.2`); Ninevites (ID: `group:Nineveh`); Nineveh (ID: `place:Nineveh`)

## Luke 11:31 (id: 20500)

*South*: The Old Testament identifies this place as Sheba, a kingdom in southern Arabia. The queen traveled a long distance to hear King Solomon's wisdom ([1 Kings 10:1–13](https://ref.ly/1Kgs10:1-1Kgs10:13); [2 Chronicles 9:1–12](https://ref.ly/2Chr9:1-2Chr9:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sheba (ID: `place:Sheba.2`); Court Case (ID: `keyterm:CourtCase`); Queen of Sheba (ID: `person:QueenOfSheba`); Judgment (ID: `keyterm:Judgment`); Solomon (ID: `person:Solomon`)

## Luke 11:33 (id: 20501)

*No one lights a lamp and puts it in a cellar or under a basket*: See study note on 8:16–17.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:33
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 8:16-17 (ID: `21061@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lampstand (ID: `realia:Lampstand.2`)

## Luke 11:35–36 (id: 20502)

Light and darkness are metaphors for good and evil ([John 1:5](https://ref.ly/John1:5); [3:19](https://ref.ly/John3:19); [8:12](https://ref.ly/John8:12); [12:35](https://ref.ly/John12:35); [Acts 26:18](https://ref.ly/Acts26:18); [Romans 13:12](https://ref.ly/Rom13:12); [2 Corinthians 4:6](https://ref.ly/2Cor4:6); [6:14](https://ref.ly/2Cor6:14); [Ephesians 5:8](https://ref.ly/Eph5:8); [1 Thessalonians 5:5](https://ref.ly/1Thess5:5); [1 Peter 2:9](https://ref.ly/1Pet2:9); [1 John 1:5](https://ref.ly/1John1:5); [2:8–9](https://ref.ly/1John2:8-1John2:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:35-36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`)

## Luke 11:37 (id: 20503)

*a Pharisee invited Him to dine with him*: See study note on 7:36.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:37
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 7:36 (ID: `21044@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 11:38 (id: 20504)

*first wash before the meal*: The Pharisees had developed elaborate washing rituals to ensure ceremonial purity (see [Mark 7:1–5](https://ref.ly/Mark7:1-Mark7:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`)

## Luke 11:39 (id: 20505)

The Pharisees had very detailed rules for washing cups and dishes in order to be religiously clean. These rules were about ceremonial cleansing (a special washing required by religious law).

Jesus said they cleaned only the outside of the cup. By this, he meant they carefully followed outward rules. But inside, they were still unclean. They kept sin and wrong desires in their inner lives (their thoughts and motives).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plate (ID: `realia:Plate.2`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`)

## Luke 11:41 (id: 20506)

*give as alms*:Giving to those in need is an outward action. Such actions reveal internal righteousness (a right relationship with God).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Clean (ID: `keyterm:Clean`)

## Luke 11:42 (id: 20508)

*pay tithes of mint, rue, and every herb*: The law required tithing (giving one tenth to God; see [Leviticus 27:30–33](https://ref.ly/Lev27:30-Lev27:33); [Numbers 18:21–32](https://ref.ly/Num18:21-Num18:32); [Deuteronomy 14:22–29](https://ref.ly/Deut14:22-Deut14:29); [2 Chronicles 31:5–12](https://ref.ly/2Chr31:5-2Chr31:12)). The Pharisees were careful to tithe everything correctly. But they ignored what was more important: justice and the love of God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mint (ID: `flora:Mint`); Rue (ID: `flora:Rue`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Judgment (ID: `keyterm:Judgment`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Luke 11:42–52 (id: 20507)

*Woe to you Pharisees!* Woes are the opposite of blessings (see study note on 6:24–26). Jesus pronounced six woes. Three were against the Pharisees ([11:42](https://ref.ly/Luke11:42), [43](https://ref.ly/Luke11:43), [44](https://ref.ly/Luke11:44)) and three were against the teachers of religious law ([11:46](https://ref.ly/Luke11:46), [47](https://ref.ly/Luke11:47), [52](https://ref.ly/Luke11:52)). 

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:42-52
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 6:24-26 (ID: `21012@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Zechariah (ID: `person:Zechariah.12`); A Scribe (ID: `person:Scribe`); Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`); Abel (ID: `person:Abel`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Judgment (ID: `keyterm:Judgment`); Mint (ID: `flora:Mint`); Rue (ID: `flora:Rue`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 11:43 (id: 20509)

* The elders of the synagogues had special seats of honor.
* The respectful greetings were honorable ways of addressing social and religious superiors. For example, people might use a special title when speaking to a leader, teacher, or judge to show respect.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 11:44 (id: 20510)

*unmarked graves*: Touching a grave or a dead body made a Jew ceremonially unclean. Jesus accused the religious leaders of being a defiling influence rather than a purifying one. This was a strongly offensive accusation ([11:45](https://ref.ly/Luke11:45)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 11:45 (id: 20511)

*experts in the law*: The experts in religious law were closely aligned with the Pharisees and shared the same zeal for keeping the law of Moses.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** A Scribe (ID: `person:Scribe`)

## Luke 11:46 (id: 20512)

*heavy burdens*: This refers to the oral tradition with all its intricate details on living according to *torah* (God’s instruction in the books of Moses). The oral tradition placed a great burden upon the Jewish people.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`)

## Luke 11:48 (id: 20513)

*They killed the prophets*: See [1 Kings 19:10](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:10), [14](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:14); [Nehemiah 9:26](https://ref.ly/Neh9:26); [Jeremiah 2:30](https://ref.ly/Jer2:30); [26:20–24](https://ref.ly/Jer26:20-Jer26:24). Luke presents Jesus as the suffering prophet ([Luke 4:22–24](https://ref.ly/Luke4:22-Luke4:24)). The suffering prophet is a prophet sent by God who is rejected and suffers. Luke shows that Jesus fits this pattern.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`)

## Luke 11:49 (id: 20514)

The quotation is not explicitly from the Old Testament.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 11:51 (id: 20516)

*from the blood of Abel to the blood of Zechariah*: Abel's murder by his brother Cain was the first murder recorded in the Old Testament ([Genesis 4:8](https://ref.ly/Gen4:8)). The stoning of Zechariah, son of Jehoiada ([2 Chronicles 24:20–22](https://ref.ly/2Chr24:20-2Chr24:22)), was the last, since Chronicles is the last book in the Hebrew Bible.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zechariah (ID: `person:Zechariah.12`); Abel (ID: `person:Abel`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 11:52 (id: 20517)

*the key to knowledge*: See [Matthew 16:19](https://ref.ly/Matt16:19); [23:13](https://ref.ly/Matt23:13).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 11:52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`)

## Luke 12:1 (id: 20527)

Yeast spreads through dough until it is entirely leavened. Leaven became a symbol of sin's spreading power ([Exodus 12:14–20](https://ref.ly/Exod12:14-Exod12:20); [1 Corinthians 5:6](https://ref.ly/1Cor5:6)). Like yeast, the hypocrisy of the Pharisees would spread until it affected all the people.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hypocrisy (ID: `keyterm:Hypocrisy`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`)

## Luke 12:2–3 (id: 20528)

These verses warn against hypocrisy (pretending to be good or righteous). All thoughts will be revealed by God, who knows all things and will judge every human being ([Job 10:4–7](https://ref.ly/Job10:4-Job10:7); [11:11](https://ref.ly/Job11:11); [Psalms 11:4](https://ref.ly/Ps11:4); [33:15](https://ref.ly/Ps33:15); [139:12](https://ref.ly/Ps139:12); [Proverbs 15:3](https://ref.ly/Prov15:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Inner Room (ID: `realia:InnerRoom`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`)

## Luke 12:5 (id: 20553)

*hell* (Greek *Gehenna*): Gehenna was the Valley of Hinnom, a ravine on the southwest side of Jerusalem. It became a picture of hell because some Israelites had sacrificed their children there to false gods ([Jeremiah 32:35](https://ref.ly/Jer32:35)). 

Jesus taught his disciples to never be afraid of enemies who can only take away their life on earth. Instead, they should fear God*,* who alone has authority over final judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gehenna (ID: `keyterm:Gehenna`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Luke 12:6 (id: 20554)

Each of the *two pennies* was worth about half an hour of work for a person who earned a living by working day jobs.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Assarion (ID: `realia:Assarion`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sparrow (ID: `fauna:Sparrow`)

## Luke 12:8 (id: 20555)

*will also confess him before the angels of God*: At the final judgment (see [Daniel 7:7–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:7-Dan7:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Luke 12:10 (id: 20520)

The Holy Spirit guides people to God. A person who speaks against (*blasphemes*) the Holy Spirit and rejects the Spirit’s witness about Jesus also rejects God. This is the unforgivable sin (see study notes on Matthew 12:31–32).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:10
* **Associated Articles:** Matthew 12:31 (ID: `21719@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Blaspheme (ID: `keyterm:Blaspheme`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Luke 12:11–12 (id: 20521)

*brought before synagogues*: Jesus predicted persecution from Jews in the synagogues and from Gentile rulers and authorities. Paul and other apostles faced both kinds of persecution throughout Acts.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Bring In (ID: `keyterm:BringIn`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 12:13 (id: 20523)

*tell my brother to divide the inheritance with me*: In Judaism, the oldest son received twice as much of the family estate ([Deuteronomy 21:17](https://ref.ly/Deut21:17)). He was responsible for dividing up the rest after his father’s death. This younger brother wanted his share of the estate (compare [Luke 15:11–32](https://ref.ly/Luke15:11-Luke15:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); A Person (ID: `person:GenericPerson`)

## Luke 12:13–21 (id: 20522)

This parable shows the danger of trusting in riches instead of in God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:13-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); A Person (ID: `person:GenericPerson`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Barn (ID: `realia:Barn`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`)

## Luke 12:15 (id: 20524)

*Guard yourselves against every form of greed*: See [Exodus 20:17](https://ref.ly/Exod20:17); [Deuteronomy 5:21](https://ref.ly/Deut5:21); [Job 31:24–25](https://ref.ly/Job31:24-Job31:25); [Psalm 49](https://ref.ly/Ps49:1-Ps49:20); [Ecclesiastes 2:1–11](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:1-Eccl2:11).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 12:16–20 (id: 20525)

On the surface, the rich man’s plan seemed wise. But he thought only about himself (compare [Ecclesiastes 5:10](https://ref.ly/Eccl5:10)). He did not think about giving any of his crops to people in need. He assumed that his wealth would last. He only wanted a place to store it and did not include God in his plans (see [James 4:13–17](https://ref.ly/Jas4:13-Jas4:17)). So God’s judgment against him was just.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:16-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Barn (ID: `realia:Barn`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`)

## Luke 12:19–20 (id: 20526)

* *myself* (Literally *soul*)*:* The man was speaking to himself.
* *You fool!* In Scripture, a *fool* is not someone with low intelligence, but someone who dishonors and disobeys God ([Psalm 14:1–5](https://ref.ly/Ps14:1-Ps14:5)).
* *your life will be required of you*: The man believed his soul was secure, but God was not pleased with him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 12:22–34 (id: 20529)

After Jesus warns against greed (not being content), he teaches about living in dependence on God ([12:13–21](https://ref.ly/Luke12:13-Luke12:21)). The disciples of Jesus were to focus on God’s kingdom and his purpose in this world rather than being obsessed with possessions.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:22-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Solomon (ID: `person:Solomon`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Having Little Faith (ID: `keyterm:HavingLittleFaith`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Oven (ID: `realia:Oven`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Wild Flower (ID: `flora:WildFlower`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Crow (ID: `fauna:Crow`); Cubit (ID: `realia:Cubit.2`); Storeroom (ID: `realia:Storeroom`); Rooster (ID: `fauna:Rooster`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`); Barn (ID: `realia:Barn`); Moth (ID: `fauna:Moth`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Luke 12:24 (id: 20530)

*Consider the ravens*: Ravens were ceremonially unclean ([Leviticus 11:15](https://ref.ly/Lev11:15); [Deuteronomy 14:14](https://ref.ly/Deut14:14)). But God cared even for them ([Psalm 147:9](https://ref.ly/Ps147:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Storeroom (ID: `realia:Storeroom`); Crow (ID: `fauna:Crow`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`); Barn (ID: `realia:Barn`); Rooster (ID: `fauna:Rooster`)

## Luke 12:25 (id: 20531)

*add a single hour to his life*(literally *add a single cubit* *to his length*): Worry cannot make life any longer.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cubit (ID: `realia:Cubit.2`)

## Luke 12:27 (id: 20532)

King Solomon was one of the wealthiest kings in the world during his time (see [2 Chronicles 9:13–22](https://ref.ly/2Chr9:13-2Chr9:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Wild Flower (ID: `flora:WildFlower`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Solomon (ID: `person:Solomon`)

## Luke 12:30 (id: 20533)

*the Gentiles*: The Greek word *ethnē* can mean “the nations” or “the Gentiles.” To a Jewish audience, it meant all who were not God’s people.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 12:31 (id: 20534)

*seek His kingdom*: Live God’s way, follow his purpose, and obey him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 12:32 (id: 20535)

*little flock*: God is the shepherd of his people ([Psalm 23](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1-Ps23:6); [Jeremiah 13:17](https://ref.ly/Jer13:17); [Ezekiel 34](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:1-Ezek34:31); [Zechariah 10:3](https://ref.ly/Zech10:3)). Israel’s leaders were also known as shepherds. The prophets criticized them for harming or neglecting their people ("flock"; [Ezekiel 34](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:1-Ezek34:31); [Zechariah 10:3](https://ref.ly/Zech10:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Luke 12:33 (id: 20536)

* *Sell your possessions and give to the poor*: This is not a command to sell everything a person owns, because other parts of the Bible assume that believers may own property. Instead, it means recognizing that everything we have belongs to God and should be used to serve him and his people (see [Acts 2:44–45](https://ref.ly/Acts2:44-Acts2:45); [4:32–34](https://ref.ly/Acts4:32-Acts4:34)). Believers are responsible for helping the poor, especially other believers ([Galatians 6:10](https://ref.ly/Gal6:10)).
* *treasure in heaven*: See [Luke 16:1–13](https://ref.ly/Luke16:1-Luke16:13) and [Matthew 6:19–21](https://ref.ly/Matt6:19-Matt6:21). When God gives wealth, he also gives an opportunity to help people in need.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Moth (ID: `fauna:Moth`)

## Luke 12:35 (id: 20538)

*keep your lamps burning*: This is a picture of being ready and staying watchful ([Exodus 27:20–21](https://ref.ly/Exod27:20-Exod27:21); [Leviticus 24:2](https://ref.ly/Lev24:2); [Psalm 18:28](https://ref.ly/Ps18:28); [Matthew 25:1–13](https://ref.ly/Matt25:1-Matt25:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:35


## Luke 12:35–48 (id: 20537)

Jesus compared his faithful followers to servants in a master's house. These servants were always ready for his return and did a good job managing the resources he left with them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:35-48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); True (ID: `keyterm:True`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`); Faithfulness (ID: `keyterm:Faithfulness`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 12:36 (id: 20539)

The master could return from the *wedding* *banquet* at any time of day or night. The followers of Jesus should always remain faithful and prepared for his return.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:36


## Luke 12:37 (id: 20540)

* *he himself will come and wait on them*: In Middle Eastern culture, the master would never serve his servants. Jesus changed the master's role by serving his disciples, offering himself for them, and providing for all their needs (see [22:24–27](https://ref.ly/Luke22:24-Luke22:27)).
* *he will dress himself to serve*:In this culture, servants often tied up or adjusted their long robes before working so they could move freely. The picture here is surprising because the master takes the place of a servant and personally cares for his guests. This shows the generosity and humility of the master.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`)

## Luke 12:40 (id: 20541)

*the Son of Man will come*: See “The Son of Man” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:40
* **Associated Articles:** The Son of Man (ID: `500342@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)


## Luke 12:42 (id: 20542)

The faithful, sensible servant was a household manager. Wealthy homeowners appointed gifted and educated slaves as chief executive officers over their affairs.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Faithfulness (ID: `keyterm:Faithfulness`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 12:44 (id: 20543)

*in charge of all his possessions*: In the Roman world, many servants were slaves and were legally considered the property of their masters. Even so, some slaves were given great responsibility. They could manage the household, oversee property, and act with the authority of the master.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** True (ID: `keyterm:True`)

## Luke 12:46 (id: 20544)

* *He will cut him to pieces*: Harsh physical punishment like this was known in the ancient world. It is not clear whether Jesus was describing punishment in this life or final judgment.
* *assign him a place with the unbelievers*: This could mean treating him like an unbeliever in this life, or condemning him with unbelievers in the final judgment. The meaning depends on whether Jesus was speaking about discipline for an unfaithful believer or condemnation of an unbeliever. In either case, the warning is clear: an unfaithful servant will face severe judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`)

## Luke 12:47–48 (id: 20545)

* *Who knows ...* *who unknowingly*: Sins committed in ignorance receive less punishment than deliberate sins ([Numbers 15:22–26](https://ref.ly/Num15:22-Num15:26); [Psalm 19:13](https://ref.ly/Ps19:13); compare [Luke 23:34](https://ref.ly/Luke23:34)).
* *From everyone who has been given much*: Believers will be held responsible for the knowledge and resources they have been given ([James 3:1](https://ref.ly/Jas3:1)). A sin in ignorance is still a sin.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:47-48


## Luke 12:49 (id: 20546)

*I have come to ignite a fire*: The ministry of Jesus brought great change. It brought judgment on the wicked and purification for the righteous (see [3:16–17](https://ref.ly/Luke3:16-Luke3:17); [9:54](https://ref.ly/Luke9:54); [17:29](https://ref.ly/Luke17:29); [Deuteronomy 4:24](https://ref.ly/Deut4:24); [Amos 5:6](https://ref.ly/Amos5:6); [Hebrews 12:29](https://ref.ly/Heb12:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:49


## Luke 12:50 (id: 20547)

*a baptism to undergo*: The Scriptures often describe God's judgment as an overwhelming flood ([Psalms 18:4](https://ref.ly/Ps18:4); [42:7](https://ref.ly/Ps42:7); [69:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps69:1-Ps69:2); [Isaiah 8:7–8](https://ref.ly/Isa8:7-Isa8:8); [30:27–28](https://ref.ly/Isa30:27-Isa30:28); [Jonah 2:5](https://ref.ly/Jonah2:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:50

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`)

## Luke 12:51–53 (id: 20548)

*No, I tell you, but division*: The ministry of Jesus demands a decision for or against God that divides even family members.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:51-53

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`)

## Luke 12:53 (id: 20549)

Jesus quotes [Micah 7:6](https://ref.ly/Mic7:6), which was interpreted by the rabbis as the crisis and persecution that would lead to the Messiah’s coming.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:53


## Luke 12:54 (id: 20550)

Clouds beginning to form in the west brought rain from the Mediterranean Sea.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:54


## Luke 12:55 (id: 20551)

The hot south wind blows into Israel from the Arabian Desert to the south and east ([Job 37:17](https://ref.ly/Job37:17); [Jeremiah 4:11](https://ref.ly/Jer4:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:55


## Luke 12:58 (id: 20552)

*on your way to the magistrate*: This case involved an unpaid debt and could lead to prison for the debtor. The officer was either a court official or a prison guard. It was wise to settle matters with your accuser before reaching the court. In the same way, people should make things right with God before the final judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 12:58

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Set Free (ID: `keyterm:SetFree`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Luke 13:1 (id: 20564)

*the Galileans whose blood Pilate had mixed with their sacrifices*: This specific event is not mentioned in other known sources. But Pilate was a harsh governor who violently put down unrest on several occasions (see Josephus, *War* 2\.9\.2–4; *Antiquities* 18\.3\.1–2\).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Galileans (ID: `group:Galilee`); Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`)

## Luke 13:1–4 (id: 20556)

Jesus was responding to a popular claim that bad things only happen to bad people. Sin has negative consequences, but not every bad thing is a result of sin. Jesus then clarified that all people are sinners who need to repent ([13:3](https://ref.ly/Luke13:3), [5](https://ref.ly/Luke13:5); see [Job 4:7](https://ref.ly/Job4:7); [8:4](https://ref.ly/Job8:4), [20](https://ref.ly/Job8:20); [22:5](https://ref.ly/Job22:5); see also [Psalms 34:21](https://ref.ly/Ps34:21); [75:10](https://ref.ly/Ps75:10); [Proverbs 3:33](https://ref.ly/Prov3:33); [10:3](https://ref.ly/Prov10:3), [6–7](https://ref.ly/Prov10:6-Prov10:7), [16](https://ref.ly/Prov10:16), [24–25](https://ref.ly/Prov10:24-Prov10:25); [John 9:2–3](https://ref.ly/John9:2-John9:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Galileans (ID: `group:Galilee`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Siloam (ID: `place:Siloam`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`); Watch-Tower (ID: `realia:Watch-tower`)

## Luke 13:4 (id: 20574)

*when the tower of Siloam collapsed*: This incident is not mentioned anywhere else in Scripture or other known records. The pool of Siloam was a reservoir in the southeastern part of Jerusalem. The tower may have been part of Jerusalem’s southern wall.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Siloam (ID: `place:Siloam`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`); Watch-Tower (ID: `realia:Watch-tower`)

## Luke 13:6 (id: 20576)

*a fig tree*: People sometimes described Israel as a fig tree without fruit or a vineyard that God would judge ([Isaiah 5:1–7](https://ref.ly/Isa5:1-Isa5:7); [Micah 7:1–2](https://ref.ly/Mic7:1-Mic7:2); see [Jeremiah 8:13](https://ref.ly/Jer8:13); [24:1–10](https://ref.ly/Jer24:1-Jer24:10); [Hosea 9:10](https://ref.ly/Hos9:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`)

## Luke 13:6–9 (id: 20575)

The parable of the barren fig tree (a story about a tree that does not produce fruit) shows the ministry of Jesus to Israel. If the nation did not produce the fruit of repentance (turning away from sin), it would face judgment. The story does not give a final ending, which shows that Israel still had a chance to respond. Compare with [Matthew 21:18–19](https://ref.ly/Matt21:18-Matt21:19) and the similar passage in [Mark 11:12–14](https://ref.ly/Mark11:12-Mark11:14).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:6-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Luke 13:9 (id: 20577)

*cut it down*: A stump or fallen tree symbolized judgement or destruction (compare [3:9](https://ref.ly/Luke3:9); see also [Isaiah 6:13](https://ref.ly/Isa6:13); [10:34](https://ref.ly/Isa10:34); [11:1](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1); [Jeremiah 46:22](https://ref.ly/Jer46:22); [Daniel 4:23](https://ref.ly/Dan4:23); [Matthew 3:10](https://ref.ly/Matt3:10); [7:19](https://ref.ly/Matt7:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:9


## Luke 13:10 (id: 20557)

*Jesus was teaching in one of the synagogues* (see [4:14–30](https://ref.ly/Luke4:14-Luke4:30)): Visiting Jewish rabbis (religious teachers with authority) often taught after reading the Law and the Prophets in the synagogue.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 13:11 (id: 20558)

*disabled by a spirit*: See study note on 11:14.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:11
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 11:14 (ID: `20489@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 13:14 (id: 20559)

*not on the Sabbath*: Jewish rabbis (religious teachers with authority) debated whether people should give medical help on the Sabbath day. They decided it was suitable only in extreme emergencies.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Religious Official (ID: `person:ReligiousOfficial`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 13:15–16 (id: 20560)

* *You hypocrites!* Hypocrites are people who pretend to be good or righteous, saying one thing but doing another. The religious leaders would care for their own animals on the Sabbath, but they would not help another person in need.

    There is also a play on words in the text. The same word is used for "untie" ([13:15](https://ref.ly/Luke13:15)) and "released" ([13:16](https://ref.ly/Luke13:16)). The leaders would free their animals but not a *daughter of Abraham*, one of God's chosen people.

* *whom Satan has kept bound*: This means she was under the power of Satan. Jesus drove out demons and healed people, which showed the kingdom of God (God’s rule) and the defeat of Satan (see [11:20](https://ref.ly/Luke11:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:15-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Animal Pen (ID: `realia:AnimalPen`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Luke 13:17 (id: 20561)

Jesus gave a wise answer that made his enemies feel shame and fall silent. In Jewish society, honor was a very important value.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glorious (ID: `keyterm:Glorious`)

## Luke 13:18–21 (id: 20562)

The parables of the mustard seed and the yeast reveal the nature of the kingdom of God. Like a mustard seed, it grows from a tiny size until it becomes large. Like yeast (*leaven*) in dough, it spreads through everything.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:18-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Saton (ID: `realia:Saton`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Mustard (ID: `flora:Mustard`)

## Luke 13:19 (id: 20563)

*the birds of the air nested in its branches*: The birds represent people who see the kingdom as a safe and secure place.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Mustard (ID: `flora:Mustard`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`)

## Luke 13:21 (id: 20565)

*It is like the leaven*: Leaven (or yeast) can represent evil (see [12:1](https://ref.ly/Luke12:1); [1 Corinthians 5:6](https://ref.ly/1Cor5:6); [Galatians 5:9](https://ref.ly/Gal5:9)). But here it shows the positive, spreading, and transforming power of the kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Saton (ID: `realia:Saton`)

## Luke 13:23 (id: 20566)

*will only a few people be saved?*: Some believed that only a small number would be saved. Others thought that all Jews would be saved because they were descendants of Abraham (see study note on 3:8).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:23
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 3:8 (ID: `20946@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); A Person (ID: `person:GenericPerson`)

## Luke 13:25 (id: 20567)

* *After the master of the house gets up and shuts the door*: At a banquet or feast, the master locks the door after the guests with an invitation arrive.
* *I do not know where you are from*: In Scripture, *knowing* often means being chosen by God for a special relationship ([Isaiah 63:16](https://ref.ly/Isa63:16); [Jeremiah 1:5](https://ref.ly/Jer1:5); [Amos 3:2](https://ref.ly/Amos3:2)). The people of Israel were God's chosen people. They came from Abraham's family line (*where you are from*), but those who failed to respond to the invitation did not have a relationship with God. The same can be said of all who fail to respond to the good news of the kingdom of God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Door (ID: `realia:Door`)

## Luke 13:28 (id: 20568)

* The phrase *weeping and gnashing of teeth* shows rejection and suffering.
* Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob were the three main founding fathers (patriarchs) of Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Luke 13:29 (id: 20569)

Although many Israelites, God's chosen people, have rejected the gospel, people from all over the world will accept God's offer of salvation and attend the future banquet of the Messiah (see [14:15](https://ref.ly/Luke14:15); [17:20](https://ref.ly/Luke17:20); [Isaiah 2:2](https://ref.ly/Isa2:2); [25:6–8](https://ref.ly/Isa25:6-Isa25:8); [51:4](https://ref.ly/Isa51:4); [52:10](https://ref.ly/Isa52:10); [55:5](https://ref.ly/Isa55:5); [56:7](https://ref.ly/Isa56:7); [59:19](https://ref.ly/Isa59:19); [Micah 4:1–2](https://ref.ly/Mic4:1-Mic4:2); [Zechariah 2:13](https://ref.ly/Zech2:13); [Malachi 1:11](https://ref.ly/Mal1:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 13:32 (id: 20570)

* *Go tell that fox*: In the first century AD, Jews saw foxes as harmful and animals that had no worth.
* *driving out demons and healing people*: These actions showed that the kingdom of God was already present. His resurrection on the third day would later show that he truly brought in God’s kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`)

## Luke 13:33 (id: 20571)

*a prophet to perish*: See “The Suffering Prophet” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:33
* **Associated Articles:** The Suffering Prophet (ID: `500360@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Luke 13:34 (id: 20572)

*as a hen gathers her chicks*: Compare [Deuteronomy 32:11](https://ref.ly/Deut32:11); [Ruth 2:12](https://ref.ly/Ruth2:12); [Psalms 17:8](https://ref.ly/Ps17:8); [36:7](https://ref.ly/Ps36:7); [57:1](https://ref.ly/Ps57:1); [61:4](https://ref.ly/Ps61:4); [63:7](https://ref.ly/Ps63:7); [91:4](https://ref.ly/Ps91:4).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rooster (ID: `fauna:Rooster`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`)

## Luke 13:35 (id: 20573)

* *Your house is left to you desolate*: Jesus predicted the destruction of Jerusalem that took place in AD 70\.
* *Blessed is He who comes in the name of the Lord*: This refers to [Psalm 118:26](https://ref.ly/Ps118:26). [Psalm 118](https://ref.ly/Ps118:1-Ps118:29) was one of the psalms sung by people traveling to Jerusalem for the annual festivals. Here, Jesus referred to his second coming.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 13:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 14:1 (id: 20584)

*in the home of a leading Pharisee*: In the Gospel of Luke, Jesus often eats with others.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 14:2 (id: 20591)

*a man with dropsy*: Dropsy, medically known as edema, is an accumulation of fluid in tissues or a body cavity that causes swelling. It is usually a symptom of a more serious illness.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:2


## Luke 14:3 (id: 20594)

*Is it lawful to heal on the Sabbath or not?*: The Jewish rabbis (religious teachers with authority) debated this question (see study note on 6:6–7).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:3
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 6:6-7 (ID: `21025@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 14:5 (id: 20595)

Jesus pointed out the hypocrisy of the Pharisees. Hypocrisy means pretending to be righteous while acting differently. See also study note on 13:15–16.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:5
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 13:15-16 (ID: `20560@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Luke 14:7 (id: 20596)

*the places of honor*: Meals in the ancient world showed social status. A person’s place at the table depended on that status. The food each guest received could also reflect status. These guests were trying to get the places of highest honor.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`)

## Luke 14:8 (id: 20597)

The response of Jesus comments on a teaching found in [Proverbs 25:6–7](https://ref.ly/Prov25:6-Prov25:7). In that passage, wisdom warns people not to take places of honor for themselves. Instead, they should wait to be invited to a higher place. When Jesus gave his response, he applied this wisdom to his own time. He taught that people should not seek honor for themselves. Instead, they should be humble and allow God or others to honor them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:8


## Luke 14:9 (id: 20598)

*in humiliation*: In first\-century Jewish culture, honor was very important. This kind of humiliation would have been almost worse than death.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:9


## Luke 14:12–13 (id: 20578)

*do not invite your friends*: Jesus challenged the common use of banquets to show and increase social status in the community. A host would invite friends of similar status and a few people of higher status. Those honored guests were then expected to invite the host back, which could improve the host’s reputation and position. 

Jesus turned this social order upside down by telling his followers to invite people who had no status and could not repay them. In this way, God invites sinful people to share in his banquet of salvation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:12-13


## Luke 14:14 (id: 20579)

*the resurrection of the righteous*: See [Daniel 12:2](https://ref.ly/Dan12:2).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`)

## Luke 14:15–24 (id: 20580)

This parable shows what was happening in the ministry of Jesus. The rich, powerful, and important people rejected his invitation to God’s banquet of salvation, so they would be left out. Meanwhile, poor people and outcasts accepted the invitation (see also [1:52–53](https://ref.ly/Luke1:52-Luke1:53); [6:21](https://ref.ly/Luke6:21), [25](https://ref.ly/Luke6:25); [10:15](https://ref.ly/Luke10:15); [18:14](https://ref.ly/Luke18:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:15-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); A Scribe (ID: `person:Scribe`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`); Interpret (ID: `keyterm:Interpret`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 14:17 (id: 20581)

*Come, for everything is now ready*: The invitations had been sent earlier. The guests were called when the meal was ready.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:17


## Luke 14:18 (id: 20582)

* *They all began to make excuses*: All these excuses would have deeply offended the host, who had spent much time and money preparing this important social event. These guests had already accepted the invitation, and their excuses were weak. Clearly, they did not want to come to the banquet.
* *I have bought a field, and I need to go see it*: No one would buy a *field* without seeing it first.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 14:19 (id: 20583)

*oxen, and I am going to try them out*: This is another weak excuse. No one would buy oxen without having seen them plow.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Interpret (ID: `keyterm:Interpret`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Luke 14:20 (id: 20585)

*I have married a wife*: Some people consider this to be a good excuse since the Old Testament excused men from military service during their first year of marriage ([Deuteronomy 20:7](https://ref.ly/Deut20:7); [24:5](https://ref.ly/Deut24:5)). But this feast was a local community event, not a distant war. Additionally, in an Israelite village, people would not have planned a marriage and a banquet at the same time, so there was no real conflict.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:20


## Luke 14:21 (id: 20586)

*The poor, the crippled, the blind, and the lame* were often treated as outsiders in Israel. Jesus reached out to them and helped them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:21


## Luke 14:23 (id: 20587)

*Go out to the highways and hedges*: These people invited might refer to the Gentile, who later received the good news about Jesus and the kingdom of God (compare [Acts 9:15](https://ref.ly/Acts9:15); [13:46–48](https://ref.ly/Acts13:46-Acts13:48); [18:4–6](https://ref.ly/Acts18:4-Acts18:6); [Romans 11:11–12](https://ref.ly/Rom11:11-Rom11:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 14:25–35 (id: 20588)

*large crowds were traveling with Jesus*: Jesus was very popular, but he was about to teach that the cost of following him was also very high.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:25-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`); Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`); Watch-Tower (ID: `realia:Watch-tower`); Foundation (ID: `realia:Foundation`)

## Luke 14:26 (id: 20589)

*If anyone comes to Me and does not hate … even his own life*: Following Jesus requires complete dedication. For the Christian, love for family and one's own life must not compete with devotion to Christ.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 14:27 (id: 20590)

*carry his cross*: This symbolizes rejecting personal desires. A criminal sentenced to crucifixion would carry the horizontal beam of the crossto the place where the authorities would kill them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 14:34 (id: 20592)

*Salt … if the salt loses its savor*: Sodium chloridecannot actually lose its saltiness. Jesus might be speaking hypothetically (if salt were to lose), or he might be referring to a combination of minerals that included salt. The salt around the Dead Sea was a mixture of sodium chloride and other minerals. If the sodium chloride dissolved or was removed, the remaining material still looked like salt but no longer tasted salty.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:34


## Luke 14:35 (id: 20593)

*It is fit neither for the soil nor for the manure pile*: Salt had various uses in the ancient world, including flavoring ([14:34](https://ref.ly/Luke14:34)), preserving, and weed\-killing. The sludge left after removing the sodium chloride was useless.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 14:35


## Luke 15:1 (id: 20604)

People greatly disliked *tax collectors* because they worked for the hated Roman authorities. The tax collectors had a reputation for not being honest.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`)

## Luke 15:1–32 (id: 20599)

Chapter 15 contains three related parables of things lost and found:

* a sheep ([15:1–7](https://ref.ly/Luke15:1-Luke15:7)),
* a coin ([15:8–10](https://ref.ly/Luke15:8-Luke15:10)), and
* a son ([15:11–32](https://ref.ly/Luke15:11-Luke15:32)).

The loss of something loved causes deep sorrow, whereas finding it brings great joy. There is great rejoicing in heaven when lost sinners return to their heavenly Father.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:1-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Hired Worker (ID: `keyterm:HiredWorker`); Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`); House (ID: `realia:House`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Drachma (ID: `realia:Drachma`); Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`); Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Be Enslaved (ID: `keyterm:Serve.2`); Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kid (ID: `fauna:Kid`); Carob (ID: `flora:Carob`); Sandal (ID: `realia:Sandal`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Seal (ID: `realia:Seal`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Luke 15:2 (id: 20610)

In their self\-righteous hard\-heartedness, the Pharisees and teachers of religious law did not care about lost people. Jesus embodied the heart of God, who longs for his wayward children to return.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`)

## Luke 15:4 (id: 20613)

* A flock of a hundred sheep was of average size for a shepherd of modest means. God’s people are often identified as the Lord’s flock ([Psalm 23](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1-Ps23:6); [Isaiah 53:6](https://ref.ly/Isa53:6); [Jeremiah 13:17](https://ref.ly/Jer13:17); [Ezekiel 34](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:1-Ezek34:31); [Zechariah 10:3](https://ref.ly/Zech10:3)).
* *leave the ninety\-nine*: Other shepherds could watch the ninety\-nine, so those left behind were not in danger. The shepherd would do anything to find the one that was lost.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Luke 15:8 (id: 20614)

* *Ten silver coins*: These coins might have been part of the woman’s dowry. A dowry was money or property given to a husband by the bride's family.
* *light a lamp, sweep her house*: Homes in Palestine were usually dimly lit and often had dirt or stone floors. Because of this, finding a small coin could be difficult.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Drachma (ID: `realia:Drachma`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 15:11–32 (id: 20600)

Like the previous two parables ([15:3–7](https://ref.ly/Luke15:3-Luke15:7), [8–10](https://ref.ly/Luke15:8-Luke15:10)), the parable of the lost son demonstrates God’s love for the lost and the joy God experiences when they return. It also reflects the ministry of Jesus. The father represents God, the younger son represents the tax collectors and sinners Jesus welcomed, and the older son represents the religious leaders.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:11-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Hired Worker (ID: `keyterm:HiredWorker`); Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`); Be Enslaved (ID: `keyterm:Serve.2`); Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Kid (ID: `fauna:Kid`); Carob (ID: `flora:Carob`); Sandal (ID: `realia:Sandal`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Seal (ID: `realia:Seal`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Luke 15:12 (id: 20601)

*give me my share of the estate*: At this time, this shocking request would basically mean, “To me, you are dead.” He could not say anything more insulting to his father.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`)

## Luke 15:13 (id: 20602)

*he squandered his wealth in wild living*: In Jewish culture, losing family property to Gentiles in a distant land was considered especially shameful. In some cases, it could even lead to exclusion from the community (see also [Deuteronomy 21:18–21](https://ref.ly/Deut21:18-Deut21:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)

## Luke 15:15 (id: 20603)

Pigs were ceremonially unclean animals ([Leviticus 11:7](https://ref.ly/Lev11:7); [Deuteronomy 14:8](https://ref.ly/Deut14:8)). So this was the most shameful job a Jew could have.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`)

## Luke 15:20 (id: 20605)

* *his father saw him*: The father was watching the road, hoping his son would return.
* *He ran to his son*: Since running was not appropriate for the leader of the family, this expressed how happy the father was that his son came back.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`)

## Luke 15:21 (id: 20606)

The son delivered the speech he had practiced. Some early Greek manuscripts add *Please take me on as a hired servant.* The absence of this sentence in the majority of the manuscripts suggests his father interrupted him before he could finish what he wanted to say.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 15:22 (id: 20607)

* The *best robe* belonged to the father. It affirmed the son’s role as an heir and a beloved member of the household.
* *Put a ring on his finger*: A ring was a symbol of authority. Like the robe, it indicated his status as son and heir.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Seal (ID: `realia:Seal`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Sandal (ID: `realia:Sandal`)

## Luke 15:23 (id: 20608)

The father had been preparing a calf for a feast. He welcomed his son with the kind of honor shown to an important guest.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Luke 15:28 (id: 20609)

The older brother was angry that his father would forgive such a sinner when he, the faithful son, had worked hard to achieve his position. Like the religious leaders, he refused to rejoice when his brother was found.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 15:30 (id: 20611)

*this son of yours*: He refused to acknowledge his own relationship to his brother.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Luke 15:31 (id: 20612)

*you are always with me*: The parable ends without telling us how the older brother responded. In the same way, the religious leaders still had a chance to respond to the offer of the kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 15:31


## Luke 16:1 (id: 20622)

*There was a rich man whose manager*: Wealthy landowners commonly put managers over their estates.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 16:2 (id: 20628)

*Turn in an account of your management*: This financial statement was probably meant for the person who would replace the manager.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Stewardship (ID: `keyterm:Stewardship`)

## Luke 16:3 (id: 20630)

*too ashamed to beg*: The Jewish wisdom writer Sirach said, “It is better to die than to beg” (*Sirach* 40:28\). This reflects how many Jews viewed begging.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Stewardship (ID: `keyterm:Stewardship`)

## Luke 16:5–7 (id: 20631)

The manager called in his master's debtors and reduced their debts. He made friends who would help him when he no longer had a job.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:5-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kor (ID: `realia:Kor.2`); Owe (ID: `keyterm:Owe`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Luke 16:6 (id: 20632)

*A hundred measures of olive oil … fifty*: The measure in Greek is a *batos* (from the Hebrew *bath*), a standard unit for measuring liquids. A hundred measures could have been about 3,000 liters (800 gallons), and fifty measures about 1,500 liters (400 gallons). The exact sizes are uncertain.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kor (ID: `realia:Kor.2`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Luke 16:7 (id: 20633)

*A hundred measures … eighty*: The Hebrew word for *measures* here is *cor*. This was the standard dry measure. A hundred measures could have been around 35,000 liters (9,300 gallons or 1,000 bushels) and eighty measures around 28,000 liters (7,400 gallons or 800 bushels). The exact size of the cor is uncertain.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kor (ID: `realia:Kor.2`); Owe (ID: `keyterm:Owe`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`)

## Luke 16:8 (id: 20635)

* The ways of the world are the opposite of God’s ways. The children of this world use all their resources to succeed or gain an advantage in this world. God’s people do just the opposite ([16:9](https://ref.ly/Luke16:9)).
* *the sons of the light*: These are God's people (see [John 12:36](https://ref.ly/John12:36); [1 Thessalonians 5:5](https://ref.ly/1Thess5:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Unjust Deed (ID: `keyterm:UnjustDeed`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`)

## Luke 16:8–9 (id: 20634)

Jesus seems to praise the manager for acting wisely, not for being dishonest. It likely means that believers should use their resources wisely as they prepare for eternity.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:8-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Unjust Deed (ID: `keyterm:UnjustDeed`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Luke 16:9 (id: 20636)

* *Worldly wealth*: This is a better translation than the traditional “mammon of unrighteousness” (KJV). Believers are to use their resources wisely for spiritual benefit.
* *they will welcome you into eternal dwellings*: This probably refers to the friends gained through faithful use of wealth. Just as the manager’s friends gave him security, the people brought into God’s kingdom will welcome believers into eternal homes.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Unjust Deed (ID: `keyterm:UnjustDeed`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Luke 16:13 (id: 20615)

*No servant can serve two masters*: Complete love and devotion can be given only to one master.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Enslaved (ID: `keyterm:Serve.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`)

## Luke 16:14 (id: 20616)

*The Pharisees* … *heard all of this and were scoffing*: People often thought wealth was a reward from God, so the Pharisees rejected the warning Jesus spoke about money.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Loving Wealth (ID: `keyterm:LovingWealth`)

## Luke 16:16 (id: 20617)

* John the Baptist was a transitional figure. He was the last of the Old Testament prophets and the messenger who announced the coming age of salvation (see [1:5–25](https://ref.ly/Luke1:5-Luke1:25), [57–80](https://ref.ly/Luke1:57-Luke1:80); [3:1–20](https://ref.ly/Luke3:1-Luke3:20); [7:18–35](https://ref.ly/Luke7:18-Luke7:35)).
* *everyone is forcing his way into it*: The verb usually refers to force or violence and may describe people trying to force their way into or oppose the kingdom (see [Matthew 11:12](https://ref.ly/Matt11:12)). More likely, however, it describes people urgently pressing forward to enter the kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Law (ID: `keyterm:Law`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); John (ID: `person:John`)

## Luke 16:17 (id: 20618)

* This does not mean the Law has lost its power. The new covenant fulfilled the Old Testament laws and prophets, but *the Law* remains (however, compare [Acts 15:23–29](https://ref.ly/Acts15:23-Acts15:29)).
* God will cause *heaven and earth to pass away* and replace them with a new heaven and earth at the end of the age ([Revelation 21:1](https://ref.ly/Rev21:1)). But God's word lasts forever ([Psalm 119:89](https://ref.ly/Ps119:89), [160](https://ref.ly/Ps119:160); [Isaiah 40:8](https://ref.ly/Isa40:8); [55:10–11](https://ref.ly/Isa55:10-Isa55:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Law (ID: `keyterm:Law`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 16:18 (id: 20619)

* This example fits the context by showing that the authoritative words of Jesus interpreted and fulfilled the Old Testament law.
* *Anyone who divorces his wife and marries another woman commits adultery*: The Old Testament allowed divorce but did not approve of it ([Deuteronomy 24:1–4](https://ref.ly/Deut24:1-Deut24:4)). In the time of Jesus, Jewish teachers debated what counted as valid grounds for divorce. The school of Shammai taught that a man could divorce his wife only for sexual unfaithfulness. The school of Hillel allowed divorce for almost any reason.

    Against this casual view of marriage, Jesus taught that divorcing a spouse and marrying another person was equal to committing adultery. In other passages ([Matthew 5:32](https://ref.ly/Matt5:32); [19:9](https://ref.ly/Matt19:9)), Jesus gave a clear exception for cases of unfaithfulness.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`)

## Luke 16:19 (id: 20621)

*Purple* was the color worn by royal people. Phoenician purple was an expensive dye made from mollusks.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`); Purple Cloth (ID: `realia:PurpleCloth`)

## Luke 16:19–31 (id: 20620)

This parable repeats the warning about the danger of riches ([16:13–15](https://ref.ly/Luke16:13-Luke16:15)). It also returns to the idea that when God’s Kingdom comes, many situations will be reversed (changed to the opposite).

The rich, proud, and powerful will be brought low, while the poor, humble, and oppressed will be lifted up.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:19-31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Lazarus (ID: `person:Lazarus`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); World of the Dead (ID: `keyterm:WorldOfTheDead`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Mercy`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Encourage (ID: `keyterm:Encourage`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Declare (ID: `keyterm:Declare`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Dog (ID: `fauna:Dog`); Purple Cloth (ID: `realia:PurpleCloth`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 16:21 (id: 20623)

*Even the dogs came and licked his sores*: This is an image of misery and poverty. In Jewish culture, dogs were seen as unclean animals and scavengers (animals that eat what others leave behind).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Dog (ID: `fauna:Dog`)

## Luke 16:22 (id: 20624)

*carried by the angels to Abraham’s side*: The Greek suggests a banquet at which guests reclined around a low table (see [John 13:23](https://ref.ly/John13:23)). Lazarus was taken to the messianic banquet in the kingdom of God (see [Luke 14:1–24](https://ref.ly/Luke14:1-Luke14:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Luke 16:23 (id: 20625)

*Hades*: This can refer to either the place where all people go when they die, or where the wicked go after death, as in this context.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lazarus (ID: `person:Lazarus`); World of the Dead (ID: `keyterm:WorldOfTheDead`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Luke 16:24 (id: 20626)

* *send Lazarus*: Ironically, the man was still treating Lazarus as a lowly servant.
* *I am in agony in this fire*: See [Matthew 25:41](https://ref.ly/Matt25:41); [Revelation 20:10](https://ref.ly/Rev20:10), [14–15](https://ref.ly/Rev20:14-Rev20:15).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lazarus (ID: `person:Lazarus`); Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Mercy`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Luke 16:26 (id: 20627)

*nor can anyone cross from there to us*: This verse gives the general principle that a person's state after death is permanent.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:26


## Luke 16:31 (id: 20629)

*they will not be persuaded even if someone rises from the dead*: Wicked people refuse to repent even when faced with overwhelming evidence of the truth.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 16:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`)

## Luke 17:1 (id: 20643)

* *Woe!*: This is a strong warning or expression of sorrow. In the Bible, it often introduces a message of judgment. See study notes on 6:24–26; 11:42–52.
* *the one through whom they come*: Leaders have more responsibility because they have to give an account for themselves and those they lead (see [James 3:1](https://ref.ly/Jas3:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:1
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 11:42-52 (ID: `20507@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 6:24-26 (ID: `21012@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Luke 17:2 (id: 20650)

A millstone is a large, round stone with a hole in its center. People used it to grind grain in a mill. A millstone could weigh hundreds of pounds.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cause to Stumble (ID: `keyterm:CauseToStumble`); Hand-Mill (ID: `realia:Hand-mill`)

## Luke 17:4 (id: 20654)

*Even if he sins against you seven times in a day*: The number *seven* here does not mean an exact number, but rather “many times” (see [Psalm 119:164](https://ref.ly/Ps119:164)). See [Matthew 18:21–22](https://ref.ly/Matt18:21-Matt18:22).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Luke 17:6 (id: 20655)

* *A mustard seed* was a common example of something very small (see [13:19](https://ref.ly/Luke13:19)).
* *you can say to this mulberry tree, ‘Be uprooted':* Black mulberry trees can live for hundreds of years and have wide and deep roots, making them very hard to pull out of the ground.
* *it will obey you*: This does not mean people should use faith to perform random miracles. God has unlimited power, and those who trust him will see him act according to his will.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mulberry (ID: `flora:Mulberry`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Mustard (ID: `flora:Mustard`)

## Luke 17:7–10 (id: 20656)

In Greco\-Roman culture, servants were expected to serve their masters faithfully. Faith ([17:5–6](https://ref.ly/Luke17:5-Luke17:6)) includes obeying Christ and submitting to his commands.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:7-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Obligated (ID: `keyterm:Ought`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

This healing reveals the compassion and power of Jesus. In Luke, the blessings of salvation are joyfully received by many outside Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:11-19

## Luke 17:12 (id: 20639)

*They stood at a distance*: See study note on 5:12. People with leprosy had to stay away from others and shout, “Unclean, unclean!” ([Leviticus 13:45–46](https://ref.ly/Lev13:45-Lev13:46)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:12
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 5:12 (ID: `20978@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 17:14 (id: 20640)

* *“Go, show yourselves to the priests.”*: [Leviticus 14:2–32](https://ref.ly/Lev14:2-Lev14:32) guides priests on how to determine what is leprosy and declare a leper ceremonially clean (see study notes on Luke 5:12, 14).
* *as they were in their way*: To leave without yet being healed required faith, which Jesus was testing (compare [2 Kings 5:9–14](https://ref.ly/2Kgs5:9-2Kgs5:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:14
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 5:12 (ID: `20978@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 5:14 (ID: `20980@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`)

## Luke 17:16 (id: 20641)
