The one man who returned to thank Jesus was a Samaritan*,* a hated foreigner in the eyes of most Jews (see study note on 10:33).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:16
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 10:33 (ID: `20475@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`); Samaritans (ID: `group:Samaritan`)

## Luke 17:19 (id: 20642)

*your faith has made you well!* (or *your faith has saved you*): The Greek verb can refer to either physical or spiritual healing. The physical healings of Jesus show the salvation that his kingdom brings.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Luke 17:20–21 (id: 20644)

* *When the kingdom of God would come*: Many Jews believed that the Messiah (Christ) would establish God's kingdom in Jerusalem. They expected him to defeat Israel's enemies and bring peace, prosperity, justice, and righteousness (see [3:15](https://ref.ly/Luke3:15); compare [Acts 1:6](https://ref.ly/Acts1:6)).

    The Pharisees were likely questioning the claim that Jesus was the Messiah (God’s chosen king). He was not defeating the Romans or setting up a visible kingdom on earth, as they expected. Jesus did not deny this future aspect of the kingdom (see [Luke 21:27](https://ref.ly/Luke21:27); [Matthew 24:30–31](https://ref.ly/Matt24:30-Matt24:31)). But he explained that the kingdom of God was being revealed through his ministry, though they were missing it.

* *The kingdom of God will not come with observable signs*: Jewish writings in the time of Jesus expected visible signs in the sky to announce the Messiah's kingdom. Jesus confirmed that such signs would appear (see [Luke 17:24](https://ref.ly/Luke17:24); [21:25](https://ref.ly/Luke21:25); [Acts 2:19–20](https://ref.ly/Acts2:19-Acts2:20)). But the Pharisees were missing how God’s kingdom was already being shown in the ministry of Jesus at that time (compare [Luke 7:22](https://ref.ly/Luke7:22)).
* *the kingdom of God is in your midst* (or *is within you,* or *is in your grasp*): The kingdom of God was already being shown through the words and actions of Jesus. It is unlikely that Jesus would say, “*the kingdom of God is in your midst*,” to those rejecting his message.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:20-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Observation (ID: `keyterm:Observation`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`)

## Luke 17:20–37 (id: 20645)

This is the first of two teachings in the Gospel of Luke about the coming of the kingdom of God and the return of the Son of Man (a title Jesus uses for himself). The second teaching appears later in chapter [21](https://ref.ly/Luke21:1-Luke21:38).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:20-37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Observation (ID: `keyterm:Observation`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); Make Alive (ID: `keyterm:Alive`); Boat (ID: `realia:Boat`); Vulture (ID: `fauna:Vulture`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Luke 17:22 (id: 20646)

*you will long to see one of the days of the Son of Man*: This may refer to his time on earth with his disciples or to the time of his second coming.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Luke 17:23–24 (id: 20647)

* *People will tell you, ‘Look, there He is!*: False messiahs have appeared throughout history. In the first century, many people claimed to be God's agents of salvation (see [Acts 5:36–37](https://ref.ly/Acts5:36-Acts5:37); [21:38](https://ref.ly/Acts21:38)). Jesus warned his disciples not to follow these false leaders.
* *just as lightning flashes:* Lightning lights up the whole sky and is visible to everyone in the area. Similarly, when Jesus suddenly returns, it will be evident to everyone.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:23-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 17:25 (id: 20648)

*suffer many things*:Jesus would soon die by crucifixion (see study note on 9:22). Crucifixion was a form of execution used by the Romans. The victim was nailed or tied to a wooden cross and left to die.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:25
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 9:22 (ID: `21106@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`)

## Luke 17:26–29 (id: 20649)

*in the days of Noah* *… in the days of Lot*: See [Genesis 6–9](https://ref.ly/Gen6:1-Gen9:29) for Noah and [Genesis 18:16–19:29](https://ref.ly/Gen18:16-Gen19:29) for Lot. Both stories describe God's severe judgment against human evil.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:26-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Boat (ID: `realia:Boat`)

## Luke 17:32–33 (id: 20651)

Lot’s wife turned into a pillar of salt when she looked longingly back at Sodom ([Genesis 19:26](https://ref.ly/Gen19:26)). She is an example of those who hold on to their old way of life instead of trusting God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:32-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Make Alive (ID: `keyterm:Alive`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`)

## Luke 17:34–35 (id: 20652)

*one will be taken and the other left*: The comparisons to God's judgment in the great flood and against Sodom ([17:26–29](https://ref.ly/Luke17:26-Luke17:29)), and the reference to vultures and a dead body ([17:37](https://ref.ly/Luke17:37)), suggest that this separation happens at the final judgment. This is when the wicked are taken away in judgment and the righteous are left (see [Malachi 3:18](https://ref.ly/Mal3:18); [Matthew 25:32](https://ref.ly/Matt25:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:34-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Luke 17:37 (id: 20653)

so these signs indicate that the end is near: This clause, which does not appear explicitly in the Greek, explains the significance of the cryptic saying about the vulture.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 17:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Vulture (ID: `fauna:Vulture`)

## Luke 18:1–8 (id: 20657)

The parable of the persistent widow follows naturally from the teaching of Jesus about coming troubles ([17:20–37](https://ref.ly/Luke17:20-Luke17:37)). Believers can face trials and persevere through persistent prayer.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:1-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Avenge (ID: `keyterm:GrantJustice`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); Unjust Deed (ID: `keyterm:UnjustDeed`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 18:2 (id: 20673)

*a judge who neither feared God nor respected men*: The two most important attributes for a good judge are regard for justice (fear of God), and compassion for people (see [2 Chronicles 19:6–7](https://ref.ly/2Chr19:6-2Chr19:7)). This judge had neither.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Luke 18:3 (id: 20682)

* *a widow*: God cares for widows, orphans, and foreigners. These were the most vulnerable people in ancient society. He has promised to judge those who treat them badly ([Exodus 22:22](https://ref.ly/Exod22:22); [Deuteronomy 10:18](https://ref.ly/Deut10:18); [24:17](https://ref.ly/Deut24:17); [27:19](https://ref.ly/Deut27:19); [Psalm 68:5](https://ref.ly/Ps68:5); [Isaiah 1:23](https://ref.ly/Isa1:23); [10:2](https://ref.ly/Isa10:2); [Jeremiah 22:3](https://ref.ly/Jer22:3); [Ezekiel 22:7](https://ref.ly/Ezek22:7); [Amos 5:10–13](https://ref.ly/Amos5:10-Amos5:13); [Zechariah 7:10](https://ref.ly/Zech7:10); [Malachi 3:5](https://ref.ly/Mal3:5)).
* *Give me justice*: The woman was right in this dispute. She was not asking for special treatment.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Avenge (ID: `keyterm:GrantJustice`)

## Luke 18:5 (id: 20683)

*keeps pestering me*: This vivid Greek expression literally means “striking the eye” or “giving me a black eye,” like a boxer hitting an opponent. It describes someone being worn down by constant persistence.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Avenge (ID: `keyterm:GrantJustice`)

## Luke 18:7 (id: 20684)

*Will not God bring about justice*: If persistence resulted in justice from this unjust judge, how much more will God, who loves both people and justice, answer our prayers.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Avenge (ID: `keyterm:GrantJustice`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`)

## Luke 18:8 (id: 20685)

* *When the Son of Man returns*: This refers back to the vision in [Daniel 7:13–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:13-Dan7:14).
* *will He find faith on earth?*: The question is whether believers will remain faithful to God during the difficulties that happen before the second coming of the Son of Man (see [Matthew 24:10–12](https://ref.ly/Matt24:10-Matt24:12); [2 Thessalonians 2:3](https://ref.ly/2Thess2:3); [1 Timothy 4:1](https://ref.ly/1Tim4:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Avenge (ID: `keyterm:GrantJustice`)

## Luke 18:9–14 (id: 20686)

The topic of prayer ([18:1–8](https://ref.ly/Luke18:1-Luke18:8)) leads into this parable about the right attitude for approaching God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:9-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); Week (ID: `keyterm:Week`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 18:10 (id: 20658)

*a tax collector*: See study note on 3:12.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:10
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 3:12 (ID: `20927@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 18:12 (id: 20659)

*I fast twice a week and pay tithes of all that I acquire*: Fasting (not eating food) and tithing (giving a tenth of one's income) were signs of religious devotion in Judaism. The law required fasting only once a year ([Leviticus 16:29–31](https://ref.ly/Lev16:29-Lev16:31)). But many devout Jews in the time of Jesus fasted twice a week (see also [Luke 5:33](https://ref.ly/Luke5:33); [11:41–42](https://ref.ly/Luke11:41-Luke11:42)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); Week (ID: `keyterm:Week`)

## Luke 18:13 (id: 20660)

*he beat his breast*: This action showed deep sorrow and repentance (turning away from sin).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 18:14 (id: 20661)

* Jesus concluded that only the tax collector went home justified before God. This would have surprised the people listening to Jesus, who considered Pharisees righteous and tax collectors evil.
* *everyone who exalts himself will be humbled*: See [1:52–53](https://ref.ly/Luke1:52-Luke1:53); [6:21](https://ref.ly/Luke6:21), [25](https://ref.ly/Luke6:25); [10:15](https://ref.ly/Luke10:15); [14:11](https://ref.ly/Luke14:11); [16:19–31](https://ref.ly/Luke16:19-Luke16:31).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 18:15–17 (id: 20662)

* This incident illustrates [18:14](https://ref.ly/Luke18:14).
* *they rebuked those who brought them*: In this cultural setting, children had little social importance (see study note on 9:48). Because of this, the disciples saw them as interrupting the important ministry of Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:15-17
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 9:48 (ID: `21128@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 18:17 (id: 20663)

*receive the kingdom of God like a little child*: Entrance into the kingdom of God requires childlike faith and dependence on God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 18:18 (id: 20665)

* The man was probably a religious leader, not a political ruler.
* *what must I do to inherit eternal life?*: This question was commonly discussed by the rabbis in the time of Jesus (see [10:27](https://ref.ly/Luke10:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`)

## Luke 18:18–30 (id: 20664)

The story of the rich religious leader warns against trusting in riches rather than pledging complete allegiance to God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:18-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Rich Young Ruler (ID: `person:RichYoungRuler`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Needle (ID: `realia:Needle`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`)

## Luke 18:19 (id: 20666)

*Why do you call Me good?*: The man called Jesus *good* as a compliment, but only God is truly good. Jesus challenged the man's understanding of goodness. True goodness requires moral perfection.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 18:20 (id: 20667)

*You know the commandments*: Jesus refers to the fifth through ninth of the Ten Commandments ([Exodus 20:12–16](https://ref.ly/Exod20:12-Exod20:16); [Deuteronomy 5:16–20](https://ref.ly/Deut5:16-Deut5:20)). These commandments are about relationships between human beings.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`); Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`)

## Luke 18:21 (id: 20668)

*All these I have kept*: The man’s claim to have kept all the commandments shows that he misunderstood the nature of true goodness.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rich Young Ruler (ID: `person:RichYoungRuler`)

## Luke 18:22 (id: 20669)

The man's love for his wealth showed he did not fully obey God. He did not love God or others as God requires ([10:25–28](https://ref.ly/Luke10:25-Luke10:28)). Loving wealth is a form of worshiping false gods (idolatry) ([Ephesians 5:5](https://ref.ly/Eph5:5); [Colossians 3:5](https://ref.ly/Col3:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 18:24–25 (id: 20670)

* *How hard it is for the rich to enter the kingdom of God!*: Scripture sometimes describes riches or wealth as a blessing from God ([2 Chronicles 1:11–12](https://ref.ly/2Chr1:11-2Chr1:12); [Psalms 112:3](https://ref.ly/Ps112:3); [128:2](https://ref.ly/Ps128:2); [Proverbs 8:18](https://ref.ly/Prov8:18); [Isaiah 61:6](https://ref.ly/Isa61:6)). But many passages warn against trusting in riches instead of God ([Psalm 62:10](https://ref.ly/Ps62:10); [Proverbs 11:28](https://ref.ly/Prov11:28); [Jeremiah 9:23–24](https://ref.ly/Jer9:23-Jer9:24); [49:4–5](https://ref.ly/Jer49:4-Jer49:5)).
* *the eye of a needle*: See study note on Mark 10:25. This is not only difficult, but impossible. No one can be saved while trusting in riches. Salvation comes only by dependence on God ([Luke 18:27](https://ref.ly/Luke18:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:24-25
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 10:25 (ID: `21237@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Needle (ID: `realia:Needle`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`)

## Luke 18:27 (id: 20671)

The point of this whole event is that salvation is impossible by human effort, but possible by the grace of God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 18:28–30 (id: 20672)

*we have left all we had to follow You*: In contrast to the rich ruler, Peter and the other disciples had given up everything to follow Jesus. Jesus affirmed that they would receive back far more than they had given up.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:28-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 18:30 (id: 20674)

*receive many times more in this age*: Their reward would not necessarily be physical, but they would enjoy the blessings that come from spiritual wholeness and a right relationship with God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`)

## Luke 18:31 (id: 20676)

The death of Jesus fulfilled all the predictions of the Old Testament prophets. It was God's plan ([Luke 24:25–26](https://ref.ly/Luke24:25-Luke24:26), [46](https://ref.ly/Luke24:46); [Acts 2:23](https://ref.ly/Acts2:23); [3:18](https://ref.ly/Acts3:18); [4:28](https://ref.ly/Acts4:28)). 

The main prophecy about the suffering Messiah is in [Isaiah 52:13–53:12](https://ref.ly/Isa52:13-Isa53:12). Luke also refers to several Old Testament prophecies, including: 

* [Psalm 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12) in [Acts 4:25–26](https://ref.ly/Acts4:25-Acts4:26),
* [Psalm 16](https://ref.ly/Ps16:1-Ps16:11) in [Acts 2:25–28](https://ref.ly/Acts2:25-Acts2:28),
* [Psalm 118:22](https://ref.ly/Ps118:22) in [Luke 20:17](https://ref.ly/Luke20:17), and
* [Isaiah 50:4–9](https://ref.ly/Isa50:4-Isa50:9) in [Luke 18:32–33](https://ref.ly/Luke18:32-Luke18:33).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Luke 18:31–34 (id: 20675)

This was the last time Jesus predicted his suffering and death before he entered Jerusalem for his final week of ministry (see also [9:22](https://ref.ly/Luke9:22), [44–45](https://ref.ly/Luke9:44-Luke9:45); [17:25](https://ref.ly/Luke17:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:31-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`)

## Luke 18:32–33 (id: 20677)

*will be mocked …* *will flog Him*: See [Isaiah 50:6](https://ref.ly/Isa50:6).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:32-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`)

## Luke 18:34 (id: 20678)

*The disciples did not understand any of these things*: The disciples did not understand that the death of Jesus would bring salvation until after he rose from the dead (see [9:45](https://ref.ly/Luke9:45); [24:13–34](https://ref.ly/Luke24:13-Luke24:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`)

## Luke 18:35 (id: 20680)

Jericho was located in an oasis in the Judean wilderness, 30 kilometers (18 miles) northeast of Jerusalem. At 250 meters (820 feet) below sea level, it is the world's lowest city and one of the oldest.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jericho (ID: `place:Jericho`)

## Luke 18:35–43 (id: 20679)

This story shows again how Jesus cares for the poor and people who had less social status in Israel. It also reminds us that Jesus is the Son of David, the Christ ([18:38](https://ref.ly/Luke18:38)), shortly before he enters Jerusalem as king ([19:28–44](https://ref.ly/Luke19:28-Luke19:44)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:35-43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); David (ID: `person:David`); Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Mercy`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Jericho (ID: `place:Jericho`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`)

## Luke 18:38 (id: 20681)

*Son of David* is a title for the Christ (the Messiah, God's chosen king). The Christ is a descendant of David who will rule on David’s throne forever (see [1:32–33](https://ref.ly/Luke1:32-Luke1:33); [2 Samuel 7:11–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:11-2Sam7:16); [Isaiah 9:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa9:6-Isa9:7); [11:1–5](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:5); [Jeremiah 23:5–6](https://ref.ly/Jer23:5-Jer23:6); [33:15–16](https://ref.ly/Jer33:15-Jer33:16); [Ezekiel 37:24–25](https://ref.ly/Ezek37:24-Ezek37:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 18:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Mercy`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); David (ID: `person:David`)

## Luke 19:1–10 (id: 20687)

The story of Zacchaeus is a fitting high point to the ministry of Jesus among those treated badly in Israel while he traveled to Jerusalem ([9:51–19:44](https://ref.ly/Luke9:51-Luke19:44)). Many see the final verse of this episode as the main theme of Luke's Gospel ([19:10](https://ref.ly/Luke19:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:1-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Zacchaeus (ID: `person:Zacchaeus`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Jericho (ID: `place:Jericho`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Sycomore Fig (ID: `flora:SycomoreFig`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 19:2 (id: 20698)

A *chief tax collector* paid the Roman government for the right to collect taxes in a certain region. He then supervised other tax collectors and received money from the taxes they collected. This system allowed Zacchaeus to become very rich. As a result, chief tax collectors were hated even more than the tax collectors under them (see study note on 3:12).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:2
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 3:12 (ID: `20927@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zacchaeus (ID: `person:Zacchaeus`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 19:4 (id: 20709)

*climbed a sycamore\-fig tree*: This action was very undignified for a man with the power and wealth of Zacchaeus. It shows that he truly wanted to see and know Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sycomore Fig (ID: `flora:SycomoreFig`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`)

## Luke 19:5 (id: 20710)

*Zacchaeus …* *I must stay at your house today*: Jesus knew Zacchaeus's name and invited himself to his home. This showed that the authority of Jesus is from God and that this meeting was part of God’s plan.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zacchaeus (ID: `person:Zacchaeus`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 19:7 (id: 20711)

*The guest of a sinful man*: People also criticized Jesus for spending time with sinners, as they did when he was with Levi ([5:27–32](https://ref.ly/Luke5:27-Luke5:32); see study note on 5:30).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:7
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 5:30 (ID: `20990@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 19:8 (id: 20712)

*I will repay it fourfold*: The usual payment for causing financial loss was to repay the amount taken plus twenty percent ([Leviticus 5:16](https://ref.ly/Lev5:16); [Numbers 5:7](https://ref.ly/Num5:7)). However, the penalty for stealing an animal was four or five times its value ([Exodus 22:1](https://ref.ly/Exod22:1)). Zacchaeus apparently viewed the money he had gained through dishonesty as theft and promised to repay it fully.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zacchaeus (ID: `person:Zacchaeus`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 19:9 (id: 20713)

*this man too is a son of Abraham*: Zacchaeus was *a son of Abraham* by birth because he was a Jew. Now he also showed the same kind of faith that Abraham had (see [Romans 4:11–12](https://ref.ly/Rom4:11-Rom4:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Luke 19:10 (id: 20688)

*to seek and to save the lost*: The Lord Jesus is like a shepherd who looks for lost sheep (see [Psalm 23:1](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1); [Isaiah 53:6](https://ref.ly/Isa53:6); [Jeremiah 13:17](https://ref.ly/Jer13:17); [Ezekiel 34](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:1-Ezek34:31); [Zechariah 10:3](https://ref.ly/Zech10:3); [13:7](https://ref.ly/Zech13:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Luke 19:11–27 (id: 20689)

This *parable* has two main purposes: 

1. To teach stewardship. This means managing gifts and resources well while the *man of noble birth* (Jesus) is away (compare [Matthew 25:14–30](https://ref.ly/Matt25:14-Matt25:30)).
2. To correct the belief that the kingdom of God would start immediately when Jesus entered Jerusalem. Most Jews in the time of Jesus expected that when the Messiah (the Christ) came, God’s kingdom would be established physically on earth, with Jerusalem at its center (see [Isaiah 2:2–4](https://ref.ly/Isa2:2-Isa2:4); [35:1–10](https://ref.ly/Isa35:1-Isa35:10); [65:17–25](https://ref.ly/Isa65:17-Isa65:25); [Jeremiah 30–31](https://ref.ly/Jer30:1-Jer31:40); [Ezekiel 37](https://ref.ly/Ezek37:1-Ezek37:28), [40–48](https://ref.ly/Ezek40:1-Ezek48:35); [Micah 4:1–5](https://ref.ly/Mic4:1-Mic4:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:11-27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mina (ID: `realia:Mina`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Faithfulness (ID: `keyterm:Faithfulness`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Band of Cloth (ID: `realia:BandOfCloth`)

## Luke 19:12 (id: 20690)

*A man of noble birth went to a distant country to lay claim to his kingship*: This account shows Judea's status as a client kingdom of the Roman Empire. After a king's death, those claiming the right to be king would travel to Rome to seek support. Similarly, Jesus would first leave for heaven to receive his royal authority and later return to rule God's people and judge those who oppose him (see [19:14](https://ref.ly/Luke19:14), [27](https://ref.ly/Luke19:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 19:14 (id: 20691)

*his subjects hated him and sent a delegation*: This happened to Archelaus, the son of Herod the Great, who violently put down protests after the death of his father. In response, the Jews sent representatives to plead against his kingship before the Roman emperor.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:14


## Luke 19:17 (id: 20692)

*you shall have authority over ten cities*:Because of the servant’s faithfulness, the king gave him a major position in his kingdom. Similarly, those who are faithful to Jesus in this life will receive greater responsibility here and great rewards in heaven.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Faithfulness (ID: `keyterm:Faithfulness`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`)

## Luke 19:23 (id: 20693)

*deposit my money in the bank (*Literally *put the money on the table*): This means to give it to those lending money who will loan it out for interest.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:23


## Luke 19:26 (id: 20694)

*will be given more*: Those who are trustworthy with a small amount will receive more (compare [8:18](https://ref.ly/Luke8:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:26


## Luke 19:27 (id: 20695)

* *And these enemies of mine*: Those who do not accept Jesus as king will encounter his judgment. This part of the parable was directed against the religious leaders.
* *bring them here and slay them*: A new king would often kill his opponents (see [1 Kings 2:13–46](https://ref.ly/1Kgs2:13-1Kgs2:46); compare [Psalm 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:27


## Luke 19:28–40 (id: 20696)

Jesus entered Jerusalem in triumph. This fulfilled [Zechariah 9:9–10](https://ref.ly/Zech9:9-Zech9:10) and symbolically announced that he was the Messiah, the King of Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:28-40

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Olive Tree (ID: `flora:OliveTree`); Bethphage (ID: `place:Bethphage`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Bethany (ID: `place:Bethany`); Mount of Olives (ID: `place:OlivesMount`); Height (ID: `keyterm:Height`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`)

## Luke 19:29 (id: 20697)

* Bethany was on the eastern slope of the Mount of Olives, over three kilometers (two miles) east of Jerusalem. It was the home of Lazarus and his sisters, Mary and Martha ([John 11:1](https://ref.ly/John11:1), [18](https://ref.ly/John11:18); [12:1](https://ref.ly/John12:1); see [Luke 10:38](https://ref.ly/Luke10:38)).

    The exact location of Bethphage is unknown, but it was likely near Bethany on the road from Jericho.

* *the Mount of Olives*: The Mount of Olives overlooks Jerusalem from the east and has significance at the return of Christ (see [Acts 1:11](https://ref.ly/Acts1:11); [Zechariah 14:4](https://ref.ly/Zech14:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bethany (ID: `place:Bethany`); Bethphage (ID: `place:Bethphage`); Mount of Olives (ID: `place:OlivesMount`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Olive Tree (ID: `flora:OliveTree`)

## Luke 19:30 (id: 20699)

*You will see a young donkey*: It is unclear whether Jesus had arranged for the donkey ahead of time or whether he used divine knowledge. Either way, his entry into Jerusalem was a symbolic act. Jesus rode on a humble donkey instead of a warhorse to show that he was the Christ who came to bring peace and reconciliation ([Zechariah 9:9–10](https://ref.ly/Zech9:9-Zech9:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`)

## Luke 19:31 (id: 20700)

*The Lord needs it*: Subjects of a king were expected to make resources available for his use (see [1 Samuel 8:16](https://ref.ly/1Sam8:16)). The donkey's colt was pure and suitable for a king because no one had ever ridden it.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 19:36 (id: 20701)

The crowds *spread their cloaks* to show respect and honor to this royal figure (see [2 Kings 9:13](https://ref.ly/2Kgs9:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)

## Luke 19:38 (id: 20702)

* *Blessed is the King who comes in the name of the Lord!* See [Psalms 118:26](https://ref.ly/Ps118:26); [148:1](https://ref.ly/Ps148:1). [Psalm 118](https://ref.ly/Ps118:1-Ps118:29) was one of the songs sung by travelers going to Jerusalem for the festivals ([Psalms 113–118](https://ref.ly/Ps113:1-Ps118:29)).
* Jesus brought *peace in heaven and glory in the highest.* He makes peace with God and humans, but peace on earth is still to come (compare [Luke 2:14](https://ref.ly/Luke2:14); see [12:51–53](https://ref.ly/Luke12:51-Luke12:53); [19:41–44](https://ref.ly/Luke19:41-Luke19:44)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Height (ID: `keyterm:Height`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Luke 19:40 (id: 20703)

Compare [Isaiah 55:12](https://ref.ly/Isa55:12), in which nature is said to rejoice at the coming of God’s salvation, and [Habakkuk 2:11](https://ref.ly/Hab2:11), in which the stones of the walls cry out against Babylon.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:40


## Luke 19:42 (id: 20704)

*now it is hidden from your eyes*: Israel’s rejection of Jesus had serious consequences. In this context, the decision was final and could not be reversed.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`)

## Luke 19:43–44 (id: 20705)

* Jesus foretold that the Romans would surround and destroy Jerusalem in AD 70\. The Jewish historian Josephus describes the terrible suffering of the people in Jerusalem during the Roman siege (*War* 5\.1\.1–7\.1\.1\).
* *will not leave one stone on another*: This expression describes complete destruction.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:43-44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Siege-Works (ID: `realia:Siege-works`)

## Luke 19:45 (id: 20707)

People who came to worship at the temple bought animals there for sacrifices. Jesus also drove out the money changers who exchanged ordinary money for the special coins used to pay the temple tax ([Matthew 21:12](https://ref.ly/Matt21:12); [Mark 11:15](https://ref.ly/Mark11:15); see [Exodus 30:13–16](https://ref.ly/Exod30:13-Exod30:16); [Nehemiah 10:32–33](https://ref.ly/Neh10:32-Neh10:33)). 

These services were necessary, but Jesus opposed the dishonest business practices that turned the temple into a place for personal profit instead of worship. The temple was meant to display God’s glory to all nations, but it had become corrupt.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 19:45–48 (id: 20706)

After entering Jerusalem as the Christ, Jesus performed an action that showed he was the Christ (see [Malachi 3:1](https://ref.ly/Mal3:1)). He *began to drive out* from the temple those who exchanged money and merchants selling animals for offerings. This action was meant to restore true worship in the temple. It also symbolized the judgment that Jesus had just announced against Israel ([19:41–44](https://ref.ly/Luke19:41-Luke19:44)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:45-48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 19:47 (id: 20708)

* The clearing of the temple increased opposition to Jesus and helped lead to his death on the cross (see study note on Mark 11:12–25).
* In Galilee, the main opponents of Jesus were the Pharisees and teachers of religious law. In Jerusalem, the leading priests also opposed him because they controlled the temple. The actions of Jesus against the buying and selling in the temple threatened their authority.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 19:47
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 11:12-25 (ID: `21264@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 20:1 (id: 20758)

*the chief priests and scribes, together with the elders*: These were the most influential Jewish leaders in Jerusalem. They united against Jesus because he threatened their authority.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 20:1–47 (id: 20751)

In this series of controversies between Jesus and the religious leaders of Jerusalem, they repeatedly questioned and challenged him, and he repeatedly outmatched them with his spiritual wisdom, insight, and authority.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:1-47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`); David (ID: `person:David`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); John (ID: `person:John`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Empty (ID: `keyterm:Empty`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Sadducees (ID: `group:Sadducee`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); A Priest (ID: `person:GenericPriest`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Judgment (ID: `keyterm:Judgment`); Hypocrisy (ID: `keyterm:Hypocrisy`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); Beloved (ID: `keyterm:Beloved`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Friendship (ID: `keyterm:Friendship`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Tribute Tax (ID: `keyterm:TributeTax`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Denarius (ID: `realia:Denarius`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Cornerstone (ID: `realia:Cornerstone`); Footstool (ID: `realia:Footstool`)

## Luke 20:2 (id: 20767)

*by what authority are You doing these things*: Since these groups were the recognized Jewish authorities in Jerusalem, they wanted to know how Jesus could claim authority to enter Jerusalem as a king and drive the merchants from the temple.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 20:4–6 (id: 20773)

The question Jesus asked trapped the religious leaders (see [20:5–6](https://ref.ly/Luke20:5-Luke20:6)) because either answer would accuse them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`)

## Luke 20:6 (id: 20781)

*The people will stone us*: Stoning was a capital punishment where people threw rocks at the offender until he or she died. Stoning was the punishment for:

* blasphemy (speaking against God; [Leviticus 24:14–15](https://ref.ly/Lev24:14-Lev24:15)).\=,
* idolatry (worshiping false gods; [Leviticus 20:2](https://ref.ly/Lev20:2); [Deuteronomy 13:10](https://ref.ly/Deut13:10)), and
* other acts of opposition against God.

The people might have considered rejecting a true prophet as blasphemy, which deserved the penalty of stoning.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 20:7–8 (id: 20782)

The Jewish authorities refused to answer the question Jesus asked, so Jesus had no obligation to answer their question. Both their question and their refusal were guided by self\-interest rather than integrity.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 20:9 (id: 20784)

*rented it out to some tenants*: This was a common practice in first\-century Palestine, especially in Galilee. Many poor farmers would rent land from wealthy owners so they could grow crops.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Luke 20:9–19 (id: 20783)

This story adapts Isaiah’s Song of the Vineyard ([Isaiah 5:1–7](https://ref.ly/Isa5:1-Isa5:7)) to describe the rejection of Jesus by Israel’s leaders. In Isaiah’s story, the vineyard owner represents God, and the vineyard represents Israel, which failed to produce good fruit and would be judged. 

Jesus added new characters to the story. The tenant farmers represent Israel’s unrighteous leaders, the servants represent the Old Testament prophets who were repeatedly rejected, and the son represents Jesus. God would judge Israel’s leaders for rejecting and killing his Son.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:9-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Empty (ID: `keyterm:Empty`); Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Beloved (ID: `keyterm:Beloved`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Cornerstone (ID: `realia:Cornerstone`)

## Luke 20:10 (id: 20752)

*the tenants beat the servan**t*: God’s prophets were often mistreated and opposed by the people of Israel ([1 Kings 19:10](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:10), [14](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:14); [2 Chronicles 24:21](https://ref.ly/2Chr24:21); [36:16](https://ref.ly/2Chr36:16); [Nehemiah 9:26](https://ref.ly/Neh9:26); [Jeremiah 2:30](https://ref.ly/Jer2:30); [26:20–24](https://ref.ly/Jer26:20-Jer26:24); [37:15](https://ref.ly/Jer37:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Empty (ID: `keyterm:Empty`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Luke 20:13 (id: 20753)

*my beloved son*: The Messiah was predicted to have a unique father\-son relationship with God (see [2 Samuel 7:14](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:14); [Psalms 2:7](https://ref.ly/Ps2:7); [89:26–29](https://ref.ly/Ps89:26-Ps89:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Beloved (ID: `keyterm:Beloved`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Luke 20:14 (id: 20754)

*Let us kill him, and the inheritance will be ours*: They believed that killing the heir would give them power over the estate. They foolishly failed to realize that the owner was still alive and would demand justice. Similarly, the religious leaders thought that they could keep their authority over the people of Israel by killing Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:14


## Luke 20:17 (id: 20755)

*The stone … cornerstone*: Jesus quoted [Psalm 118:22](https://ref.ly/Ps118:22) to predict that he would be rejected and later restored to honor. The cornerstone was the most important stone in a building because it helped support and align the walls. Jesus was rejected by the builders, meaning the religious leaders, but he would become the foundation of a new building, the church.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Cornerstone (ID: `realia:Cornerstone`)

## Luke 20:18 (id: 20756)

Jesus expanded the stone metaphor ([20:17](https://ref.ly/Luke20:17)) with references to [Isaiah 8:14–15](https://ref.ly/Isa8:14-Isa8:15) and [Daniel 2:34](https://ref.ly/Dan2:34), [44–45](https://ref.ly/Dan2:44-Dan2:45). Although Israel rejected him, Jesus became the foundation for the new people of God. He began the reign of a kingdom that would last forever.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:18


## Luke 20:19 (id: 20757)

*realized that Jesus had spoken this parable against them*: The story provoked the religious leaders to act it out by arresting and killing Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Luke 20:20–26 (id: 20759)

The religious leaders asked this question to trap Jesus, but he answered in a way that avoided their trap.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:20-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Hypocrisy (ID: `keyterm:Hypocrisy`); Tribute Tax (ID: `keyterm:TributeTax`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Denarius (ID: `realia:Denarius`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Luke 20:21 (id: 20760)

*we know that You speak and teach correctly*: These dishonest words of praise were meant to trick Jesus (see [Psalms 5:9](https://ref.ly/Ps5:9); [12:2–3](https://ref.ly/Ps12:2-Ps12:3); [78:36](https://ref.ly/Ps78:36); [Proverbs 26:28](https://ref.ly/Prov26:28); [28:23](https://ref.ly/Prov28:23); [29:5](https://ref.ly/Prov29:5); [Ezekiel 12:24](https://ref.ly/Ezek12:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)

## Luke 20:22 (id: 20761)

*Is it lawful for us to pay taxes to Caesar or not?*: If Jesus said yes, he would make the people angry, because they hated the burden of Roman taxation. If he said no, he would be accused of rebellion against the Roman authorities.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Tribute Tax (ID: `keyterm:TributeTax`); Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`)

## Luke 20:24 (id: 20762)

*a denarius*: This Roman coin had the image and inscription of the emperor Tiberius Caesar (see [3:1](https://ref.ly/Luke3:1)) stamped on it.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`); Denarius (ID: `realia:Denarius`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Luke 20:25 (id: 20763)

*Give to Caesar what is Caesar’s*: Jesus gave a clever answer. There are two ways to understand this statement. On the surface, he said that the coin belonged to Caesar and should be given to him. But his Jewish listeners also understood that, in the end, nothing truly belongs to Caesar, because everything belongs to God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`)

## Luke 20:27 (id: 20764)

*the Sadducees, who say there is no resurrection*: See "The Sadducees" Profile.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:27
* **Associated Articles:** The Sadducees (ID: `500538@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sadducees (ID: `group:Sadducee`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`)

## Luke 20:28 (id: 20765)

*Moses wrote for us*: A dead man’s brother would marry the widow to produce children who would maintain the dead man’s name and property ([Deuteronomy 25:5–6](https://ref.ly/Deut25:5-Deut25:6); see also [Genesis 38:8–10](https://ref.ly/Gen38:8-Gen38:10); [Ruth 4:1–12](https://ref.ly/Ruth4:1-Ruth4:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Luke 20:29–33 (id: 20766)

Well, suppose there were seven brothers: A similar story of a woman married to seven husbands appears in the Old Testament Apocrypha (*Tobit* 3:7–17; 6:10–8:18\).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:29-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`)

## Luke 20:33 (id: 20768)

*in the resurrection, whose wife will she be?*: The Sadducees told this made\-up story to show that the idea of resurrection (rising from the dead) did not make sense. They wanted people to think that belief in resurrection was foolish.

They likely used this same argument many times. It was a common attack that the Sadducees used when they debated the Pharisees.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`)

## Luke 20:35 (id: 20769)

*will neither marry nor be given in marriage*: Jesus taught that marriage belongs to this present life on earth. It is not part of life after the resurrection (when the dead rise to life again).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`)

## Luke 20:36 (id: 20770)

*because they are like the angels*: Angels are not married, and they are immortal. The Bible never teaches that people become angels (compare[Hebrews 1:5–2:18](https://ref.ly/Heb1:5-Heb2:18)), but that we will be like them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 20:37 (id: 20771)

* After answering the Sadducees’ question about marriage, Jesus turned to the larger question of the resurrection (rising from the dead).
* *Even Moses demonstrates that*: Jesus used the books of Moses to show that the resurrection is true. These books (Genesis, Exodus, Leviticus, Numbers, and Deuteronomy) were the only Scripture the Sadducees recognized as authoritative.
* *the God of Abraham … Jacob*: God called himself *the God of Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob* long after these patriarchs had died. Since he was still their God, they must have had a continuing existence after death.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`)

## Luke 20:39 (id: 20772)

*Teacher, You have spoken well!*: Most teachers of religious law were Pharisees, so they agreed with Jesus on the resurrection and were pleased that he refuted the Sadducees.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); A Priest (ID: `person:GenericPriest`)

## Luke 20:40 (id: 20774)

The wise answers of Jesus confused his opponents. They could not defeat him in argument.

They had tried to trap him with difficult questions. Instead, his replies exposed their motives and showed his wisdom.

As a result, their plans to trap him failed.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:40


## Luke 20:41 (id: 20776)

The Old Testament predicted that the Messiah would be a descendant of King David (see study note on 18:38).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:41
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 18:38 (ID: `20681@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); David (ID: `person:David`)

## Luke 20:41–44 (id: 20775)

After Jesus silenced his opponents, he began to challenge the religious leaders and their understanding of the Messiah.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:41-44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Footstool (ID: `realia:Footstool`)

## Luke 20:42–43 (id: 20777)

Jesus quotes [Psalm 110:1\.](https://ref.ly/Ps110:1) By quoting this verse, Jesus shows that the Messiah (God's chosen leader) is more than a descendant of David. The Messiah is also David’s Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:42-43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); David (ID: `person:David`); Footstool (ID: `realia:Footstool`)

## Luke 20:44 (id: 20778)

Jesus asked why King David called his son (meaning his descendant) his Lord. The implied answer was that the Christ was more than a human king from David's family line. He is Lord of all (see [2:11](https://ref.ly/Luke2:11); [Acts 10:36](https://ref.ly/Acts10:36)). 

[Psalm 110:1](https://ref.ly/Ps110:1) became an important passage for the apostles. It confirms that Jesus is the Son of God (see [Mark 14:62](https://ref.ly/Mark14:62); [Acts 2:34](https://ref.ly/Acts2:34); [7:56](https://ref.ly/Acts7:56); [Romans 8:34](https://ref.ly/Rom8:34); [1 Corinthians 15:25](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:25); [Ephesians 1:20](https://ref.ly/Eph1:20); [Colossians 3:1](https://ref.ly/Col3:1); [Hebrews 1:3](https://ref.ly/Heb1:3), [13](https://ref.ly/Heb1:13); [8:1](https://ref.ly/Heb8:1); [10:12–13](https://ref.ly/Heb10:12-Heb10:13); [1 Peter 3:22](https://ref.ly/1Pet3:22); [Revelation 3:21](https://ref.ly/Rev3:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`)

## Luke 20:46–47 (id: 20779)

* Jesus continued confronting his opponents by warning people about the hypocrisy and exploitation practiced by the teachers of the law. These leaders sought honor and respect for themselves while taking advantage of vulnerable people.
* *the greetings in the marketplaces*: See study note on 11:43.
* *the places of honor at banquets*: See study notes on 5:30; 14:7.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:46-47
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 11:43 (ID: `20509@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 14:7 (ID: `20596@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 5:30 (ID: `20990@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Judgment (ID: `keyterm:Judgment`); Friendship (ID: `keyterm:Friendship`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Luke 20:47 (id: 20780)

*They defraud widows*: This might mean taking advantage of widows' property that they should have protected. It could also mean forcing widows to give more money than they could afford. 

Widows were the most vulnerable members of society. God had special concern for them. He would judge those who took advantage of them ([Exodus 22:22](https://ref.ly/Exod22:22); [Deuteronomy 10:18](https://ref.ly/Deut10:18); [27:19](https://ref.ly/Deut27:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 20:47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judgment (ID: `keyterm:Judgment`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 21:1 (id: 20792)

The Greek term for *treasury* likely refers to boxes with trumpet\-shaped openings for collecting offerings.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Treasury (ID: `realia:Treasury`)

## Luke 21:1–4 (id: 20785)

Jesus set the sacrificial offering of the poor widow in contrast to the greed and hypocrisy of the rich people*,* including the teachers of religious law ([20:45–47](https://ref.ly/Luke20:45-Luke20:47)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); True (ID: `keyterm:True`); Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`); Lepton (ID: `realia:Lepton`); Treasury (ID: `realia:Treasury`)

## Luke 21:2 (id: 20798)

*two small copper coins*: These coins (Greek *lepta*) were worth about 1/64 of a denarius. A denarius was the wage a laborer would receive for one day of work.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lepton (ID: `realia:Lepton`)

## Luke 21:4 (id: 20804)

The size of the gift matters less than the attitude of the person who gives it.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Daily Life (ID: `keyterm:DailyLife`)

## Luke 21:5 (id: 20806)

* *adorned with beautiful stones*: Herod the Great rebuilt and restored the temple in Jerusalem, producing one of the great structures of the ancient world. According to the historian Josephus, the massive white stones reflected the sun with such brilliance that from a distance the temple looked like snow\-covered mountains (*War* 5\.5\.6\).
* The memorial decorations were probably funded by offerings given by worshipers to fulfill vows.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 21:5–38 (id: 20805)

When Jesus predicted the destruction of the temple in Jerusalem, his disciples asked when it would happen and what signs would accompany it ([21:6,](https://ref.ly/Luke21:6)[7](https://ref.ly/Luke21:7)). Jesus responded by describing the events surrounding the destruction of the temple and the return of the Son of Man ([21:8–36](https://ref.ly/Luke21:8-Luke21:36)). This sermon is known as the Olivet Discourse because the Mount of Olives was where Jesus taught (see [Matthew 24:3](https://ref.ly/Matt24:3); [Mark 13:3](https://ref.ly/Mark13:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:5-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Liberation (ID: `keyterm:Liberation`); Avenge (ID: `keyterm:GrantJustice`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Everyday Matters (ID: `keyterm:OfThisLife`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Mount of Olives (ID: `place:OlivesMount`); Dreadful Event (ID: `keyterm:DreadfulEventOrSight`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); Net (ID: `realia:Net`); Olive Tree (ID: `flora:OliveTree`)

## Luke 21:6 (id: 20807)

*not one stone will be left on another*: See study note on 19:43–44.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:6
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 19:43-44 (ID: `20705@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 21:8 (id: 20809)

*claiming, ‘I am He,’*: See study note on Mark 13:5–6.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:8
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 13:5-6 (ID: `21345@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Luke 21:8–11 (id: 20808)

Jesus spoke about events that many people misunderstand as signs of the end of the age. These events include false messiahs (people who claim to be God’s chosen one), wars, earthquakes, famines (times when there is not enough food), and severe persecution (violent suffering because of faith).

However, Jesus made it clear that these events do not mean the end will happen right away. The end will not come immediately after these things.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:8-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Dreadful Event (ID: `keyterm:DreadfulEventOrSight`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 21:11 (id: 20786)

* Destructive events like these are often associated with:

    + the judgment of God ([1 Samuel 14:15](https://ref.ly/1Sam14:15); [Psalm 18:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ps18:7-Ps18:8); [Isaiah 2:19](https://ref.ly/Isa2:19), [21](https://ref.ly/Isa2:21); [5:25](https://ref.ly/Isa5:25); [13:13](https://ref.ly/Isa13:13); [24:18](https://ref.ly/Isa24:18); [29:5–6](https://ref.ly/Isa29:5-Isa29:6); [Jeremiah 14:12](https://ref.ly/Jer14:12); [21:6–7](https://ref.ly/Jer21:6-Jer21:7); [Ezekiel 14:21](https://ref.ly/Ezek14:21); [Amos 1:1](https://ref.ly/Amos1:1)) and
        + the end of the age ([Isaiah 24:18](https://ref.ly/Isa24:18); [29:5–6](https://ref.ly/Isa29:5-Isa29:6); [Ezekiel 38:19](https://ref.ly/Ezek38:19); [Joel 2:10](https://ref.ly/Joel2:10); [Haggai 2:6](https://ref.ly/Hag2:6), [21](https://ref.ly/Hag2:21); [Zechariah 14:4](https://ref.ly/Zech14:4); compare [Revelation 6:12](https://ref.ly/Rev6:12); [8:5](https://ref.ly/Rev8:5); [11:13](https://ref.ly/Rev11:13), [19](https://ref.ly/Rev11:19); [16:18](https://ref.ly/Rev16:18)).
* *great signs from heaven*: See study note on 21:25.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:11
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 21:25 (ID: `20797@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Dreadful Event (ID: `keyterm:DreadfulEventOrSight`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 21:12 (id: 20788)

*deliver you to the synagogues*: Jesus and his early followers were Jewish. The first conflicts were with Jews who did not accept Jesus as the Christ. The followers of Jesus were sometimes tried in the synagogues before a Jewish court (compare [2 Corinthians 11:24](https://ref.ly/2Cor11:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Luke 21:12–19 (id: 20787)

*they will seize you and persecute you*: Jesus described the persecution his followers would experience (see also [Matthew 10:17–22](https://ref.ly/Matt10:17-Matt10:22); [Mark 13:9](https://ref.ly/Mark13:9)). Jewish writings from the time of Jesus also described a period of great hardship before God's final salvation would arrive.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:12-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Luke 21:15 (id: 20789)

*I will give you speech and wisdom*: God often assured his prophets that he would give them the words to say ([Exodus 4:12](https://ref.ly/Exod4:12), [15](https://ref.ly/Exod4:15); [Deuteronomy 18:18](https://ref.ly/Deut18:18); [Isaiah 50:4](https://ref.ly/Isa50:4); [51:16](https://ref.ly/Isa51:16); [Jeremiah 1:9](https://ref.ly/Jer1:9)). The disciples of Jesus were to prepare themselves to speak clearly as witnesses (see [Colossians 4:5–6](https://ref.ly/Col4:5-Col4:6); [2 Timothy 2:21](https://ref.ly/2Tim2:21); [1 Peter 3:15](https://ref.ly/1Pet3:15)). They knew that the Holy Spirit would help them respond as powerfully as Jesus had done ([Luke 20:20–44](https://ref.ly/Luke20:20-Luke20:44)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:15


## Luke 21:16 (id: 20790)

Families in Jewish and Gentile communities have often been divided as some members follow Jesus and some reject him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:16


## Luke 21:18–19 (id: 20791)

*not even a hair of your head will perish*: This expression means complete protection ([1 Samuel 14:45](https://ref.ly/1Sam14:45); [2 Samuel 14:11](https://ref.ly/2Sam14:11); [1 Kings 1:52](https://ref.ly/1Kgs1:52); [Daniel 3:27](https://ref.ly/Dan3:27)). Here, it refers to the disciples' *souls*, because some would be killed because of their faith ([Luke 21:16](https://ref.ly/Luke21:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:18-19


## Luke 21:20–24 (id: 20793)

Jesus was describing the siege and destruction of Jerusalem that would take place in AD 70\.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:20-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Avenge (ID: `keyterm:GrantJustice`)

## Luke 21:21 (id: 20794)

*those in the city get out*: Because of this prophecy, Christians in Judea fled to the town of Pella in Decapolis before the destruction of Jerusalem in AD 70 (Eusebius, *Church History* 3\.5\).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judea (ID: `place:Judea`)

## Luke 21:22 (id: 20795)

*these are the days of vengeance*: The destruction of Jerusalem was part of God’s judgment for Israel’s rejection of the Messiah.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Avenge (ID: `keyterm:GrantJustice`)

## Luke 21:24 (id: 20796)

*until the times of the Gentiles are fulfilled*: Daniel predicted that a series of Gentile empires (kingdoms ruled by non\-Jews) would come before God’s kingdom ([Daniel 2:44](https://ref.ly/Dan2:44); [7:27](https://ref.ly/Dan7:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`)

## Luke 21:25 (id: 20797)

The prophets predicted that *signs* in the sky would come before God's judgment at the end of time ([Isaiah 13:9–10](https://ref.ly/Isa13:9-Isa13:10); [34:4](https://ref.ly/Isa34:4); [Jeremiah 4:23](https://ref.ly/Jer4:23), [28](https://ref.ly/Jer4:28); [Ezekiel 32:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ezek32:7-Ezek32:8); [Joel 2:10](https://ref.ly/Joel2:10), [30–31](https://ref.ly/Joel2:30-Joel2:31); compare [Revelation 6:13–14](https://ref.ly/Rev6:13-Rev6:14); [8:10](https://ref.ly/Rev8:10); [9:1](https://ref.ly/Rev9:1); [12:1](https://ref.ly/Rev12:1),[3](https://ref.ly/Rev12:3); [15:1](https://ref.ly/Luke15:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`)

## Luke 21:31 (id: 20799)

All these things refer to the events described in [21:25–30](https://ref.ly/Luke21:25-Luke21:30).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 21:32 (id: 20800)

*this generation will not pass away*: This saying is difficult to understand. Jesus seems to have predicted that he would come again before the first generation of his disciples died out. There are three main interpretations: 

1. the reference is to the destruction of Jerusalem in AD 70 when Jesus "came" in judgment against Israel;
2. *this generation* refers to a future generation alive when these signs start to happen;
3. the term *generation* could mean “people,” referring to the Jewish people, who will not disappear before Christ returns.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`)

## Luke 21:33 (id: 20801)

*Heaven and earth will pass away*: See study note on 16:17.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:33
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 16:17 (ID: `20618@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 21:34 (id: 20802)

The main theme of this teaching is, “Watch out!” Believers must always stay alert and be ready for Christ’s return.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Everyday Matters (ID: `keyterm:OfThisLife`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 21:36 (id: 20803)

*to stand before*: This means to "pass the test" and stand approved before Jesus Christ at his second coming.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 21:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 22:1 (id: 20818)

The Passover meal was held on the 15th of Nisan (March\~April) in the Jewish calendar and was immediately followed by the seven\-day Feast of Unleavened Bread ([Exodus 12:1–20](https://ref.ly/Exod12:1-Exod12:20); [23:15](https://ref.ly/Exod23:15); [34:18](https://ref.ly/Exod34:18); [Deuteronomy 16:1–8](https://ref.ly/Deut16:1-Deut16:8)). This feast is sometimes also called Passover.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`)

## Luke 22:1–6 (id: 20810)

The religious leaders who had been plotting against Jesus (see [19:47](https://ref.ly/Luke19:47)) found their opportunity when Judas Iscariot approached them with an offer to betray Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judas (ID: `person:Judas.2`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Luke 22:3 (id: 20834)

*Satan entered Judas Iscariot*: See [John 13:2](https://ref.ly/John13:2); [Acts 5:3](https://ref.ly/Acts5:3). Earlier, Satan had tempted Jesus and had been waiting for this opportunity ([Luke 4:2](https://ref.ly/Luke4:2)[, 13](https://ref.ly/Luke4:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judas (ID: `person:Judas.2`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`)

## Luke 22:4 (id: 20840)

*temple officers*: These were leaders within the temple’s own security force. The temple had its own police force to keep order and protect the sacred area.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:4


## Luke 22:5 (id: 20845)

Judas wanted the *money*. Judas wanted the money. John says that he was a thief and used to steal from the money bag of the disciples ([John 12:6](https://ref.ly/John12:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:5


## Luke 22:6 (id: 20852)

*in the absence of a crowd*: They waited until the crowds were not present. They did this because Jesus was very popular with the people.

The leaders were afraid of how the crowd might react if they arrested him in public.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:6


## Luke 22:7 (id: 20856)

The Passover lamb was killed at twilight (the time between sunset and darkness). The Passover meal was then eaten that same evening (see [Exodus 12:6–8](https://ref.ly/Exod12:6-Exod12:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Luke 22:7–30 (id: 20853)

Jesus celebrated the traditional Jewish Passover but transformed it with reference to his own sacrificial death as the Passover lamb. He also prepared his disciples for his coming death and the leadership role they would assume over his church.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:7-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Upstairs Room (ID: `realia:UpstairsRoom`); Guest Room (ID: `realia:GuestRoom`); Clay Jar (ID: `realia:ClayJar`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Luke 22:8 (id: 20857)

*Go and make preparations for us to eat the Passover*: The lamb was roasted and eaten with bitter herbs and unleavened bread at the Passover meal ([Exodus 12:8](https://ref.ly/Exod12:8); [Numbers 9:11–12](https://ref.ly/Num9:11-Num9:12); [Deuteronomy 16:3](https://ref.ly/Deut16:3)). The bitter herbs represented the bitterness of the Israelites’ slavery in Egypt, and the unleavened bread reminded them of their hurried departure from Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John.2`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`)

## Luke 22:10 (id: 20811)

*a man carrying a jug of water*: This was unusual because women usually carried the water.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Clay Jar (ID: `realia:ClayJar`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 22:11–12 (id: 20812)

The text does not say whether Jesus knew about the room because of his divine nature or if he had made arrangements for this room ahead of time (see also [19:30–34](https://ref.ly/Luke19:30-Luke19:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Upstairs Room (ID: `realia:UpstairsRoom`); Guest Room (ID: `realia:GuestRoom`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Luke 22:14 (id: 20813)

*Jesus reclined at the table*: The Jewish people adopted the Greek custom of reclining on couches next to tables at banquets (see study note on John 13:23).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:14
* **Associated Articles:** John 13:23 (ID: `18909@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 22:15 (id: 20814)

Jesus ate the Passover meal with his disciples and followed the ritual order of the Passover celebration (see study note on Matthew 26:26–29).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:15
* **Associated Articles:** Matthew 26:26-29 (ID: `22048@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Luke 22:16 (id: 20815)

*until it is fulfilled in the kingdom of God*: This celebration pointed forward to the messianic banquet, the great feast in God’s kingdom (see [14:1–24](https://ref.ly/Luke14:1-Luke14:24)). The death of Jesus as the true Passover lamb established the kingdom of God ([1 Corinthians 5:7](https://ref.ly/1Cor5:7)). During his earthly ministry, Jesus began God’s saving rule, and God’s kingdom will be fully completed when he returns.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 22:17 (id: 20816)

*After taking the cup*: The traditional Passover meal included four cups of wine:

* one cup was shared at the beginning blessing,
* another after the Passover explanation,
* another after the meal, and
* the last after a final psalm.

The cup Jesus took here was probably the first cup used in the Passover ceremony.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:17


## Luke 22:19–20 (id: 20817)

* Using the bread and the cup as symbols of his body and blood, Jesus established the communion meal that his disciples were to continue in remembrance of his death.
* *This is My body, given for you*: The unleavened bread of the Passover represented the death of Jesus as the Passover lamb. He would allow his blood to pour out "for the forgiveness of sins" ([Matthew 26:28](https://ref.ly/Matt26:28); compare [1 Corinthians 11:24–25](https://ref.ly/1Cor11:24-1Cor11:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Matthew 26:28; Luke 22:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`)

## Luke 22:20 (id: 20819)

In the Old Testament, God confirmed his covenants with the blood of a sacrifice ([Genesis 15:9–10](https://ref.ly/Gen15:9-Gen15:10); [Exodus 24:8](https://ref.ly/Exod24:8)). The death of Jesus established the new covenant that God had promised to his people ([Jeremiah 31:31–34](https://ref.ly/Jer31:31-Jer31:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`)

## Luke 22:21–22 (id: 20820)

*it has been determined*: Wicked men betrayed and killed Jesus, but this took place as part of God’s purposeful plan to bring salvation (see [Acts 2:23](https://ref.ly/Acts2:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:21-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`)

## Luke 22:22 (id: 20821)

*woe to that man who betrays Him*: Judas suffered guilt, judgment, and a terrible death for betraying Jesus ([Matthew 27:5](https://ref.ly/Matt27:5); [Acts 1:18](https://ref.ly/Acts1:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disaster (ID: `keyterm:Disaster`)

## Luke 22:24 (id: 20822)

*which of them should be considered the greatest*: See also [Mark 9:33–37](https://ref.ly/Mark9:33-Mark9:37) and [10:35–45](https://ref.ly/Mark10:35-Mark10:45).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:24


## Luke 22:25 (id: 20823)

*benefactors*: This translates a Greek word that refers to the practice of rulers bestowing gifts and favors on their subjects to gain loyalty and honor. Jesus contrasted this worldly style of leadership (military power, coercion, and bribery) with his own servant leadership in giving himself for others.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`)

## Luke 22:27 (id: 20824)

*I am among you as one who serves*: Jesus defined true leadership as serving others. This means meeting the needs of others and empowering them to be all that God has called them to be (see [Mark 10:45](https://ref.ly/Mark10:45)). This idea was surprising in a culture where status and power were important.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`)

## Luke 22:29–30 (id: 20825)

*you may eat and drink at My table*: See “The Messianic Banquet” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:29-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Luke 22:30 (id: 20826)

*judging the twelve tribes of Israel*: This could be a figurative reference to the apostles’ leadership in the church. Or it could refer to their special role in the future, when God’s kingdom will be fully established on earth (compare [Ephesians 2:20](https://ref.ly/Eph2:20); [Revelation 3:21](https://ref.ly/Rev3:21); [20:4](https://ref.ly/Rev20:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`)

## Luke 22:31 (id: 20827)

* *Satan*: See [4:2](https://ref.ly/Luke4:2); [10:18](https://ref.ly/Luke10:18); [11:15](https://ref.ly/Luke11:15). Satan is the accuser of God’s people (see [Job 1:7](https://ref.ly/Job1:7); [2:2](https://ref.ly/Job2:2)). He would test Simon Peter and the rest of the apostles.
* *has asked to sift*: People would sift wheat to separate grain from the parts you do not eat using a device called a sieve. This image represents extreme testing (see [Isaiah 30:28](https://ref.ly/Isa30:28); [Amos 9:9](https://ref.ly/Amos9:9)).
* *all of you:* The Greek here is plural. It refers to all the disciples. Jesus predicted Peter's denial and the difficult experiences of the other disciples.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`)

## Luke 22:32 (id: 20828)

*when you have turned back*: The *you* here is singular and refers to Peter's restoration after he denied Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 22:33 (id: 20829)

See [Acts 4:3](https://ref.ly/Acts4:3); [5:18](https://ref.ly/Acts5:18); [12:1–9](https://ref.ly/Acts12:1-Acts12:9), where Peter suffered trials and was repeatedly jailed. Church tradition relates that Peter was crucified in Rome, but his declaration of loyalty here was premature.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Luke 22:34 (id: 20830)

On Thursday evening, Jesus said that Peter would deny him before the rooster crows tomorrow morning (literally *a rooster will not crow today until*). This does not contradict Mark’s account: "this very night, before the rooster crows twice" ([Mark 14:30](https://ref.ly/Mark14:30)). The Gospels of Luke and Matthew provide a more general account, and in Jewish tradition, the next day begins at sunset.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Rooster (ID: `fauna:Rooster`)

## Luke 22:36 (id: 20831)
