* *the one with a purse should take it, and likewise a bag*: The apostles had traveled without supplies and relied on the support of other people for their missions to Israel's villages ([9:3](https://ref.ly/Luke9:3); [10:3–4](https://ref.ly/Luke10:3-Luke10:4)). In the dangerous days to come, they would have to plan more carefully. Jesus prepared his disciples for his suffering and death, and also for the establishing of the church and the worldwide proclamation of the good news.
* *a sword*: Jesus was probably not telling his followers to use weapons or fight. Instead, he was speaking figuratively, warning them to prepare for a time of violent conflict and suffering.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`)

## Luke 22:38 (id: 20832)

*That is enough*: The disciples’ two swords were probably not enough to defend the whole group, and Jesus elsewhere rejected violence ([22:49–51](https://ref.ly/Luke22:49-Luke22:51); [Matthew 26:52](https://ref.ly/Matt26:52)). Jesus might have meant that two swords were sufficient to fulfill [Isaiah 53:12](https://ref.ly/Isa53:12). Or perhaps he meant something like, “Enough of this foolish talk, you do not understand me.”

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`)

## Luke 22:39 (id: 20833)

*Mount of Olives*: See study note on 19:29. This was the location of the garden of Gethsemane (see [Matthew 26:36](https://ref.ly/Matt26:36); [Mark 14:32](https://ref.ly/Mark14:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:39
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 19:29 (ID: `20697@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Olive Tree (ID: `flora:OliveTree`)

## Luke 22:42 (id: 20835)

*take this cup from Me*: A cup is a metaphor for experiencing either judgment or blessing (see, for example, [Psalms 23:5](https://ref.ly/Ps23:5); [75:8](https://ref.ly/Ps75:8); [116:13](https://ref.ly/Ps116:13); [Isaiah 51:17](https://ref.ly/Isa51:17)). Jesus felt all the emotions of his humanity, including fear and anxiety.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 22:43 (id: 20836)

*an angel from heaven*: Angels often help and encourage human beings (see [Hebrews 1:14](https://ref.ly/Heb1:14); compare [1 Kings 19:5–8](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:5-1Kgs19:8); [Psalm 91:11–12](https://ref.ly/Ps91:11-Ps91:12); [Daniel 3:28](https://ref.ly/Dan3:28); [10:16–19](https://ref.ly/Dan10:16-Dan10:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 22:44 (id: 20837)

*His sweat became like drops of blood falling to the ground*: The text does not say that Jesus sweated blood, as people often think. It says that his sweat fell to the ground like blood. This probably means that, in his agony, he was sweating very heavily.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)

## Luke 22:45–46 (id: 20838)

Jesus prayed in great distress, while the disciples failed to remain alert against temptation.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:45-46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Put to the Test (ID: `keyterm:PutToTheTest`)

## Luke 22:47 (id: 20839)

A kiss was a common way for friends to greet each other. Judas’s kiss made his betrayal even more clear.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judas (ID: `person:Judas.2`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 22:51 (id: 20841)

*He touched the man’s ear and healed him*: The Gospel of John tells us that the man’s name was Malchus and that Peter was the one who cut off his ear ([John 18:10](https://ref.ly/John18:10)). Peter probably believed that he was beginning the final battle and that God’s heavenly army was about to appear and destroy the Romans. When Jesus refused to fight, the frightened disciples ran away ([Matthew 26:56](https://ref.ly/Matt26:56); [Mark 14:50](https://ref.ly/Mark14:50)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 22:52 (id: 20842)

*outlaw*: The Greek word here typically means "thief." However, the Romans used this term for violent revolutionaries who opposed Roman authority (such as Barabbas, [23:18–19](https://ref.ly/Luke23:18-Luke23:19)). To many patriotic Jews, these revolutionaries were seen as freedom fighters. To the Romans, they were violent criminals.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 22:54 (id: 20843)

*the house the high priest*: This home could have belonged to Caiaphas, the current high priest, or Annas, his father\-in\-law and a previous high priest (see study note on 3:2; [John 18:12–14](https://ref.ly/John18:12-John18:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:54
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 3:2 (ID: `20939@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Luke 22:59 (id: 20844)

*for he too is a Galilean*: Peter’s way of speaking made it clear that he was from Galilee (compare [Judges 12:5–6](https://ref.ly/Judg12:5-Judg12:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:59

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Galileans (ID: `group:Galilee`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)

## Luke 22:61 (id: 20846)

*the Lord turned and looked at Peter*: At that time, Jesus was inside the house, where the leaders were questioning him. He may have looked through a window or an open entryway (a covered space at the entrance of a house).

When their eyes met, the rooster crowed. Then Peter remembered what Jesus had said earlier, that Peter would deny him ([22:34](https://ref.ly/Luke22:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:61

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Rooster (ID: `fauna:Rooster`)

## Luke 22:63 (id: 20847)

*began to mock Him and beat Him*: See [18:32](https://ref.ly/Luke18:32); [Isaiah 50:6](https://ref.ly/Isa50:6); [53:3–5](https://ref.ly/Isa53:3-Isa53:5).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:63


## Luke 22:64 (id: 20848)

*Prophesy! Who hit You?*: Jesus had a reputation as a prophet ([4:24](https://ref.ly/Luke4:24); [7:16](https://ref.ly/Luke7:16), [39](https://ref.ly/Luke7:39); [13:33](https://ref.ly/Luke13:33); [24:19](https://ref.ly/Luke24:19)), so the soldiers mocked this claim.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:64

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Speak as a Prophet (ID: `keyterm:Speak`)

## Luke 22:66 (id: 20849)

* *At daybreak*: It was Friday morning (see study note on 22:34).
* *the council of the elders … met together*: The Jewish high council (the Sanhedrin) was originally made up of the Jerusalem nobility, both civil leaders and priests, with the high priest as its leader. In the time of Jesus, the Sadducees still led the Sanhedrin ([Acts 5:17](https://ref.ly/Acts5:17)). But leading Pharisees also held important positions on the council ([Acts 23:7](https://ref.ly/Acts23:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:66
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 22:34 (ID: `20830@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 22:67–68 (id: 20850)

*If I tell you, you will not believe*: Jesus pointed out the hypocrisy in their question. They had already decided that he was not the Christ (the Messiah, God's chosen king), so answering them would not change their minds.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:67-68

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 22:69 (id: 20851)

Jesus referred to [Psalm 110:1](https://ref.ly/Ps110:1) (compare [Luke 20:42–43](https://ref.ly/Luke20:42-Luke20:43)). Although the Romans would crucify him, God would vindicate Jesus through his resurrection. God would exalt Jesus to his right hand (see [Acts 2:33–36](https://ref.ly/Acts2:33-Acts2:36); compare [Daniel 7:13–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:13-Dan7:14)). Furthermore, Jesus would be seated in the place of power as Judge. He would soon sit in judgment over those who were trying him.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:69

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 22:70 (id: 20854)

* The council members clearly understood what Jesus meant in [22:69](https://ref.ly/Luke22:69). They expected the Christ to be the Son of God and have a unique father\-son relationship with God (see study notes on 1:32–33; 4:41; compare [2 Samuel 7:14](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:14); [Psalms 2:7](https://ref.ly/Ps2:7); [89:26](https://ref.ly/Ps89:26)). The Sanhedrin accused Jesus of claiming to be the Christ (compare study notes on Mark 14:62, 63–64).
* *You say that I am*: This was a qualified affirmation, meaning that Jesus partly agreed with their statement. He probably meant, “What you say is true, but you do not understand what ‘Christ’ means.” The council understood this as a confirmation ([Luke 22:71](https://ref.ly/Luke22:71)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:70
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 1:32-33 (ID: `20431@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Luke 4:41 (ID: `20969@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Mark 14:62 (ID: `21382@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Mark 14:63-64 (ID: `21383@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Priests (ID: `group:GenericPriests`); Elders (ID: `group:GenericElders`); Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`)

## Luke 22:71 (id: 20855)

*Why do we need any more testimony?*: The high council believed that when Jesus claimed to be the Christ and the Son of God, he was blaspheming (speaking against God). They thought this was proof of his guilt.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 22:71

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Priests (ID: `group:GenericPriests`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); Elders (ID: `group:GenericElders`); Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`)

## Luke 23:1 (id: 20862)

The Jewish council did not have the authority to carry out capital punishment under Roman rule ([John 18:31](https://ref.ly/John18:31)). So they took Jesus to the Roman governor, Pontius Pilate. Pilate was in Jerusalem to keep order during the Passover festival, when large crowds could easily lead to unrest.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`)

## Luke 23:2 (id: 20867)

The religious charges against Jesus were now replaced by political ones in order to gain a Roman conviction. Pilate was only interested in what concerned Rome. The Jewish charge of blasphemy would not be sufficient, so the council had to show that Jesus was a danger to Rome. They accused Jesus of inciting insurrection against Roman taxation and claiming to be a king. All but the last charge were false.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tribute Tax (ID: `keyterm:TributeTax`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Tiberius (ID: `person:Tiberius`)

## Luke 23:3 (id: 20875)

*You have said so*: This is the same indirect answer that Jesus gave to the high council ([22:70](https://ref.ly/Luke22:70)). Jesus accepted the title King of the Jews, but he understood it differently from Pilate.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Luke 23:4 (id: 20883)

*I find no basis for a charge against this man*: Jesus did not appear to be a political threat to Pilate.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`)

## Luke 23:5 (id: 20889)

*He stirs up the people*: This accusation was also false, but the religious leaders knew that Pilate feared civil unrest and revolt against Rome.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`)

## Luke 23:6–7 (id: 20890)

Pilate realized that Jesus was from Galilee and was under the authority of Herod Antipas. This gave Pilate a way to avoid the situation without condemning an innocent man or upsetting the religious leaders.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Galileans (ID: `group:Galilee`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`)

## Luke 23:8 (id: 20891)

*he was greatly pleased*: He was curious about Jesus and his power to do miracles and teach, just as he had been curious about John the Baptist ([9:7](https://ref.ly/Luke9:7), [9](https://ref.ly/Luke9:9); [Mark 6:20](https://ref.ly/Mark6:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`)

## Luke 23:9 (id: 20892)

*He gave no answer*: See [Isaiah 53:7](https://ref.ly/Isa53:7).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:9


## Luke 23:12 (id: 20858)

*they had been enemies*: There was a history of conflict between Herod Antipas and Pilate. Herod and three of his brothers had earlier accused Pilate before Tiberius Caesar. This happened when Pilate placed gold shields with pagan symbols in Jerusalem, and Tiberius ordered him to remove them.

Pilate also ruled Judea, a region that Herod Antipas’s father, Herod the Great, had once ruled. Antipas hoped that he might one day rule this area himself.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`)

## Luke 23:14–15 (id: 20859)

Pilate and Herod repeatedly declared Jesus innocent ([23:4](https://ref.ly/Luke23:4), [22](https://ref.ly/Luke23:22)). Jesus was the righteous and innocent servant who suffered for the Lord (see also [23:41](https://ref.ly/Luke23:41), [47](https://ref.ly/Luke23:47); [Isaiah 53:11](https://ref.ly/Isa53:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:14-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Discern (ID: `keyterm:Discern`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`)

## Luke 23:16–18 (id: 20860)

*I will punish Him*: The Greek verb can mean “instruct,” “punish,” or “discipline.” It refers to a mild whipping for minor offenses. This was different from the severe flogging Romans gave before crucifixion (see [Matthew 27:26](https://ref.ly/Matt27:26); [Mark 15:15](https://ref.ly/Mark15:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:16-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Barabbas (ID: `person:Barabbas`)

## Luke 23:19 (id: 20861)

Palestine was a center of rebellion in the years leading up to the Jewish revolt against Rome in AD 66–74\. The Jewish historian Josephus describes several first\-century (see also [Acts 5:36–37](https://ref.ly/Acts5:36-Acts5:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Luke 23:24 (id: 20863)

*Pilate sentenced that their demand be met*: *Pilate* wasa practical but harsh leader. He thought it was better to allow injustice than to upset the religious leaders and crowds. He wanted to protect his political career (see [John 19:12](https://ref.ly/John19:12) and corresponding study note).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:24
* **Associated Articles:** John 19:12 (ID: `19023@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`)

## Luke 23:26 (id: 20864)

* Simon may have been a Jewish traveler visiting Jerusalem for Passover.
* *they put the cross on him*: Prisoners bound for crucifixion were normally forced to carry the crossbeam to the place of execution. It was fastened to the upright beam at the crucifixion site.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cyrene (ID: `place:Cyrene`); Simon (ID: `person:Simon.5`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 23:28 (id: 20865)

Jesus warned about the terrible things that would happen to Jerusalem during its siege and destruction in AD 70\.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Luke 23:29 (id: 20866)

*Blessed are the barren women*: In that culture, not having children was usually considered a great shame (see study note on 1:7). But during this terrible disaster, women without children would be considered fortunate because they would not have to watch their children suffer and die from hunger.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:29
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 1:7 (ID: `20452@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Happy (ID: `keyterm:Happy`)

## Luke 23:30 (id: 20868)

*‘they will say to the mountains, “Fall on us!”‘*: This refers to a time when people would prefer death over the terrible suffering they would face. Jesus warned that Jerusalem would experience such terrible pain and destruction that people would beg for mountains to fall on them to end their suffering quickly.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:30


## Luke 23:31 (id: 20869)

If the Romans crucified an innocent man while conditions were still calm and stable (*while the tree is green*), what worse things would happen when rebellion spread through the land (*when it is dry*)? Jesus used the picture of green wood and dry wood to contrast a peaceful time with a time ready for destruction. During the siege of Jerusalem (AD 66–70\), the Romans crucified thousands of Jews outside the city.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:31


## Luke 23:33 (id: 20870)

* *The Skull*: The Greek name is *kranion*, and the Aramaic name is *Golgotha* ([Matthew 27:33](https://ref.ly/Matt27:33); [Mark 15:22](https://ref.ly/Mark15:22)). The exact location is unknown, but people traditionally associate it with the Church of the Holy Sepulchre. Another suggested location is Gordon’s Calvary, a rock formation near the Garden Tomb, though scholars consider this less likely.
* *They crucified him there*: People who were crucified were often tied to the cross or nailed through the wrists and ankles to speed up death. In this case, the bodies needed to be buried before sunset because the Sabbath was about to begin.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`); Golgotha (ID: `place:Golgotha`)

## Luke 23:34 (id: 20871)

*by casting lots* (see [John 19:23–24](https://ref.ly/John19:23-John19:24)): The prisoner’s possessions were treated as spoils of war and divided among the soldiers.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Lot (ID: `realia:Lot`)

## Luke 23:35 (id: 20872)

*The people stood watching, and the rulers sneered at Him* (see [Psalm 22:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ps22:7-Ps22:8)): [Psalm 22](https://ref.ly/Ps22:1-Ps22:31) describes David, a righteous sufferer, crying out to God for help. Jesus, a descendant of David, was the ultimate righteous sufferer and fulfilled the words of [Psalm 22](https://ref.ly/Ps22:1-Ps22:31).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 23:36 (id: 20873)

*sour wine*: This was a popular drink of the lower classes, especially among soldiers (compare [Psalm 69:21](https://ref.ly/Ps69:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sour Wine (ID: `realia:SourWine`)

## Luke 23:38 (id: 20874)

The sign confirmed that Jesus was crucified on the charge of claiming to be the King of the Jews (compare [John 19:19–22](https://ref.ly/John19:19-John19:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)

## Luke 23:42 (id: 20876)

*remember me when You come into Your kingdom!*: The criminal was perhaps thinking of the resurrection at the end of time, when Jesus would be raised up and vindicated by God.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 23:43 (id: 20877)

* Jesus corrected the man by assuring him that today he would be vindicated in God’s presence(see also [2:11](https://ref.ly/Luke2:11); [4:21](https://ref.ly/Luke4:21); [5:26](https://ref.ly/Luke5:26); [19:9](https://ref.ly/Luke19:9)).
* *Paradise*: This wordcomes from a Persian word meaning "garden." It was used in Jewish literature for the garden of Eden and also for heaven, the place of eternal peace and joy for God’s people (see [2 Corinthians 12:4](https://ref.ly/2Cor12:4); [Revelation 2:7](https://ref.ly/Rev2:7); compare [Luke 16:22–23](https://ref.ly/Luke16:22-Luke16:23); [2 Corinthians 5:8](https://ref.ly/2Cor5:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paradise (ID: `keyterm:Paradise`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`)

## Luke 23:44 (id: 20878)

Darkness symbolizes sorrow as well as God’s judgment ([Psalm 23:4](https://ref.ly/Ps23:4); [Isaiah 8:22](https://ref.ly/Isa8:22); [9:1–2](https://ref.ly/Isa9:1-Isa9:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:44


## Luke 23:45 (id: 20879)

*the veil of the temple*: One curtain separated the temple courtyard from the Holy Place, and another separated the Holy Place from the Most Holy Place. Luke does not say which was torn down the middle*,* but it was probably the latter. The death of Jesus put an end to the sacrificial system of the temple and opened a new and permanent way into the presence of God (see [Hebrews 10:19–20](https://ref.ly/Heb10:19-Heb10:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Curtain (ID: `realia:Curtain`)

## Luke 23:46 (id: 20880)

*Father, into Your hands I commit My Spirit* (see [Psalm 31:5](https://ref.ly/Ps31:5)): [Psalm 31 i](https://ref.ly/Ps31:1-Ps31:24)s another psalm about a righteous sufferer (a person who suffers but remains faithful to God; see study note on Luke 23:35).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:46
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 23:35 (ID: `20872@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`)

## Luke 23:47 (id: 20881)

*righteous* (compare [Mark 15:39](https://ref.ly/Mark15:39)): Compare [Luke 23:14–15](https://ref.ly/Luke23:14-Luke23:15). The innocent and righteous servant of the Lord died as a sacrifice for the sins of his people ([Isaiah 53:11](https://ref.ly/Isa53:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Luke 23:48 (id: 20882)

*they returned home beating their breasts*: This was a sign of sorrow and mourning.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:48


## Luke 23:50–51 (id: 20884)

* Not all the religious leaders opposed Jesus. Joseph had disagreed with the decision of the council.
* The exact location of Arimathea in Judea is unknown. It may have been Ramathaim, or Ramah, about 32 kilometers (20 miles) northwest of Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:50-51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Arimathea (ID: `place:Arimathea`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.6`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)

## Luke 23:52 (id: 20885)

*He went to Pilate to ask for the body of Jesus*: The Romans used crucifixion as a public warning against revolt, so they did not generally allow a victim to be buried. Instead, they left the body exposed or rotting on the cross. An exception was made in this case, probably because Pilate knew that Jesus was innocent and because of Joseph’s position of honor on the Jewish high council.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 23:53 (id: 20886)

The new tomb had probably been purchased by Joseph as a family tomb.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:53

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Luke 23:54 (id: 20887)

* *Preparation Day*: This happened late on Friday afternoon. The Greek text does not specifically mention Friday. It refers to the day Jews prepare for the Sabbath day ceremonies during Passover week. The Sabbath day begins on Friday evening.
* As the Sabbath was about to begin, they had to bury the body quickly. The Jews could not work during the Sabbath, which began at sunset.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:54

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`)

## Luke 23:55–56 (id: 20888)

The women took special note of the tomb’s location because they would return with spices and ointments to anoint his body. The spices were to mask the stench of a rotting corpse. A body would be placed on a stone bench in the side of the tomb until the flesh decomposed. The bones would then be collected and placed in a small ossuary, or bone box, and placed on a shelf in the tomb. In this way, a tomb could be used for many family members over an extended period of time.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 23:55-56

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aloes (ID: `realia:Aloes`); Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Myrrh (ID: `realia:Myrrh`); Myrrh (ID: `flora:Myrrh`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 24:1 (id: 20901)

*On the first day of the week*: The early church worshiped on the first day of the week (Sunday) instead of the seventh (Saturday, the Sabbath) because Jesus rose on a Sunday morning (compare [Acts 20:7](https://ref.ly/Acts20:7); [Revelation 1:10](https://ref.ly/Rev1:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Week (ID: `keyterm:Week`); Aloes (ID: `realia:Aloes`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 24:1–12 (id: 20893)

Women were the first to find the tomb empty and to hear the announcement of the resurrection. This is strong evidence for the historicity of the resurrection accounts. In first\-century Judaism, women were not regarded as reliable witnesses, so the church would never have created stories in which women discovered the empty tomb.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:1-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Joanna (ID: `person:Joanna`); Week (ID: `keyterm:Week`); James (ID: `person:James.3`); Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Disbelieve (ID: `keyterm:Disbelieve`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Mary Magdalene (ID: `person:Marymagdalene`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Aloes (ID: `realia:Aloes`)

## Luke 24:2 (id: 20907)

The stone was probably a large round stone that was rolled down a track to cover the tomb’s entrance.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 24:4 (id: 20917)

*suddenly two men … stood beside them*: Angels often appear as young men in the Bible ([Genesis 18:2](https://ref.ly/Gen18:2); [19:1](https://ref.ly/Gen19:1), [10](https://ref.ly/Gen19:10); [Joshua 5:13](https://ref.ly/Josh5:13); [Judges 13:6–11](https://ref.ly/Judg13:6-Judg13:11); [Hebrews 13:2](https://ref.ly/Heb13:2)). *Radiant apparel* (bright, shining clothes) is a symbol of purity and holiness ([Daniel 10:5–6](https://ref.ly/Dan10:5-Dan10:6); [Acts 1:10](https://ref.ly/Acts1:10); [Revelation 4:4](https://ref.ly/Rev4:4); [19:14](https://ref.ly/Rev19:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)

## Luke 24:5–7 (id: 20918)

Jesus had told his followers many times that he would rise from the dead (see [9:22](https://ref.ly/Luke9:22), [44](https://ref.ly/Luke9:44); [17:25](https://ref.ly/Luke17:25); [18:31–34](https://ref.ly/Luke18:31-Luke18:34)). The resurrection of Jesus is a key belief of the Christian faith (see [1 Corinthians 15:3–7](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:3-1Cor15:7), [12–57](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:12-1Cor15:57)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:5-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 24:7 (id: 20923)

Jesus rose on the third day, counting Friday as the first day and Sunday as the third.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 24:9 (id: 20924)

*the Eleven*: These were the Twelve disciples minus Judas. Judas Iscariot had betrayed Jesus and was no longer with them.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 24:10 (id: 20894)

[Luke 8:2–3](https://ref.ly/Luke8:2-Luke8:3) mentions Mary Magdalene and Joanna. Mary the mother of James is not the mother of James, the son of Zebedee (see [Matthew 27:56](https://ref.ly/Matt27:56)). But she might be the mother of James, the son of Alphaeus ([Luke 6:15](https://ref.ly/Luke6:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joanna (ID: `person:Joanna`); James (ID: `person:James.3`); Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); Mary Magdalene (ID: `person:Marymagdalene`)

## Luke 24:12 (id: 20895)

*Peter* … *ran to the tomb*: John went with him ([John 20:2–9](https://ref.ly/John20:2-John20:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 24:13 (id: 20897)

*two of them*: Jesus had many disciples in addition to the Twelve (see [10:1](https://ref.ly/Luke10:1), [17](https://ref.ly/Luke10:17)). Nothing else is known of Cleopas or the other disciple, who might have been his wife ([24:18](https://ref.ly/Luke24:18)). They were probably returning from celebrating the Passover feast in Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Emmaus (ID: `place:Emmaus`); Stade (ID: `realia:Stade`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Luke 24:13–34 (id: 20896)

Luke alone records this resurrection appearance. Jesus corrected the disciples’ misunderstanding by showing from the Scriptures that the Christ had to suffer. Then he opened their eyes to understand that he had risen from the dead.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:13-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Written (ID: `keyterm:Scripture`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`); Cleopas (ID: `person:Cleopas`); Emmaus (ID: `place:Emmaus`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Liberation (ID: `keyterm:Liberation`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Judgment (ID: `keyterm:Judgment`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Stade (ID: `realia:Stade`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Luke 24:16 (id: 20898)

*were kept from recognizing Him*: The Greek text uses a passive construction here. This is called a “divine passive” (a way of speaking that shows God is acting, even when God is not named). So here, even though the sentence does not name God as the one acting, the meaning is that God is the one who prevented them from recognizing Jesus. God chose not to let them see who he was until the moment he revealed himself, when Jesus broke bread with them ([24:30–31](https://ref.ly/Luke24:30-Luke24:31); compare [2 Kings 6:17](https://ref.ly/2Kgs6:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:16


## Luke 24:18 (id: 20899)

*Are You the only visitor to Jerusalem who does not know the things*: The Romans carried out crucifixions at busy public crossroads to make an example of the victims and warn others not to rebel. For these disciples, it was hard to imagine that a visitor in Jerusalem for Passover (a major Jewish festival) would not have heard about the crucifixion of Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cleopas (ID: `person:Cleopas`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Luke 24:19 (id: 20900)

Jesus is often portrayed as a prophet in Luke’s Gospel (see [7:16](https://ref.ly/Luke7:16); compare with the description of Moses in [Acts 7:22](https://ref.ly/Acts7:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 24:21 (id: 20902)

*we were hoping He was the One who would redeem Israel*: This refers to the Messiah. The crucifixion had ended their hopes.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Liberation (ID: `keyterm:Liberation`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`)

## Luke 24:22 (id: 20903)

*some of our women astounded us*: See [24:10–11](https://ref.ly/Luke24:10-Luke24:11).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`)

## Luke 24:25–26 (id: 20904)

The prophets wrote that the Christ (the Messiah, God’s chosen king) would have to suffer. The crucifixion did not show that Jesus was not the Christ. Instead, it confirmed that he is the Christ, because Scripture had already said that the Christ would suffer and die. Jesus corrected the two disciples because they did not understand from Scripture that this had to happen (see [Isaiah 50:4–9](https://ref.ly/Isa50:4-Isa50:9); [52:13–53:12](https://ref.ly/Isa52:13-Isa53:12); [Psalms 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12), [16](https://ref.ly/Ps16:1-Ps16:11), [22](https://ref.ly/Ps22:1-Ps22:31), [118](https://ref.ly/Ps118:1-Ps118:29); compare [Luke 18:32](https://ref.ly/Luke18:32); [20:17](https://ref.ly/Luke20:17); [23:37](https://ref.ly/Luke23:37); [Acts 2:25–28](https://ref.ly/Acts2:25-Acts2:28); [4:25–26](https://ref.ly/Acts4:25-Acts4:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:25-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Luke 24:27 (id: 20905)

*all the Scriptures*: This means all of the Old Testament. The Scriptures point forward to the coming of Jesus the Messiah and the salvation that he would bring.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Written (ID: `keyterm:Scripture`)

## Luke 24:29 (id: 20906)

*they pleaded with Him, “Stay with us“*: In Middle Eastern culture, hospitality (kindness to guests) required people to offer food and a place to stay, even to a stranger ([Genesis 18:3](https://ref.ly/Gen18:3); [19:2](https://ref.ly/Gen19:2); [Judges 19:5–9](https://ref.ly/Judg19:5-Judg19:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:29


## Luke 24:30 (id: 20908)

* *He was reclining at the table with them*: The meal is a symbol of the messianic banquet (the future feast when God’s chosen king gathers his people) and of the salvation that God is bringing about ([Isaiah 25:6–8](https://ref.ly/Isa25:6-Isa25:8); [Luke 14:1–24](https://ref.ly/Luke14:1-Luke14:24)).
* *and broke it, and gave it to them*: This action recalls the feeding of the 5,000 ([9:16](https://ref.ly/Luke9:16)) and the institution of the Lord's Supper ([22:19](https://ref.ly/Luke22:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Luke 24:31 (id: 20909)

* *Their eyes were opened*: This is another example of the “divine passive” (a way of speaking that shows God is acting, even when he is not named; see study note on 24:16). God opened their eyes when Jesus broke the bread. This means they were finally able to recognize Jesus.
* *and He disappeared from their sight*: In his resurrected and transformed body, Jesus showed abilities he had not shown before the resurrection (see [24:36](https://ref.ly/Luke24:36); [1 Corinthians 15:20–23](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:20-1Cor15:23), [35–57](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:35-1Cor15:57)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:31
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 24:16 (ID: `20898@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Luke 24:34 (id: 20910)

*Simon*: The Gospels do not describe this appearance to Simon Peter, but Paul also confirms it in [1 Corinthians 15:5](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:5).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`)

## Luke 24:39 (id: 20911)

*a spirit does not have flesh and bones, as you see I have*: After rising from the dead, Jesus was not a spirit without a body. He had a real body that could never die again or decay. Believers will receive similar bodies at the final resurrection ([1 Corinthians 15:35–57](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:35-1Cor15:57)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`)

## Luke 24:44 (id: 20912)

*in the Law of Moses, the Prophets, and the Psalms*: Jesus referred to the three sections of the Hebrew Scriptures: the Law, the Prophets, and the Writings. The entire Old Testament points to God’s salvation through Christ.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Law (ID: `keyterm:Law`)

## Luke 24:46 (id: 20913)

*The Christ will suffer and rise from the dead*: See study note on 24:25–26.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:46
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 24:25-26 (ID: `20904@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Luke 24:47 (id: 20914)

r*epentance and forgiveness of sins will be proclaimed to all nations*: See [Isaiah 42:6](https://ref.ly/Isa42:6); [49:6](https://ref.ly/Isa49:6); [Joel 2:28–32](https://ref.ly/Joel2:28-Joel2:32); [Amos 9:11–12](https://ref.ly/Amos9:11-Amos9:12); [Acts 2:17–21](https://ref.ly/Acts2:17-Acts2:21); [13:47](https://ref.ly/Acts13:47); [15:16–18](https://ref.ly/Acts15:16-Acts15:18).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:47

## Luke 24:48 (id: 20915)

The primary role of the apostles in the book of Acts was to be witnesses to the fulfillment of Scripture in the life, death, and resurrection of Jesus (see [Acts 1:8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`)

## Luke 24:49 (id: 20916)

*I am sending the promise of My Father*: The Old Testament prophesied that God would pour out his Holy Spirit on all people ([Isaiah 32:15](https://ref.ly/Isa32:15); [Jeremiah 31:33](https://ref.ly/Jer31:33); [Ezekiel 36:26–27](https://ref.ly/Ezek36:26-Ezek36:27); [Joel 2:28–32](https://ref.ly/Joel2:28-Joel2:32)). This prophecy came true on the day of Pentecost ([Acts 2:14–21](https://ref.ly/Acts2:14-Acts2:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Height (ID: `keyterm:Height`); Promise (ID: `keyterm:Promise`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Luke 24:50 (id: 20920)

*Bethany*: See study note on 19:29. See also [Acts 1:11–12](https://ref.ly/Acts1:11-Acts1:12); [Zechariah 14:4](https://ref.ly/Zech14:4).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:50
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 19:29 (ID: `20697@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bethany (ID: `place:Bethany`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Luke 24:50–53 (id: 20919)

Luke also tells about the ascension of Jesus (his return to heaven) in [Acts 1:1–11](https://ref.ly/Acts1:1-Acts1:11). For Luke, this event showed that God had:

* vindicated Jesus (proved that he was right),
* exalted him (lifted him to the highest place), and
* seated him at his right hand as Messiah (God’s chosen king) and Lord (the one with full authority; [Acts 2:24–36](https://ref.ly/Acts2:24-Acts2:36)).

From this place of glory and authority in heaven, Jesus leads and guides his church.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:50-53

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Bow Down (ID: `keyterm:BowDown`); Bethany (ID: `place:Bethany`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Luke 24:51 (id: 20921)

Compare [2 Kings 2:11](https://ref.ly/2Kgs2:11). The ascension of Jesus marked his exaltation to a position of authority over all creation (see [Acts 2:32–36](https://ref.ly/Acts2:32-Acts2:36); [5:31](https://ref.ly/Acts5:31); [Ephesians 1:19–23](https://ref.ly/Eph1:19-Eph1:23); [Philippians 2:9–11](https://ref.ly/Phil2:9-Phil2:11); [Hebrews 1:13](https://ref.ly/Heb1:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Luke 24:53 (id: 20922)

*the temple*: This was a place for worship and sacrifice. The apostles continued in their Jewish manner of worship because their goal was not to create a new religion but to announce the fulfillment of Scripture’s promises of salvation through Jesus the Messiah.

* **Associated Passages:** Luke 24:53

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)
